# FRENKISK KIT — hand this over as it stands at the start of a session

You are receiving the complete description of **Frenkisk**, a plausible
reconstruction of Merovingian Frankish (~550 AD, Salian Low Franconian).
Your task: translate into and out of Frenkisk, keeping STRICTLY to this
kit. The dictionary below is THE source of truth.

⚠ **The dictionary and the index are in English**, as is the grammar.
The move was finished on 2026-08-30. ⚠⚠ **This paragraph said the
opposite until 2026-09-01** — it told you the glosses were "still in
French" and to query them in French, which by then would have sent you
to look for words that were no longer there. It had been written to
protect a reader during the move, and it went on being read, in the
imperative, after the move was done. *Both translators of the 82nd round
caught it and disobeyed it; a control could not, because `anglo`
measures whether a line IS French and nothing measures whether a line
SAYS the file is.*

## ⚠⚠ WHAT THIS LANGUAGE IS — ruling of 2026-08-18

> **Frenkisk reconstructs the OLD FRANCONIAN of the Scheldt–Meuse–Lower
> Rhine area around 550: the continental West Germanic spoken INLAND
> under Frankish rule, the common ancestor of Old Dutch AND of the
> Ripuarian, Moselle and Rhenish Franconian dialects — but not of
> Alemannic or Bavarian, nor of the coastal Germanic of the North Sea.**

⚠ **The earlier definition — "Low Franconian, close to Old Saxon, without
the second sound shift" — was wrong on all three counts**, and two
independent enquirers established it: in 550 the shift had happened
NOWHERE (a shared retention does not group anything); Old Dutch is NOT
Ingvaeonic at its core (nasal kept, **three distinct persons in the
verbal plural**, no plural in `-s`); and the extent was too generous in
the south (neither Alemannic nor Bavarian) and too mean in the east
(**Cologne, in 550, speaks this language**). The detail, including where
the two enquirers diverged on nomenclature, is in the README.

> *A language reconstructed for a date is A POINT IN A CONTINUUM, not a
> node in a tree.*

## ⚠⚠ WHERE TO START — the table of QUESTIONS (2026-08-19)

**Read this before the table of contents.** The table of contents that
follows arranges the kit by **rulings**, that is by **date**; this one
arranges it by **what you are looking for**. Both translators of the
38th round put navigation **ahead of grammar** in what had hindered
them:

> *"To write six words I had to go to four different places, none of
> which points to the other three. **The kit files by history; one
> translates by need.**"*

### ⚠⚠⚠ IF YOU ONLY HAVE ONE SESSION — READ THIS FIRST (2026-08-24)

**This kit cannot be read whole, and you should not try.** The two

what they skipped — this is a record, not an estimate.

rule of use. *"Had I handed in my first draft, the only measure

### ⚠⚠ THE TWO ABSTRACT SUFFIXES — AND THERE IS NO RULE (2026-08-24)

The lexicon carries two live series: **`-itha`** (`hauhitha` "height",
`braiditha`, `diupitha`, `haunitha`, `māritha`) and **`-ī`** (`wirdi`,
`wraithi`, `stolti`, `menigi`).

⚠⚠⚠ **No rule separates them**, and a double enquiry established this by
refuting all three hypotheses. Gothic lines up **`braidei` + `laggei` +
`hauhiþa` + `diupiþa` in a single verse** (Eph. 3:18): two suffixes, one
semantic domain, one author. And `māritha` is built on a `-ja` adjective,
which ruins the morphological rule.

> *"Any rule I gave you would be manufactured to satisfy you."*

**What remains, and it is a DECISION of normalisation, not a datum**: in
this branch, `-itha` pulls towards the **measurable dimension** (that is
the Low Franconian outcome — Du *diepte*, *hoogte*, *breedte*) and `-ī`
towards the **inherent quality**. ⚠ Follow it for consistency, **but
never present it as a law.**

⚠ **And these `-ī` feminines are WEAK `-īn` STEMS**, not `-i` stems —
Goth. `managei`, gen. **`manageins`**.

### ⚠⚠ HOW TO READ A MARKER IN THE DICTIONARY (2026-09-07)

Three marks follow a government, and they are not the same strength:

· ***(government by double enquiry DATE, a %/b %; not attested; one
  witness overturns it)*** — two blind enquirers agreed on the cognate
  evidence. It is a hypothesis with a rating, not a text.
· ***READ DATE Go. lemma ×n: obj ACC ×k, obl DAT ×m*** — the raw
  count of the cases the GOTHIC cognate takes in PROIEL, written by a
  tool. It is a witness for Gothic; it says nothing by itself about
  Frankish, and where it reads "(generated cognate)" the pairing itself
  is by sound law and may be a false friend.
· ***ATTESTED DATE for the <slot>, <CASE>*** — a line, or a count, that
  proves ONE argument of the verb. ⚠⚠ **The marker names the slot it
  proves and nothing else**: *"for the recipient, DATIVE"* on `dailian`
  leaves the accusative of the thing shared exactly as unproven as
  before. Both translators of the 84th round asked for this, having
  taken a witness for one slot as a warrant for the other.

### ⚠⚠⚠ TRANSITIVE OR INTRANSITIVE — THE RULE IS READ, NOT MANUFACTURED (2026-08-25)

English is **labile**: "open", "freeze", "fold", "grow" are transitive
**and** intransitive. ⚠⚠ **Frenkisk is not**, and the gloss will not tell
you which of the two you are holding.

**THE RULE, in two halves of which only one is permitted to you:**

> ✓ **DECODING** — *if* a pair exists between a **strong** verb and a
> **weak I in `-jan`** (`liggian` "to lie" / `leggian` "to lay" ·
> `ganesan` "to come through" / `nerian` "to save" · `līthan` "to go" /
> `laidian` "to lead"), then **the strong one is the intransitive and the
> weak one is the transitive causative**.
>
> ⚠⚠⚠ **ENCODING — FORBIDDEN.** **You do not manufacture a `-jan`
> causative on a strong verb that has none** — adding `-jan` to
> `wahsan`, to `freusan` or to `brekan` to get a transitive produces a
> form that **nothing attests**. **The system is INHERITED and CLOSED
> since before 550**: of fifteen verbs examined, **ten have no causative
> at all, in any Germanic language**. A living process would have filled
> those gaps; it did not.

⚠ **AND THE CLASS PREDICTS NOTHING.** `brekan`, `sleutan`, `heffian`,
`faldan` are **strong AND transitive**, without the least causative. A
strong verb is intransitive **only in the inherited pairs**.

**WHAT IS PRODUCTIVE, ON THE OTHER HAND, AND WHERE YOU MAY BUILD** — the
**deadjectival** system:

| base | "to become X" | "to make X" |
|---|---|---|
| an adjective | ⚠⚠⚠ **NO SUFFIX — `werthan` + the adjective**, in STRONG agreement | **weak I in `-jan`** — transitive factitive |
| `nat` wet | **`wirthid nat`** "becomes wet" | **`nattian`** to wet |
| `opan` open | **`wirthid opan`** | **`opanōn`** to open |
| `ald` old | **`wirthid ald`**, pl. **`werthand alda`** | — |

⚠⚠⚠ **THERE IS NO INCHOATIVE SUFFIX.** The weak class III in `-ēn` is **dissolved** (2026-08-19): †`natēn`, †`aldēn` do not exist. *"To become X" is said with `werthan` + the adjective, and that is all.* ⚠ This column prescribed †`natēn` until 2026-08-26 — **the kit's only licence to build sent you forging in an abolished class.**

⚠⚠⚠ **WHEN THE CAUSATIVE IS MISSING — and it is missing two times in three:**
· **`werthan` + participle or adjective.** This is the central recourse,
  and it is **structurally necessary**: West Germanic has **LOST** the
  dedicated anticausative that Gothic still has (`uslūkan` to open /
  `uslūknan` to open by itself). **Frenkisk has no suffix for "to … by
  itself".** *"The door opens"* = **`thiu duri wirthid opan`**.
· **change the word.** For "to make grow", the language does not derive
  from `wahsan`: it takes another verb. ⚠ *The ones the enquirers name —
  `tiohan` "to rear", `fōdian` "to feed" — **are not in the
  dictionary**: that is a declared gap.*
· ⚠ **NOT the reflexive.** `sik` exists, but its **anticausative** use
  ("the door opens itself") only flourishes in Middle High German.
  **Both enquirers advise against it for 550.**

⚠⚠ **THE PREVERB NEVER CAUSATIVISES — one single exception, `bi-`.**
Preverbs **applicativise**: they promote an oblique to the rank of
object. They do not add a *causer*.
· **`bi-` transitivises, systematically**: `sitan` "to be seated" →
  `bisitan` "to dwell near" (+acc.), whence *bisitand* "neighbour";
  `sprekan` "to speak" → `bisprekan` "to speak ill of" (+acc.).
  ⚠ **It REASSIGNS the object**: `sleutan thia duri` = to shut the door,
  but `bisleutan thana man` = **to shut the man in**. Treat the
  preverbed form as a separate entry.
· **`ga-` does not transitivise** — it telicises, which *favours* the
  expression of an object: an effect of sense, not a rule.
· ⚠ **Decisive counter-test**: Gothic `ur-reisan` "to rise" stays
  **intransitive** despite its preverb; for "to raise" you still need the
  causative `ur-raisjan`. **A preverb never replaces a causative.**
· ⚠ **`and-` forms the ANTONYM without touching the valency**: `sleutan`
  "to lock" → **`andsleutan` "to unlock"**, both transitive.

### ⚠⚠⚠ AN UNMARKED RECIPIENT IS IN THE BARE DATIVE (2026-08-25)

The default-government ruling says that a **transitive with no mention
governs the ACCUSATIVE**. ⚠ It settled only the OBJECT — and it is
through that gap that two translators invented a syntax for `sendian`.
**Here is the missing half:**

> **A three-argument verb with no mention puts the RECIPIENT in the BARE
> DATIVE.** Gothic, Old Saxon and Old High German give this
> **independently** — the rule owes nothing to Old English.

⚠⚠ **Three corollaries, and they remove the three inventions observed:**
· **the recipient is NEVER in the accusative.** The double accusative
  exists, but for other patterns (teaching, depriving, predicatives);
· **no preposition is required.** An added preposition is either a
  Latinism or a **real direction** — `te` + dative if one goes *towards*
  a place, `fan` + dative for the **source**;
· **default order: verb — DATIVE — ACCUSATIVE**, the prepositional
  phrase after the accusative, **the complement clause always last**.
  ⚠ A pronoun, whatever its case, comes early.

⚠ **WHAT THE RULE DOES NOT SETTLE**, and both enquirers declare it:
**the order of TWO pronoun complements.** No witness gives it for 550 —
the *Heliand* is an alliterative poem where order follows the metre, and
the psalter is an interlinear tracing of Latin. *We have no continental
prose that is not a calque before the 9th century.* **Impose
dative–accusative on yourself and declare it as your convention.**

⚠ **And under NEGATION**, an accusative object may shift to the
**partitive genitive** — a common Germanic trait, attested in Gothic, in
OHG and in Norse. This holds for every verb of this class.

### ⚠⚠⚠ A GLOSS IS NOT A DEFINITION (2026-08-23)

**Before using a verb on the strength of its gloss, ask yourself one
question: is the glossing verb labile?**

⚠ **The glosses are English since 2026-08-30** (this line said they were
still French until 2026-09-01, and it was wrong for four days), and
*to finish, to open, to freeze, to fold, to grow, to heal, to pass* are
**transitive AND intransitive in English exactly as they were in
French** — the labile gloss is a property of the glossing language
whichever one it is, and that is why the question below survives the
translation unchanged —
exactly as *finish, open, freeze, fold, grow, heal, pass* are in English.
The trap is the same in both languages, and it does not go away when the
glosses turn. An entry that writes only "finir" **has told you nothing**.
⚠ **Sixteen verbs are in this position**; the entry carries `tr.` or
`intr.` when the question has been settled.

**And an entry's silence covers only the OBJECT.** The default-government
ruling gives the accusative to an unmarked transitive — ⚠⚠ *it says
nothing about the RECIPIENT or the SOURCE.* "To sell", "to send", "to
show" call for a third argument that no gloss announces, and that
`rektio` cannot see: it looks for a preposition **left hanging**, and
those glosses are closed.

> ⚠⚠⚠ **A SILENT GLOSS IS NOT A PERMISSIVE GLOSS.** To build a verb on a
> slot its entry does not name **is to invent** — report it. *It is the
> only fault that leaves no trace in a successful translation.*

⚠ **And two glossing words under one lemma are not always synonyms**:
`duri` glosses "porte" AND "seuil" — door and threshold, two different
places. **Thirty lemmas carry two senses from distinct domains this
way.** When your sentence opposes the two, the language does not.

### ⚠⚠⚠ THE REGISTER OF USES — the third register (2026-08-23)

**You have all the words and you cannot build the sentence.** This table
is made for that case, and for that case only. ⚠ *Neither a missing word
nor an inflection slot: the other two registers cannot see it.*

**How to read the "status" column — and this is the instruction manual:**

| status | what you do |
|---|---|
| ✓ **SETTLED NEGATIVELY** | the language cannot say it that way. **The rendering is not a makeshift, it is the right form.** Obey |
| ⚠ **CONVERGENT** | no rule, but **two translators who never met wrote this rendering**. Copy it: a convergent precedent removes invention as well as a form does |
| ⚠⚠ **UNSETTLED** | no rule, **and no measured convergence**: the rendering opposite is what **ONE** translator wrote, once. Use it as a starting point, **never as a warrant** |
| ⚠⚠ **DIVERGENT** | they wrote two different things. **The slot is genuinely open**: you are inventing, but you know it, and you report it |
| ⚠⚠⚠ **INTERSECTION** | each rule is sound alone, their crossing is settled nowhere. ⚠ *This is the most dangerous kind: it leaves no trace in a translation — one crosses it without seeing it* |

⚠⚠ **A CONVERGENT PRECEDENT IS NOT A RULING**, and this table does not
dress it up as one. It says: *two people who never met wrote this.*
⚠ *The reasoning behind each line is in the register `emplois.md`,
outside the kit: **you do not need it in order to write.***

| you want to say… | write | status |
|---|---|---|
| the **RECIPROCAL** — “they speak to one another” | **`the ain … themu andaron`** “the one … to the other” — ⚠⚠ **THE CASE COMES FROM THE VERB'S GOVERNMENT, NOT FROM THE FORMULA**: with `sprekan`, which requires `te` + dative, one writes **`the ain te themu andaron`** | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| the **WEATHER IMPERSONAL** — “it is raining”, “it is freezing” | **`it reganōd`**, **`it friusid`** — dummy subject `it` | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “**unless**” | **turn it into a negative protasis**: `of hē ni sunnid` “if he pleads no impediment” | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “**later**” | **`lator`**, the `-or`/`-ost` series, indeclinable | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “**at last**”, “in the end” | **`te leston`** (`te` + substantivised weak dative) | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “**since** + duration” — “for three days now” | **the accusative of duration**: `thrī dagā` — ⚠ which says *“FOR three days”* | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “to **fine** N shillings” | `themu werthēn N skillingā gadōmd` · `the sī N skillingō skuldīg` | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “to **begin to**” + infinitive | **drop the inchoative**: `samnōda sik` “gathered” | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “**at the price that**” — a genitive of price without a numeral | **`thes werthas, the …`** — genitive + article + relative | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| the **PARENTHETICAL of direct speech** — “he said”, “the woman said” | **postpose in V1**: `”, quath hē` | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| **`thār` + preposition** in DEICTIC use — “thereon” | **`thār oba`** as an autonomous adverb | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “**six hundred**” — ellipsis of the counted noun | **repeat the noun**: `sehs hund skillingō` | **✓ SETTLED NEGATIVELY** |
| impersonal `man` **×** a subordinate clause with a final verb **×** an object | **switch to the process passive**: `that … gasald werthē` | **⚠⚠⚠ INTERSECTION** |
| second-position clitic **×** a NEGATIVE QUANTIFIER subject | **`that ina nihain man ni dwingan mahti`** — clitic before the quantifier | **⚠⚠⚠ INTERSECTION** |
| the negative **V1 licence** **×** a COORDINATED second member | **apply it all the same**: `endi ni standis thū hwergin` | **⚠⚠⚠ INTERSECTION** |
| an adjective under a POSSESSIVE — strong or weak? | **follow the calibration**: `mit sīnero aiganero hendi` | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| the **FREE RELATIVE `that`** after a verb that selects `that` — “one does not say what he must do” | **`hwat hē dōn skal`** — switch to the interrogative | **⚠⚠⚠ INTERSECTION** |
| the **REFLEXIVE POSSESSIVE** `sīn` under a **COORDINATED SUBJECT** — “the smith and his neighbour” | **`sīn gabūr`** — the reflexive refers to the first member | **⚠⚠⚠ INTERSECTION** |
| the **GENITIVE OF THE THING** of `frāgōn` **×** a complement clause | **`frāgōda ina, hwat hē …`** — with no genitive mark | **⚠⚠⚠ INTERSECTION** |
| the **CLITIC GROUP** in the **COORDINATED SECOND MEMBER** — “and he sold it to…” | **`endi salda it ainemu kaupmanne`** — verb welded to `endi`, clitic after | **⚠⚠⚠ INTERSECTION** |
| “**the eve**” — the day before | **`thiu naht furi …`** “the night before” — ⚠ *and it is better than a makeshift*: Germanic **counts in NIGHTS, not in days** | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “to **discuss**” — in the neutral sense of exchanging views | **`sprekan umbi`** + accusative, the interlocutor in **`mid`** + dative — `sprekan mid imu umbi thea saka` | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| the **THRESHOLD** against the **DOOR** | **FORM NOT SETTLED**: `duristōthal` (n.) “door-support” · or `swalli` (n.) — ⚠ in the meantime, `duri` for the door and a periphrasis for the threshold | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “**ugliness**” — the abstract of `unskauni` | **`unskaunī`** (weak fem., on the direct OHG model) · or **`unskaunitha`** (fem. `-ō`, which **disambiguates** the homophony with the adjective) | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “**proverb**”, the sententious saying one quotes | **a phrase, not a lexeme**: `ald spel` “ancient saying”, or `frōd word` “wise word” | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “**rare**” — the adjective | **`seldan` + verb** (`seldan kumid hē`) for frequency · **`thunni`** for density | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “to **scatter**” | **`tefaran`** (VI, intransitive) “to go in all directions” · **`tedrīban`** (I, + acc.) “to scatter someone” | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “to **decide**”, “to take a decision” | **`rād neman`** “to take counsel”, or **`rādan`** + dative “to deliberate and settle” | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “to **hope**” | **`bīdan` + GENITIVE** “to wait for in expectation” · or **`wānian` + genitive** · or **`trūon an`** + dative | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “to **stop**”, to halt | **`stillo (ga)standan`** — the adverb is obligatory, the verb alone does not say stopping | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “**three times**”, a COUNTED frequency | **`thrī stundā`** — numeral 1–3 declined + adverbial accusative plural | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “**nothing to say**” — the MODAL infinitive | **`niowiht te seggianne`** | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| the complement of **CAUSE** — “woke AT the noise” | A **changed the subject** (`haurda thena galm, warth gawakōd`); B stretched the source (`fan themu galmē`) | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| the **INSTRUMENT NOUN** — “the thing one does X with” | A compounded `*īsarnhand` “iron hand”; B derived `*tanga` from Proto-Germanic | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| “**without** (his doing X)” | A **cut the source sentence in two** and wrote an autonomous negative assertion (`Ni hreup ina nihain man`), on the model of the return to a negative protasis prescribed for “unless”; B wrote `āna that` + subjunctive | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| `werthan` + participle — **process passive** or **inchoative**? | A: **`warth gawakōd`**, participle of the INTRANSITIVE `wakōn` — “he became awake” · B: **`warth gawakt`**, participle of the CAUSATIVE `wekkian` — “he was woken **by someone**” | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| the **MOOD** of the complement clause after a verb of KNOWLEDGE in the preterite — “he knew that…” | A: present indicative **`līthid`** (the present carries what is to come, and the content is taken as a fact) · B: preterite subjunctive **`quāmi`** (by analogy with the open slot for commitment) | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| the **MASS** noun modified by a genitive or an adjective — “the water OF THE BUCKET”, “the COLD iron” | A: **`watar thes aimbaras`**, bare · B: **`that watar thes aimbaras`** and **`that kalda īsarn`**, determined | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “**few**” before a COUNTABLE | **`mit luttil-emu mannō`** — quantifier in the case of the preposition + complement in the genitive plural | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| “to **be worth**”, comparing the value of two things | C changed verb: **`wag`** (`wegan` “to weigh”) · D stretched the recorded use: **`gald`** (`geldan`) | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| “**the winter before**” — a time relative to the narrative | C: **`thena leston wintar`** (lexicalised superlative, “the last winter”) · D: **`thena wintar air`** (on the pattern `that jār air`) | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| the **ACTIVE PERFECT** `hebbian` + participle at 550 | both wrote **`hebbiam … gageban`** | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| ⚠⚠⚠ the **PLACE OF THE VERB** in a coordinated second member **with its own subject** | E: **`endi fader nam watar`** (subject then verb, V2) · F: **`endi nam fader watar`** (verb welded to `endi`, subject postposed) | **✓ SETTLED** |
| **VERB ELLIPSIS** (gapping) — “the mother took the bread and the father the water” | **both REPEATED the verb** (`endi … nam watar`), in the name of the absolute rule “never a calque” | **✓ SETTLED** |
| **two `endi` in one sentence** | **both CUT the source sentence in two** | **✓ SETTLED NEGATIVELY** |
| the **SERIES** — coordinating **three** clauses | ⚠⚠⚠ **DO NOT COPY THIS RENDERING — THE RULING ON COORDINATION REFUSES IT** (see the right-hand column). What **the two translators of the 60th round** had written, before the enquiry: asyndeton for the intermediate members, `endi` for the last | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| the cry of an **ANIMAL** — “the dog barked” | **both wrote `hreup`** (`hrōpan` extended to a dog), **both** declaring the shift of sense | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| the **WALL OF A HOUSE** | **both wrote `mūra`**, **both** declaring it false by material | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| two subjects under **NEGATIVE correlation** — “neither of the two spoke” | **both wrote, word for word: `Ni sprak noh the ain noh the andaro`** — verb in the **SINGULAR** | **⚠ CONVERGENT** |
| two **NEGATED** coordinated members | **`noh … noh`** (Old Du `nohne`, OHG `noh ni`, Goth. `nih`) — and the preverbal `ni` **is repeated before each FINITE verb**; a single `ni`, on the other hand, **carries over** two coordinated **infinitives** | **✓ SETTLED** |
| the **clitic immediately after `endi`** | **`endi im …`**, **`endi ina …`** — the unstressed pronoun leans immediately on the coordinator | **✓ SETTLED** |
| “**a reason**”, a motive | Q **restructured** (*ni quithis thū bī thiu wār* “you do not speak true for all that”) · R used **`saka`** while declaring the judicial tinge | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| “to **belong to someone**” — predicative possession | Q: **predicative genitive** with `wesan` — *thia akkrā sint mīn*, *sia sint thero erda* · R: **reversal on `aigan`** “to own” — *thia akkrā aih ik*, *thiu erdu sia aih* | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| the **IMPERFECT** — “he was carrying”, “no one knew” | **the plain preterite**, and nothing else | **✓ SETTLED NEGATIVELY** |
| “to **summon**” in the neutral sense | no recorded turn | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| “to **be mistaken**” | the two translators of the 42nd round had **diverged**; the two of the 67th and the two of the 68th used `mislingid`, without conferring | **✓ SETTLED** |
| “to **give a judgement**” | no recorded turn | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| a **DURATION MEASURED FROM A POINT** — “for three winters now”, “two years ago” | ⚠ the kit has `thrī dagā` “**for** three days” — **duration, not anteriority** | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| the **MOOD OF THE INDIRECT QUESTION** — “tell us what he was carrying”, “he asked when” | **both renderings, and both are argued from the kit**: · **SUBJUNCTIVE** — `Segi uns, hwat hē drōgi`: *“the trigger of the subjunctive is the speaker's NON-COMMITMENT”* (ruling on `wānian`) · **INDICATIVE** — `Segi uns, hwat hē drōg`: *“the subjunctive marks NON-REALISATION, never subordination in itself; here the event is presupposed real, only a variable is unknown — unlike `of`, where it is POLARITY that is at stake”* | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “**not … anything any more**” — the cumulation of “no longer” and “nothing” | both renderings: · `ni sprak hē mair` “he spoke no more”, declaring that `niowiht` would be **a calque of the source language** · `ni quath hē mair niowiht`, by negative concord, licit and frequent | **⚠⚠ DIVERGENT** |
| “to **establish as true**” — and WHICH of the two verbs | take `wārōn`: it is the one whose government is written | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| “a **blade**”, the cutting part of a weapon or a tool | name the whole object — `swerd`, `sahs` — or the metal, `īsarn` | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| “a **tool**”, the generic instrument | name the particular tool, or turn it into the verb — “what one works with” | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| “to **melt**”, “to **mend**” — the smith's two operations | not settled; a candidate must come from an enquiry, not from the neighbouring languages | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| “a **sequence**”, an ordered series | `riga` under protest, and say so | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |
| “**unpredictable**”, “an **output**”, “a **property**” | unpack into a clause — “what no man can say beforehand”, “what the system produces” | **✓ SETTLED NEGATIVELY** |
| the 3rd singular in **`-it`** — after WHICH stems does it survive? | write `-id`, and say so, unless the entry prints `-it` | **⚠⚠ UNSETTLED** |

**67 recorded uses, of which 25 the language DOES NOT SETTLE** (divergent and intersections). ⚠ *The register is young: it was born 2026-08-23 out of what ten rounds had already shown without anyone knowing where to write it.*

⚠ **If you cross an intersection that is not here, it is worth more than
the rest of your report**: it leaves no trace in a translation, so
nobody will find it again without you.


### ⚠⚠ WHAT THE LANGUAGE DOES NOT HAVE — the DECLARED gaps

> ⚠⚠⚠ **THIS SECTION AND THE REGISTER OF USES CARRIED DIFFERENT SUBSETS
> OF THE SAME THING, AND THAT WAS corrected ON 2026-08-26.** Of its ten
> items, **five were not in the register** — the imperfect, "to summon",
> "to be mistaken", "to hand down a judgment", duration measured from a
> point. They are there now.
>
> ⚠⚠ **WHAT THAT CHANGES FOR YOU**: the register of uses is the complete
> list and **it is guarded by a control**; this section carries the
> **REASONING** and the **PROVENANCE** of each gap — who found it, when,
> and what it cost. *If you are looking for what the language lacks, go
> to the register. If you want to know why we believe it, stay here.*
>
> ⚠ **AND IT IS THIS STRUCTURE THAT PRODUCED EVERY CONTRADICTION OF THE
> WEEK**: two places saying the same thing, **of which only one is
> guarded**.
**Established — do not look, the language does not have it:**
· **no imperfect.** One preterite only. *"The word he was keeping back"*
  and *"the word he spoke"* have the same form: **the aspectual contrast
  English marks disappears**, and that is the language, not you.
· **no neutral "to summon"** — `lathōn` is a summons to court.
· ⚠⚠ **NO VERB OF JOY** — "to rejoice", "to be glad". The lexicon has the
  adjective `blīthi` and the noun `blīthskepi`, **and no verb**. The
  impersonal-affect section gives `mik hriuwid`, `mik skamōd`, `mī
  langōd`: **the whole range of the negative is there; joy is not.**
  Reported separately by both translators of the 42nd round, one noting
  that *"the gap is exactly where the kit boasts of being better than
  the source language"*. **One writes `blīthi werthan`** — it is a
  periphrasis, and we know it.
· ⚠⚠ **NO VERB OF ERROR** — "to be mistaken". `dreugan` is "to deceive
  SOMEONE", transitive and agentive; `missidād` is the **actionable**
  misdeed. ⚠ And the reflexive rule licenses `sik` only for *"a subject
  that moves or alters of itself"* — **being mistaken is neither a
  movement nor an alteration.** The two translators of the 42nd round
  diverged here, one forging †`sik dreugan`, the other shifting sense to
  `themu mislingid` "it fails for him". *It is one of only two
  divergences in that round, and it is lexical.*
· ⚠ **NO TURN FOR "IT IS BETTER TO"** — no verb "to be worth" in the
  sense of relative value; `geldan furi` is legal. The eight recorded
  impersonals cover affect and necessity, **never evaluation**. Both
  translators assembled `bet ist that…` + complement clause, **each on
  his own and identically** — so that is the workaround to use, knowing
  the kit does not supply it.
  ⚠⚠ **THIS GAP IS NARROWED, 2026-09-02, by a double enquiry** — and
  narrowed by a word the dictionary already had. **The value equation is
  `werth` + GENITIVE**, `thit ist thero tweio werth` "this is worth two
  of those"; both enquirers reached it separately and rated it alike.
  For **"worth more than"**, the comparison does not ride on `werth` at
  all — Germanic never built a comparative of it — but on a **degree
  word**, with the standard in the **NOMINATIVE** after `than`:
  **`hros ist diuriro than ohso`**.
  ⚠ `obar`/`ouir` is FORBIDDEN here: it is a word-for-word calque of
  Latin *super*, and the
  Wachtendonck psalter — the primary witness of Frankish — yields no
  native value-comparison at all. ⚠⚠ **WHAT REMAINS OPEN IS NOT THE
  KIT'S DOING: no West Germanic witness compares the value of two goods,
  in any text, and both enquirers reported that separately.**
  Merovingian law tariffs each thing against a price; it never sets two
  of them against each other.
    *A gap the record itself carries cannot be closed by looking harder.
     It can only be declared, and assembled from parts.*
· ⚠⚠ **NO VERB "TO WAKE UP"** — the intransitive change of state.
  `wakōn` is durative, "to be awake, to keep watch"; `wekkian` is its
  transitive causative, "to wake someone". **Neither says that a sleeper
  woke**, and no periphrasis on `werthan` will say it either: settled
  2026-09-02, `warth gawakt` means "was woken **by someone**". The
  language wants a verb of its own here — Gothic type `gawaknan`, OHG
  `irwahhan` — and the dictionary has none. ⚠ **Opened by the enquiry
  that closed the passive**, and it is the 58th round's own sentence
  that has been waiting on it.
· ⚠⚠ **NO VERB "TO AVAIL, TO PROFIT"** — "that avails nothing", "what
  does it profit a man". The gap was invisible for as long as the kit
  read the English **"not worth the trouble"** as a thing to translate.
  An enquirer of 2026-09-02 rated that phrase **35%** as inherited
  Germanic and named it a probable medieval Romance idiom (*valere
  poenam*, whence Italian and Spanish *vale la pena* — the two
  identical to the letter — beside *der Mühe wert* and *de
  moeite waard*). **The source phrase was itself the calque**, and the native
  turn it should have been translated by needs a verb the dictionary
  does not carry.
    *A gap can hide behind a bad translation of the very thing that
     would have revealed it.*

⚠⚠ **THESE THREE ARE OF A DIFFERENT NATURE FROM THE OTHERS, AND IT MUST
BE SAID.** Every gap above is morphological or syntactic; these are
**lexical**. A translator in the 42nd round gives the reason:
> *"The imbalance does not show in the project's instruments **because
> the instruments count SLOTS, and a missing word is not a slot**. The
> kit is armed for the Salic law and disarmed for a tale."*
*The register of slots, `kollat`, `kasus`, `klassa`: not one of them can
see an absent verb. Only a round of translation meets it.*
· **no turn for "to hand down a judgment"** — `dōmian` alone, or the
  figura etymologica, which no witness records.
· ⚠⚠ **no productive MOTIONAL FEMININE** — "the neighbour-woman", "the
  shepherdess" have no derivation. `kuningin` is the sole witness to an
  `-in` suffix that the word-formation section **does not declare
  productive**, and the suffix is declined nowhere in the kit. **A SYSTEM gap**, found in the 40th
  round, the only forge of that round.

· **no FREESTANDING POSSESSIVE PRONOUN** — "his", "mine". `sīn` is an
  adjective, and its reflexive use points to the subject of its **own**
  clause: in "older than his", it would say something else. **One repeats
  the noun** (*is hros*). A real loss, not a fault.
  ⚠ **RECLASSIFIED ON 2026-08-21 — this is NOT a gap, it is a slot closed
  by periphrasis**, and the double ⚠⚠ is withdrawn from it. Translator L,
  who met it: *"the kit supplies the complete solution AND the trap to
  avoid. **It is misfiled: filing it with the real gaps dilutes the
  warning.**"* **A gap is what a translator has to invent in front of.**
  Here he does not invent: he applies.
· ⚠ **the WEATHER verb has no recorded subject** — the impersonal section
  covers only **affect** (`mik hriuwid`), which has an experiencer. "It is
  raining" has none. The 40th round wrote `it regano` — ⚠⚠ *a WRONG form:
  `reganōn` is a weak II, its 3rd sg. is **`reganōd`**. Caught in the 52nd
  round, where a translator had to recompute it: "a false precedent is
  worse than an absent one".* **Write `it reganōd`**, **guessing**, and
  say so.
· ⚠⚠ **THE COMPLEMENT CLAUSE AFTER A VERB OF UNDERTAKING IN THE
  PRETERITE — OPEN SLOT, DECLARED 2026-08-21, AND BOTH FORMS ARE GIVEN.**
  "He promised that he would come back", "he swore that he would not
  leave". The kit settles the mood after a verb of **opinion**
  (subjunctive) and after a **prospective** temporal clause (present
  indicative); **it does not settle the complement clause governed by a
  verb of undertaking in the preterite.**
  · **`that hē witherkumē`** — **present** subjunctive, on the rule that
    "the present carries what is to come";
  · **`that hē witherquāmi`** — **preterite** subjunctive, by analogy
    with *ni fargitid hē thes, that thū dādi*.
  ⚠ **The two translators of the 44th round each wrote one of the two**,
  and one notes: *"I do not know whether I wrote 'that he would come
  back' or 'that he had come back'"*. **No enquiry has been run. One
  writes either and declares it; one does not pretend there is a rule.**
· ⚠⚠ **THE MOOD OF THE INDIRECT QUESTION — A DECISION, TAKEN
  2026-09-03 by double enquiry.** It is entered as a DECISION and not as
  a finding, and the difference is not modesty: the witnesses do not
  settle it. Every admissible corpus is a translation; **Latin requires
  the subjunctive in indirect questions obligatorily**, by a formal rule
  of its own that is indifferent to meaning, so a subjunctive in Tatian
  or in the Wachtendonck psalter is predicted by the source and is worth
  nothing. The two places where a Germanic indirect question stands
  outside that shadow are the free passages of the *Heliand* and
  Otfrid's own composition — and **neither enquirer could cite a line of
  either**. What follows is therefore a default with an address, not a
  reading of the record.

  **THE RULE — after `hwat`, `hwanne`, `hwār`, `hwē`, `hwō`: the
  INDICATIVE.** One declared exception: the **DELIBERATIVE** clause, an
  act not yet chosen, takes **the SUBJUNCTIVE**. The minimal pair, which
  both enquirers wrote separately and identically:
  · `hē ne wissa hwat hē sagdi` — "he did not know **what to say**",
    the words not yet chosen: SUBJUNCTIVE;
  · `hē ne wissa hwat hē sagda` — "he did not know **what he had
    said**", the words spoken: INDICATIVE.

  ⚠⚠⚠ **THE INTERROGATIVE WORD IS INERT.** Both enquirers say so, and no
  rule of the shape "after `hwat`, use X" can be right. The mood is
  chosen by **what the embedded clause is**, never by what governs it —
  which is the reverse of the Latin habit, and worth noticing, because
  every factor the kit was tempted by is a property of the MATRIX.
  Three are rejected outright, by both enquirers separately.
  · **NEGATION of the matrix does nothing.** "Nobody knew where the
    boundary ran" is INDICATIVE. Denying the knowing does not cancel the
    boundary: the clause still asserts that there was one. Gothic
    negated verbs of knowing take the indicative regularly.
  · **INTERROGATION of the matrix does nothing.**
  · **THE TENSE of the matrix does nothing.** *Consecutio temporum* is a
    Latin rule keyed to tense, and Germanic at this depth has no
    sequence of moods. A preterite matrix changes nothing.

  ⚠⚠⚠ **AND THE SETTLED RULE DOES NOT GENERALISE — IT IS COMPLETE
  AS IT STANDS.** The rule for `of` had been treated as a fragment
  awaiting the rest of its paradigm. It is not. `of` asks **whether** a proposition
  holds, and suspends the whole of it: non-assertion, hence the
  subjunctive. `hwat` asks only **which value** fills a variable in a
  proposition already presupposed true — "tell us what he was carrying"
  presupposes that he carried something. **The feature that earns the
  subjunctive after `of` is absent by definition from the interrogative
  words.** The kit's own stated motive for the `of` rule — *"the
  question is open, the indicative would close it"* — is precisely the
  motive that forbids extending it.
  ⚠ One enquirer supplies the test that separates the two readings, and
  it answers the obvious objection that `of` is subjunctive merely
  because it is also the conditional "if". Gothic marks the polar
  indirect question with the **enclitic `-u`, which has no conditional
  use whatever** — and it still writes the optative there **against a
  Greek indicative**. The mood belongs to polar interrogativity, not to
  the conditional homonym.
  ⚠⚠ **A UNIFORM "INDIRECT QUESTION → SUBJUNCTIVE" RULE IS LATIN'S RULE**,
  and both enquirers warn that adopting it for tidiness would import the
  very calque this kit exists to exclude. The asymmetry is the finding.
    *Two rules, however inelegant, are what the evidence carries. One
     rule would be Latin syntax wearing a Germanic coat.*

  ⚠⚠ **WHAT REMAINS DIVERGENT, and it is registered and not averaged:
  whether the MATRIX VERB conditions the mood at all.** One enquirer
  accepts it as the second-ranked factor: a verb of ASKING defines its
  complement as unresolved, so *"he asked when the man would come"*
  takes the **subjunctive**: `hē frāgōda hwanne thē man kwāmi`. The
  other rejects
  it as **epiphenomenal**: verbs of asking correlate with the
  subjunctive only because their complements happen more often to be
  open, and where the content is settled the indicative returns, `hē
  frāgōda hwanne thē man kwam` — adding expressly that *"the subjunctive
  would say the coming is deliberated, and nobody is deciding whether to
  come."* **They give opposite moods on two of three test sentences.**
  Until that is settled, the rule above stands on its first clause: when
  the content is settled, write the indicative even after a verb of
  asking; when it is deliberative, write the subjunctive whatever the
  matrix.

  ⚠⚠ **READ 2026-09-07 — THE LINES EXIST, AND THEY WERE READ.** Two
  readers, separately, on the Heliand's free passages alone (the
  witnesses being on the machine since that morning):
  · the DIRECT deliberative question takes **`skulan` or `magan` in the
    INDICATIVE + infinitive** — Hel. 3259 *huat scal ik manages duan*,
    3308 *huat sculun uui thes te lône nimen*, 4606 *hue scal that, frô
    mîn, uuesen*, Hel. 272 *huô mag that giuuerðen sô*: seven lines, and no
    bare subjunctive in a direct question anywhere;
  · the INDIRECT deliberative puts the modal in the subjunctive, or
    stands in the bare subjunctive — Hel. 1901 *huat gi im than tegegnes
    sculin*, 3856 *saga huat thu is uuillies*, Tat. 183 *ni uuestun uuaz
    sie imo antuurtitin*;
  · the SETTLED content after a negated verb of knowledge stays in the
    INDICATIVE — Hel. 2040 *ne uuissa te uuârun, huô he it mid is uuordu
    gesprac*, Tat. 046 *ni uuesta uuanan íz uúas*.
  So the rule stands ATTESTED on both its clauses, and the 83rd round's
  fork `dōmis · dōmies` is closed: in the direct question write `hwat
  skal ik dōmian?`, in the indirect `hē ne wissa hwat hē dōmian skoldi`
  (or the bare `dōmdi`). Both readers 80 %.

  ⚠⚠ **THE TENSE, WHICH THIS DECISION ILLUSTRATED AND NEVER STATED —
  added 2026-09-06 by double enquiry.** The 83rd round wrote "nobody knew
  when the men would eat" as `hwanne sia ātun`, and both enquirers
  say that means "when the men ate": a fact, not a prospect. **Under a
  preterite matrix the subordinate verb takes the PRETERITE** — a
  sequence of tenses that is Germanic-native: at Luke 1:29 Wulfila writes
  the preterite `wesi` where the Greek offers only a present optative,
  and Greek has no past optative he could have copied. That is tense,
  not mood. And **relative futurity is carried by `skulan` in the
  preterite**, not by any tense of the lexical verb: `hwanne sia
  etan skoldun` — the Gothic device at John 12:33, `skulda gaswiltan`
  "was to die", and the Wachtendonck psalter's own future in `sal`,
  chosen against a Latin that has no periphrastic future to copy.
  · the present (`etand`) is out — 5 % from both, separately: no witness
    shifts to a present under a preterite matrix outside quasi-direct
    speech;
  · the plain preterite indicative is FACTUAL ONLY — "when they ate";
  · ⚠ **DIVERGENT on the form that carries the prospect**, and
    registered: one enquirer puts the preterite subjunctive of the verb
    first (`ātin`, 45 %, on Mark 9:6 and 14:40 where Gothic writes a
    preterite optative against a Greek aorist subjunctive), the other
    the modal (`skoldun` / `skoldin`, 65 %). Both admit both.
    ⚠ **READ 2026-09-07 — the Heliand writes the MODAL**: 583 *thô sagda he that
    hêr scoldi cuman ên uuîscuning*, 585, 586, 590, 622, 130 — six lines
    of `scoldi` + infinitive after *quað / sagda*; the bare preterite
    subjunctive once, 2102 *quað that he thar quâmi*; *uueldi* is
    volitional, never a future. The modal is written first, ATTESTED;
    the bare subjunctive is the attested minority (both readers 75–80 %).
    Write the
    modal when the sense is "were to", the subjunctive when it is
    "should"; write neither the present nor the bare preterite.
  ⚠ Both enquirers add that after a NEGATED verb of knowing the
    witnesses show the subjunctive more often than this decision's
    "negation does nothing" allows — for prospective content. The
    decision stands for FACTUAL content, which is what it was written
    for; a future-in-the-past is not factual, and takes the preterite
    forms above.

  ⚠ **THE DEFAULT WAS CHOSEN BY THE WIDTH OF ITS LICIT RANGE, NOT BY
  FREQUENCY.** The indicative is always available for settled content;
  the subjunctive there is at best optional and at worst wrong. So an
  indicative default yields sentences a speaker would accept even where
  he might have chosen otherwise, whereas a subjunctive default is wrong
  across the whole majority environment. **It is the asymmetry of the
  error that picks the default, not the balance of the usage.**
  ⚠ **AND THE COST IS DECLARED**: if the true distribution was three to
  one rather than uniform, this kit writes a language flatter than the
  one it reconstructs. That loss is real, bounded, and visible — which
  is more than can be said for the alternative. Declining to rule does
  not avoid the choice; **it hands the choice to whichever language the
  translator learned the construction in**, and every one of them pushes
  toward the subjunctive.

  ⚠⚠ **WHAT WOULD OVERTURN THIS DECISION** — one shape, and both
  enquirers named it: a passage of the *Heliand* with **no Latin
  counterpart**, carrying a `hw-` question of **settled** content under
  a **positive** matrix, whose verb is an unambiguous **strong preterite
  subjunctive** (`bāri`, `quāmi`, `gengi`). The strong verb matters
  because the mood then sits in the stem vowel and not in a levellable
  ending — and the metre matters more: **the subjunctive carries one
  syllable more than the indicative, and in alliterative verse the
  syllable count is load-bearing**, so such a form cannot be a scribal
  slip. Two or three such lines and this default is wrong.
  ⚠ **AND ONE WITNESS BEARS ON THE SETTLED RULE ITSELF**, the one
  for `of`: an enquirer
  reports that Gothic *John* 9:25 may show the **indicative** after
  `jabai`, which would make the settled rule a tendency and not an
  absolute. He flags it against his own argument. **Reference not
  verified.**
· ⚠⚠ **NO DURATION MEASURED FROM A POINT** — "for three winters now",
  "two years ago". The three recorded uses of time (determined moment,
  indefinite moment, iterative) are **all internal to the narrative**;
  `fon` is spatial, `sīthor` is adverb and conjunction, never a
  preposition of duration. ⚠ The kit has `thrī dagā` "**for** three days"
  — **duration, not anteriority**. Caught in the 43rd round, which wrote
  the accusative of duration for want of better: *"under negation the two
  almost overlap; **outside negation the slot would be empty**"*.
  ⚠ **This gap was neither in this list, nor in table ③, nor in the
  table of questions** — so it was not "unrecorded", it was
  **invisible**.
· ⚠ **the TEMPORAL DEICTIC — and one must be precise, my first draft was
  not.** The **word** exists: `morgan` is glossed "morning; **tomorrow**"
  in the dictionary. **It is the USE that is unsettled**: "tomorrow"
  belongs to none of the three uses of time (determined moment, indefinite
  moment, iterative), which are all **internal to the narrative**; a
  deictic is fixed on the **speaker**, a category the kit does not have.
  One writes `morgan`, and does not know whether it takes the accusative,
  the article, or nothing.
    ⚠⚠ *I had written "UNRECORDED" for a word the dictionary glosses —
    on the very day I was recasting this list to correct exactly that
    fault. Caught by a translator in the 41st round.*

**⚠ REMOVED FROM THIS LIST ON 2026-08-19 — THESE WERE FALSE GAPS:**
· ~~no oblique for the `-io` masculines~~ → `grāfio` carries `[n]`, and
  the key gives `-on`: the oblique is regular and derivable;
· ~~no genitive singular for the `-ō` feminine~~ → it is **`-a`**,
  written three times, including in the dictionary key
  (*thero wahsunga*);
· ~~the `-i` paradigm is not recorded~~ → it is, **in full**: dative `-i`
  umlauting on an `a` root, **feminine genitive = dative**, masculines on
  the a-stem.
  ⚠⚠ ~~But a real question opens there: the kit writes `gastes`,
  `gaste`…~~ → **SETTLED on 2026-08-21**: it was indeed an unmigrated
  line. **One writes `gastas`, `gastē`.** See § *THE `-i` MASCULINES*.
    ⚠⚠ **AND THE WAY THAT LINE SURVIVED IS THE LESSON OF THE 43rd
    ROUND.** The ruling was promulgated and **three passages citing the
    abrogated form remained**, two of them in the table generated from
    `cases.md` — that is, **inside the instrument built to prevent
    exactly this**. Translator L:
    > *"The kit diagnosed this defect, named it, made a golden rule of it
    > — **and committed it the same day**. It is the one point where I had
    > to arbitrate **against** the document instead of arbitrating with
    > it."*
    ⚠ **A declaration of openness OUTLIVES ITS OWN CLOSING if one does
    not sweep the list of gaps at the same time as the body.** The
    translator paid for the arbitration: *"writing the ten sentences took

> ⚠⚠ **WHAT FOLLOWS IS NOT DOCUMENTATION: it is where half the open
> slots live.** *(Order reversed on 2026-08-23: the list used to come
> after 3,000 tokens of narrative.)*


The criterion for what precedes comes from a translator in the 36th
round, and the project adopted it:

> *"A slot declared open makes the translator **give up**; a slot simply
> absent makes him **invent without knowing it**."*

⚠⚠ **THIS CRITERION IS CORRECTED ON 2026-08-20, and the correction comes
from BOTH translators of the 42nd round, separately.** The text put the
queen in the

**dative**, that is on the `kuningin` slot which the register declares
open, dated, motivated — and which even warns that two translators had
escaped it by chance.

> **I**: *"I read it, I recognised it, and **I invented anyway** —
> because to give up meant not translating the sentence. **A slot declared
> open does not make you GIVE UP: it makes you INVENT KNOWINGLY.** That
> is better than inventing unknowingly, but it is not what the criterion
> announces."*
>
> **J**: *"A warning without the form does not make you give up: it makes
> you invent knowingly."*

**Both wrote the SAME form** — `theru kuningin`, zero ending — and both
declared it. **The corrected criterion is therefore:**

> **A slot declared open does not remove invention: it makes it VISIBLE
> and CONVERGENT.** The translator invents all the same, because to give up
> would mean not translating — but he says so, and two independent
> translators then invent the same thing.

⚠⚠ **THIRD CORRECTION — 43rd ROUND, AND THE TWO TRANSLATORS REACH IT
SEPARATELY FROM TWO DIFFERENT ANGLES.** They had been asked, for every
open slot met: *did you see it before you stumbled on it? what did you
do? and did the declaration help you, or did it merely let you declare
your fault more tidily?* **Between them they met ten, and the ten are not
alike.**

**K finds the condition of PLACE.** Faced with `kū` and `bōk` he read the
entry, applied the class feature, and **invented without knowing he was
inventing** — the reservation existed, filed in the register of the noun:
> *"It is not in the list of gaps, nor in the dictionary, nor in the
> index. **The entry for `kū` carries `[rac]` without a word of
> reservation.** An open slot exists only if it is declared where one
> LOOKS FOR THE FORM, that is in the ENTRY. A declaration that carries
> only a doubt, filed in a register, is invisible: **it does not even
> make the invention visible, since the translator does not know he is
> inventing.**"*

**L finds the condition of CONTENT.** Of his six encounters, only one did
any work — the one that carried a form:
> *"The benefit of the declaration is neither surrender nor even
> traceability: **it is that the kit, by declaring, puts itself in a
> position to SUPPLY the reconstructed form.** Where it supplies nothing,
> the declaration is a statement of damage. **A useful open slot is a
> slot declared open AND FILLED UNDER RESERVE. The others serve the
> project only, never the translator.**"*

**The ten encounters then sort into four species, and only one is a real
gap:**
| what the declaration carries | effect on the translator | species |
|---|---|---|
| a **reconstructed form** (free relative, fem.) | he **copies** — no invention | ✓ slot **filled under reserve** |
| an **arbitration** (final clitic) | he **obeys** | ✓ slot **closed**, open on paper |
| a **workaround** (freestanding possessive) | he **obeys** | ✓ slot **closed by periphrasis** |
| a **doubt filed off the path** (`kū`, `bōk`) | ⚠⚠ he **invents without knowing** | ✗ **the only real gap** |

> ⚠⚠⚠ **FIFTH DRAFT — AND THE FIRST ONE MEASURED.** In the 51st round a
> translator classified the **eight** slots he met:
> · the two that carried a **FORM** or a **PRECEDENT** — the entry for
>   `hand`, table ⑧ — **removed the invention**;
> · the three with a **WARNING** gave nothing but *the right to
>   declare cleanly*;
> · the one **off the path he was searching** returned nothing.
>
> ⚠⚠ **TWO ADDITIONS.**
> **① A CONVERGENT PRECEDENT IS ENOUGH.** A useful slot does not need to
> be filled with a reconstructed form: table ⑧ prints only a precedent —
> *"… endi swīgōda"* — and **five sentences of a translation rest on it
> without divergence**. *That is cheaper than an enquiry, and it works.*
> **② THE PLACE IS THREEFOLD, NOT SINGLE.** The kit offers three paths —
> the table of QUESTIONS, the tables of use, the entry — and **a slot
> declared on one and silent on the others does not exist**: "since" was
> at the head of the kit and absent from table ③, where both translators
> looked for it.
>
> ⚠⚠ **CRITERION, THIRD DRAFT — a slot declared open only works if it
> meets TWO conditions: it is written WHERE ONE LOOKS FOR THE FORM, and
> it carries a FORM, an ARBITRATION or a WORKAROUND. A declaration with
> neither the place nor the content produces exactly the silent invention
> it claims to remove — with, on top, the illusion that it has been dealt
> with.**


⚠⚠ **AND A LIMIT OF THE INDICATOR ITSELF, FOUND ON 2026-08-21 BY AN
ENQUIRER WHO WAS NOT WORKING ON THIS QUESTION.** Everything above rests
on the convergence of two independent translators. But:

> *"Your two translators are not two witnesses. They share a first
> language that puts an article in front of 'la nuit' and 'der Nacht'.
> Two speakers of the same source language committing the same
> interference **do not confirm each other: they confirm it ONCE**. That
> both broke the rule is not a datum about Frankish; it is a datum about
> their own language."*

**They are independent of each other. They are not independent of the
SOURCE LANGUAGE.** Convergence is therefore worth something only if the
possible error is not an interference from that language. When it is —
**article, word order, a preposition where Germanic has a case** — the
convergence measures the source language, **and it looks exactly like a
success.**

⚠⚠⚠ **AND THIS WARNING GETS WORSE, NOT BETTER, NOW THAT THE SOURCE
LANGUAGE IS ENGLISH.** French interferences were CONSPICUOUS: an article
too many, a preposition where a case was wanted, Romance word order —
they stood out against a Germanic target. **English is itself Germanic.**
An English speaker who imports an English turn produces something that
*looks right*, and neither he nor a reader will flinch at it.

  *An interference you can see costs one correction. An interference
   that looks like the target language costs nothing at all — and stays.*

⚠ This is the same reason Old English is forbidden as a witness, arriving
by a second road: what is nearest is not what is safest. Distrust your
own fluency here more than you distrusted a Romance reflex.

**THE PROOF IS IN MY OWN FILES, AND IT HAD BEEN THERE FOR WEEKS.** An
earlier round saw both translators write †*an theru naht* for "by night"
— a form **unattested in all three witnesses**, an exact calque of the
French *la nuit*. **I recorded it as a trap avoided, in a tally headed
"6 traps out of 6".** It was 5 out of 6, and the sixth was a convergence
on an error.
  > **RULE: a convergence between translators counts as testimony ONLY if
  > one can say why the source language would not have produced it.
  > Otherwise it counts as ONE voice, not two — and on the points where
  > the source language has one construction and Germanic another, it
  > does not count at all.**


⚠⚠ **FOURTH DRAFT OF THE CRITERION — 44th ROUND, AND THE TWO TRANSLATORS
REACH IT SEPARATELY, BY TWO DIFFERENT FORMULATIONS OF THE SAME FACT.**

The register declared the `[ī]` class "NOT INSTRUCTED — the whole
inflection". **A ruling of 2026-08-17, four days earlier, gives its
complete singular paradigm**: `thiu menigi`, `thero menigi`, `theru
menigi`, with its warrant in the two continental witnesses. **Both
translators read the declaration, believed it, and prepared a
workaround** — one was considering changing the source text, the other
had already built †*mit wraithero wīsu*. **Both found the form by
accident, while looking for something else.**

> **M — the fifth species**: *"Your taxonomy is missing one: the FALSE
> declaration, written exactly where one looks for the form. It has the
> worst of both worlds — the place of the good declaration and the
> content of the bad one. It does not make you invent, **it makes you
> GIVE UP WRONGLY** — and a wrongful surrender leaves no divergent trace
> that `mesura` can count. **A translator who abandons a form the kit
> supplies hands in a poorer text AND A MORE FLATTERING REPORT.**"*
>
> **N — the third condition**: *"K found that the declaration must be in
> the right **place**. L found that it must carry a **content**. My fifth
> encounter is in the right place and carries an explicit content. **It
> is simply false.**"*

> ⚠⚠ **CRITERION, FOURTH DRAFT — a slot declared open only works if it
> meets THREE conditions: it is written WHERE ONE LOOKS FOR THE FORM ·
> it carries a FORM, an ARBITRATION or a WORKAROUND · **and it is
> TRUE**.**

⚠ **THE DANGER PECULIAR TO THE THIRD IS THAT IT IS INVISIBLE TO
MEASUREMENT.** A misplaced declaration makes you invent in silence — and
divergent invention shows up in the "divergences" column. **A false
declaration makes you give up — and two translators who both give up
CONVERGE.** *The instrument's worst defect produces its finest figure.*

⚠⚠ **AND THE CAUSE IS STRUCTURAL — N names it, and it condemns the way
`cases.md` is fed:**
> *"As long as the register's source is **what translators fail to
> find**, the register will measure the NAVIGABILITY of the kit and
> record it as a state of the LANGUAGE. A register that does not, before
> declaring a slot open, sweep the kit automatically for the very form it
> is about to declare absent, will go on turning gaps of navigation into
> gaps of language indefinitely."*
**This is exactly the fault the kit reproached itself with on 2026-08-19
under the name "confusing UNFINDABLE with ABSENT" — reproached then to
the list of gaps, corrected in the list of gaps, and REPEATED in the
instrument built to replace it.**

  ⚠ **The sweep has existed since 2026-08-21**: it is `deklar`, the 23rd
  control. It goes red as soon as a slot declared open on an inflection
  is contradicted by an oblique phrase written elsewhere in the kit. *It
  went red on `[ī]` before the slot was closed — the only proof worth
  anything that a control knows how to go red.*
⚠ *This cancels none of the rounds. It changes what one reads in the
"divergences" column of `mesura`: **a divergence remains a reliable
signal — it says that one of the two resisted the source language. A
convergence has to be qualified before it is credited.***

⚠ **Three consequences were drawn the same day**: the six root-noun
entries now carry the reservation **in the dictionary**; the freestanding
possessive and the final clitic are **reclassified out of the list of
gaps** (*"filing them with the real gaps dilutes the warning"*, L); and
the list of gaps is **swept at the same time as the body** at every
ruling, failing which a declaration outlives its own closing.

⚠⚠ **AND A FOURTH DEFECT WAS NAMED BY L — SINCE CLOSED, AND THIS
PARAGRAPH SAID IT WAS OPEN UNTIL 2026-09-01**: *"my three real blocks — 'few', 'since +
duration', the parenthetical of direct speech — are **unsettled USES of
existing words**. `luttil` exists, `sīthor` exists. The kit knows this
category, it identified it for `morgan` — **it has not generalised it
into an instrument.** The register counts neither absent words nor
unsettled uses, **and it is the second category that cost me most.**"*
⚠⚠⚠ **IT IS CLOSED. `emplois.md` IS THAT INSTRUMENT**, built after L
wrote and now the project's third register: it answers *how does one
SAY this?* where the dictionary answers *does this word exist?*. **All
three of his blocks are in it today** — “few”, “since + duration”, the
parenthetical of direct speech — each with a status, a rendering and
the round that met it. It carries thirty-six unsettled or divergent
uses, and since 2026-09-01 an order of work drawn from what the rounds
actually cost.
⚠ **The declaration outlived its own closing, which is exactly what the
paragraph two lines above forbids** — *“the list of gaps is swept at
the same time as the body at every ruling, failing which a declaration
outlives its own closing.”* It was not swept.
  *The sentence that names the discipline stood two lines from the
   sentence that broke it, and neither knew about the other. A rule
   about sweeping does not sweep.*

*The 42nd round had shown that the instruments cannot see an absent
WORD; the 43rd shows they cannot see an unsettled USE of a word that is
present either. **A third register was missing beside `cases.md` and
`lexique.md`.***
  ⚠⚠⚠ **IT HAS EXISTED SINCE 2026-08-23** — `emplois.md`, whose table
  stands at the head of the kit. *Four rounds called for it; the 53rd
  made it unavoidable by no longer stumbling on a single word.* It is
  born with its control, `emploi`.


⚠ **This is a real gain, and it must be named for what it is**: the
declaration does not protect the language from invention, it protects the
PROJECT from silent invention. *The benefit is not the abandonment — it
is traceability, and the convergence traceability produces.*

⚠⚠ **BEFORE YOU ABANDON A FORM, CHECK THE CLASS KEY.** On 2026-08-19
this list carried **three false entries out of five** — slots the kit
fills elsewhere, sometimes with a dated ruling and a worked example.
Translator E of the 40th round: *"a translator who reads the list gives
up; a translator who reads the key writes the form. **Both are reading
the same document.** The safety instrument has become a source of
error."*
  ⚠ **The cause is named**: I had written this list from the
  **translators' reports**, not from the kit. They said *"I did not
  find"*; I transcribed *"it does not exist"*.
    *Confusing "unfindable" with "absent" reclassifies a defect of
    NAVIGATION as a gap of LANGUAGE — and engraves it in the very
    instrument used to give up.*

### ⚠⚠ SIX SLOTS THAT WERE EMPTY THIS MORNING, AND ARE NO LONGER

**Four double enquiries returned on 2026-08-19**, all on stripped corpora
— the complete *Heliand*, the *Muspilli*, the Gothic Bible, and
**Behaghel, *Die Syntax des Heliand*, 1897**, which two enquirers opened
in full text. **Five of these six "gaps" were not gaps in the language:
they were my errors or my silences.**


| what was missing | what should have been written | where |
|---|---|---|
| **the future** | **the present indicative carries it**, and nothing else. †`skulan`, †`willian`, †`werthan`+pres. part. are **not** futures | § THE FOUR CELLS THE 38th ROUND LEFT EMPTY, ① |
| **"this one"** | ⚠ the slot was not on the proximal side: **`the` is used ALONE, with no noun**, and it is the ordinary pronoun of resumption | § ANAPHORA |
| **"the one who"** | ⚠⚠ my prohibition was **FALSE**: three attested turns, including `sō hwē sō` with a complete paradigm | § THE FREE RELATIVE OF PERSON |
| **"the one that", fem.** | RECONSTRUCTED — `thiu the` / `thia the` / `theru the`, **unattested**, the only real gap of the batch | ibid. |
| **the absolute use** | **licit and free** — it was the ENTRY that lied, not the language | § THE FOUR CELLS THE 38th ROUND LEFT EMPTY, ③ |
| **the mood after `wānian`** | **SUBJUNCTIVE**, 9/9 and 11/11 | § THE FOUR CELLS THE 38th ROUND LEFT EMPTY, ④ |

⚠ **ONE ALONE REMAINS OPEN, AND IT IS DECLARED**: **case attraction** in
a bare free relative under a conflict of case — the two enquiries
diverge, 92 % against 93 %, **and one does not average them**. The kit
prescribes the turn they agree on (the correlative `thes … that`), which
makes the question moot.

> *Five gaps out of six were of my own making. The only real one — the
> free relative in the feminine singular — is an accident of corpus, not
> a fact of language.*

⚠ **If you stumble on anything else, that is a find: report it.** The kit
keeps a journal of the translation rounds; what you could not write is
worth more there than what you did write.

## ⚠⚠ THE TABLES OF USE — information in the shape in which it is used


**Introduced on 2026-08-19, on a sentence from translator F of the 40th
round:**

> *"The table 'how one relativises the object of each class' is **the
> only place in the kit where information is presented in the shape in
> which it is used**. There ought to be ten like it."*

⚠ **A table of use is not a summary.** It files by **decision to be
taken**, it gives a **written form** and not a rule, and it says **"not
recorded"** where the kit is silent — because a table's silence reads as
an answer.

### ① MAKING A COMPOUND TENSE

| you want to say | you write |
|---|---|
| **he has done / he had done** (transitive) | `hebbian` + past participle, **participle INVARIABLE** — *hē habda mik bifangan* |
| **he has come / he had come** (movement, state) | `wesan` + participle — *hē was kuman* |
| **he had kept watch, he had slept, he had lived there** (an intransitive ACTIVITY — durative, no object, no change of state) | **the plain preterite, and nothing else** — *the wardo the alla naht wahtōda*; anteriority may be underlined by `air` "earlier". ⚠⚠ **There is no fourth slot, and that is a finding, not a gap** — double enquiry 2026-09-06, 75 % and 80 %: Gothic, with a Greek pluperfect in front of it, writes the plain preterite plus *juþan* "already" (John 6:17, 9:22), and `hebbian` + participle reaches activity intransitives only in the ninth and tenth centuries. The 83rd round's two translators wrote the preterite and were right. Not `hebbian` (10–20 %), not `wesan` (3–5 %), not `wesan` + present participle, which is an imperfect and never anterior. |
| **it is done, it is written** (resulting state) | `wesan` + participle of a **transitive** — ⚠ not to be confused with the previous line |
| **it is being done, it was judged for him** (process under way) | `werthan` + participle — *wirthid gahaldan*, *warth imu gadōmd* |
| ⚠⚠ **it HAD BEEN done** — passive in the perfect or pluperfect | ***`wesan`* + participle + `wordan`** — *was gageban **wordan*** "had been given". ⚠ `wordan` is the participle of `werthan` **without the preverb**; `gawordan` is reserved for the independent verb | ⚠⚠ **SLOT ADDED 2026-08-21: this table had four lines and not one carried `wordan`.** Translator U: *"a translator who reads the table for 'had been born' writes †`was gaboran` — and the table does not tell him a slot exists that he has just missed"*. ⚠ **The difference is one of ASPECT, not of tense, and it can be tested** — requisition R46 |
| **he will do** | **the present**. The language has no future — see the declared gaps |
| **where does the participle go?** | the **non-finite before** the auxiliary in OV order; when the finite verb rises to second position, it **leaves the non-finite behind it** |

### ② PLACING A PRONOUN

| your question | the answer |
|---|---|
| **where does an unstressed pronoun go?** | **in second position**, immediately after the complementiser — **before even a nominal subject**: *that **imu** the kuning that land gaf*, not †*that the kuning that land imu gaf*. **It is the most immediately archaic feature of the kit.** |
| **two pronouns, in what order?** | **nominative, then accusative, then dative** — *hē it imu gaf* |
| **and two full noun phrases?** | ⚠ **THE REVERSE**: dative before accusative — *the kuning gaf themu manne **thia** bōk* ⚠⚠ **`bōk` is FEMININE** — this example carried the NEUTER article until 2026-08-20, and **I copied the mistake from an earlier passage while writing this table**. Translator I of the 42nd round caught it: *"seven pronouns in my translation depended on this slot"*. The pronoun/noun inversion is an old Germanic fact, kept into modern Dutch |
| **what is forbidden** | any unstressed pronoun **separated from second position by a full noun phrase or prepositional phrase** |


⚠ **The accusative-before-dative order is a DECLARED CONVENTION**, not a
proven fact: between two objects the order is not fixed in the old texts.
It is laid down for consistency with what Germanic went on to do.

⚠⚠ **AND ONE COLLISION THE KIT DOES NOT ARBITRATE — reported by both
translators of the 40th round, who diverged on it.** The clitic block
lays down an **absolute prohibition** — *"no unstressed pronoun at the
end of a clause, ever"* — and its **own canonical example** breaks it:
***thō gaf hē it imu***, where `imu` is indeed final, second position
having pushed the verb in front. The conflict is real as soon as a
verb-second main clause carries two pronouns.
  · one of the two translators followed **the example**;
  · the other followed **the prohibition**, noting that norm N1
    (verb-second) was downgraded to a **tendency** on 2026-08-16, while
    the prohibition calls itself "absolute".
⚠⚠ **AND MY FIRST DRAFT OF THIS TABLE SENT YOU NOWHERE: THE COLLISION IS
  ARBITRATED.** Corrected 2026-08-20, both translators of the 41st round
  having reported it. **THE ARBITRATION, AND IT IS CLEAR** — § THE SEVEN
  COLLISIONS OF THE 31st ROUND, no. 3:
  > *"**The clitic group stays welded and keeps its order: it simply
  > follows the verb instead of preceding it.** `thō gaf hē it imu` —
  > nominative, accusative, dative, unchanged."*
  **So: when the verb rises to second position, the clitic group follows
  it, entire, in the same order.** That is the form to write.
  ⚠ **What remains open is ONLY the residue**: the prohibition *"no
  unstressed pronoun at the end of a clause"* has not been lifted, and
  this arbitrated form breaks it. **Write the arbitrated form**; the
  prohibition is to be reworded by double enquiry.
    *The only three divergences of the 41st round — over ten sentences of
    which seven came out identical — all come from this slot. My table
    said "unsettled" of a case settled five thousand lines below:
    **it manufactured the divergence it was there to remove.***


### ③ SAYING A TIME

| you want to say | you write |
|---|---|
| ⚠⚠ **a SEASON, a MONTH** — "in winter", "in summer" | **the ADVERBIAL GENITIVE**: `wintras`, `sumaras` — like `nahtas` "by night". ⚠ **Do NOT write `an themu wintarē`**: that form is *reconstructed by extension, unattested*, and the kit prefers the genitive. ⚠⚠⚠ *Line added 2026-08-25: BOTH translators of the 56th round missed this prescription — it was complete, motivated, dated, and filed inside the § on pronouns. One of them got it only by chance of reading.* |
| **that evening, that night** (a determined moment in the narrative) | **accusative singular, NO PREPOSITION — and it keeps its determiner** — *thena āband*, *thia naht*. ⚠ “bare” is not used here: this document writes *bare noun* for one with no determiner, and these have one. |
| **by night, by day, night and day** (indefinite or habitual) | ⚠⚠ **the ADVERBIAL GENITIVE, no preposition and no determiner** — ***nahtas***, ***dagas***, ***dagas endi nahtas*** *(ruling of 2026-08-21; †`an theru naht` is withdrawn, the whole construction was a calque of the source language)* |
| **every autumn** (iterative) | **accusative PLURAL** — *alla herbistā* ⚠ *(macron restored 2026-08-21 — it had been placed on `wintrā` that same morning and had not travelled this far; reported by translator M of the 44th round, who notes that this is the table the kit designates as the one that GIVES the form)* |
| **every thirty years** | ***alla thrītig jārō*** — ⚠ the genitive of the tens is **internal** to the numeral group, the iterative bears on the **whole group**: the two rules do not quarrel |
| **the next day** | *thena andaron dag* ~ *thena aftaron dag* |
| **tomorrow, yesterday, today** | **`hiudu`** today · **`gestron`** yesterday · **`morgan`** (m.) morning, tomorrow. ⚠⚠ *This slot said "NOT RECORDED": false, corrected 2026-08-23.* ⚠ **WHAT IS OPEN is the CONSTRUCTION** — bare adverb or accusative of time? The two translators of the 48th round both wrote the bare adverb, without warrant |

### ④ RENDERING A POSSESSIVE

| the possessor is… | you write |
|---|---|
| **masculine or neuter singular, AND the possessor = SUBJECT of the clause** | `sīn`, **agreeing with the THING POSSESSED** (full table at the § on the pronominal genitive) |
| ⚠⚠⚠ **masculine or neuter singular, possessor OTHER than the subject** | **`is`** — **INVARIABLE**, it never agrees. *"His wife sent" when "he" is not the subject: `is quenu gasanda`.* ⚠⚠ **`sīn` here would point to somebody else**, and the sentence would stay impeccable: this is the fault that leaves no trace. Both translators of the 55th round grazed it, one escaping only by chance of reading |
| **feminine singular** | **`ira`** |
| **plural** | **`iro`** |
| **"his", "mine" with no noun** | **SLOT CLOSED BY PERIPHRASIS** *(reclassified 2026-08-21; the table still carried †"NOT RECORDED" which the list of gaps had withdrawn the same day — reported by translator P of the 45th round)*. There is no substantival possessive: **one repeats the noun** — *than is hros* "than his horse". A loss of concision, not of sense |

⚠ **Two traps, and both bit in the 40th round:**
· reflexive `sīn` **points to the subject of its OWN clause** — in "older
  than his", writing `sīn` would say something else;
· the choice is made on the **sex of the possessor, but the rule is
  written on GENDER** — and `wīf` "woman" is **neuter**, which
  mechanically yields `sīnan brōther` for a woman. ⚠ **The kit does not
  say whether agreement *ad sensum* is licit**, although it settles the
  agreement of an apposed participle and that of coordinated subjects.
  **Open slot.**


### ⑤ COMPARING

| you want to say | you write |
|---|---|
| **more X**, ATTRIBUTIVE — "a greater king" | comparative **`-iro`**, declined **weak** |
| ⚠⚠ **more X**, PREDICATIVE — "fear is harder" | the comparative stays **BARE**, uninflected, whatever the gender — *forhtu ist **hardiro***. **There is NO strong series of the comparative** (ruling of 2026-08-14). ⚠ *This line was missing until 2026-08-21: translator R of the 46th round wrote †`hardira` from this table, and found the ruling only by accident.* See table ⑩ |
| **the most X** | superlative **`-ist`**, declined weak |
| **than** (second term) | **`than`** + the case the term would have in the understood clause — hence the **NOMINATIVE** if it would be subject there: *bet than **ain bodo*** "better than a messenger" |
| **as much as, as long as** | correlative **`sō … sō`** — *sō filu sō*, *sō lango sō* |
| **so high that** | **`sō hauh that`** |
| **half as much** | **`half sō filu`** — ⚠ this is **not** a calque of the source language, it is the common Germanic turn (Du *half zoveel*) |
| **towards, with regard to** | **`wither` + accusative** — *ain niowiht wither that endilausa* |

⚠⚠ **AND THERE ARE TWO SERIES, NOT ONE — added 2026-08-20.** My first
draft set out only the ADJECTIVE's, and a translator of the 41st round
writes: *"for 'weeps **less**', it would have made me write
†`luttiliro`"*. **The comparative of the ADVERB is a distinct series** —
see § *THE DEGREE OF THE ADVERB*:

| you are comparing… | you write |
|---|---|
| **an adjective** | `-iro` / `-ist`, **declined weak** |
| **an adverb or a verb** | the series in **`-or` / `-ost`**, ⚠ **indeclinable** |
| **more, less, better, longer** | the suppletives **`mair`**, **`min`**, **`bet`**, **`leng`** |

*A table that files by decision — "comparing" — and sets out only one of
the two devices is not merely incomplete: it makes you write wrongly,
because it inspires confidence. **A table's silence reads as an answer**,
and that is written at the head of this section.*

⚠ **LEXICALISED comparatives in `-aro` NEVER syncopate**:
*aftar → **aftaro***, *oba → **obaro***.

### ⑥ DECLINING THE WORD I HAVE JUST FOUND IN THE DICTIONARY

⚠⚠ **THIS TABLE IS THE BRIDGE BETWEEN TWO NOMENCLATURES, AND IT WAS
WRITTEN NOWHERE.** Translator N of the 44th round:
> *"The register files by **class name** — 'ī-stem', 'rac'; the
> dictionary marks by **code** — `[ī]`, `[rac]`. The bridge between the
> two is written nowhere, and it is not trivial: `[i]` and `[ī]` are two
> different classes one macron apart. **The translator ALWAYS starts from
> the code and NEVER from the class name: the table is generated by the
> wrong key.**"*

**You read the entry, you find the code, you come in here.**

| what the entry carries | class in the register | the distinguishing trait | lemmas | slots |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`(m.) with no bracket`** | *a-masc* | the type-word `dag`. **It is the default class**: a masculine entry without a bracket is an `-a` stem | 262 | 9 |
| **`(n.) with no bracket`** | *a-neuter* | like the masculine, **except that the plural is not marked** | 170 | 8 |
| **`(f.) with no bracket`** | *ō-fem* | the type-word `geba`. **It is the default class of the feminine**; ⚠ the entry cites in `-a`, the nominative is in `-u` | 203 | 8 |
| **`[n]`** | *n-weak* | stem in `-n`, called “weak”: **obliques in `-on`**, a barely differentiated singular | 103 | 6 |
| **`[ja]`** | *ja-stem (masc. and neuter)* | ⚠ **it FOLLOWS the migrated finals** — `-as` / `-ē`, like the a-stem, and **without `-i-`**: `rīkē`, not †`rīkiē` | 96 | 7 |
| **`[i] · masculine`** | *i-masc* | ⚠⚠ **THE MARK IS NOT ENOUGH, THE GENDER MUST BE READ.** The masculine **has converged towards the a-stem**: `gastas`, `gastē` | 81 | 8 of which **⚠ 2 open** |
| **`[i] · feminine`** | *i-fem* | ⚠ the feminine **has NOT converged** and keeps its own ending — **`-ī` in the genitive as in the dative**: `maht`, `stad`, `kraft`. *Two paradigms under one mark* | 81 | 4 of which **⚠ 1 open** |
| **`[ī]`** | *ī-stem* | deadjectival abstracts. **INVARIABLE SINGULAR**: `thiu menigi`, `thero menigi`, `theru menigi` | 9 | 3 |
| **`[jō]`** | *jō-fem* | feminine in `-jō` | 13 | 3 of which **⚠ 1 open** |
| **`[u]`** | *u-stem* | stem in `-u`. ⚠ the **neuter** (`fehu`) does not mark its plural | 10 | 6 |
| **`[wa]`** | *wa-stem* | stem in `*-waz`. ⚠ **not to be confused with `[u]`**: `ehu` “horse” is `*ehwaz` | 9 | 6 |
| **`[r]`** | *r-kinship* | kinship nouns. **Invariable singular** — `brōther` in every case of the singular | 7 | 4 |
| **`[rac]`** | *rac — root nouns* | ⚠⚠ **THE MARK IS NOT ENOUGH HERE EITHER: THE GENDER MUST BE READ** (bound added 2026-08-21). **FEMININES** (`naht`, `burg`): **dat. sg. with a ZERO ending**, transfer NEVER. **MASCULINES** (`man`, `fōt`): ⚠ **EARLY transfer, before 550** — `mannes`, `manne`, `fōtes`. *The trait common to both genders is the **UNMARKED plural**, not the zero dative.* ⚠ This line gave the zero dative as the class trait: **the table warned of the gender trap for `[i]` and committed it for `[rac]`.** Reported by U at the 47th round | 16 | 11 of which **⚠ 2 open** |
| **`[nd]`** | *nd-stem* | participial (`fīand`, `wīgant`). A **mixed** paradigm: genitive remade, dative archaic | 3 | 5 of which **⚠ 1 open** |
| **`[ō]`** | *ō-fem* | ⚠ a **redundant** mark — the feminine without a bracket is already an `-ō` stem. One lemma alone carries it | 2 | 8 |

✓ **Every class of the register is reachable from an entry.** *2 of them are declared SUB-CASES, with no mark of their own:* **`io-masc`** ← `[n]` — the register: “`grāfio` carries `[n]`” · **`u-neuter`** ← `[u]` — the neuter does not mark its plural

⚠ **This table is GENERATED.** The numbers — lemmas, slots, open slots —
are **recomputed at every generation** from `lexique.csv` and `cases.md`;
only the *distinguishing feature* is editorial. *The 44th round showed
what a hand-written class table costs: the `ī-stem` one carried the
apocope of `gast`, which belongs to another class, and both translators
read it that way.*

⚠⚠ **AND THE TRAP TO KNOW BEFORE ANY OTHER**: `[i]` **is not enough**.
You must read the gender beside it. The masculine converged on the
a-stem, the feminine did not — *two paradigms under one code*.
