### ⑦ NEGATING

⚠⚠ **CALLED FOR BY TRANSLATOR M OF THE 44th ROUND, AND HIS ARGUMENT IS
UNANSWERABLE**:
> *"The 'negate' line is **the only one of the twenty in the table of
> QUESTIONS that sends you to prose instead of to a table**. It says
> 'negative concord is licit and frequent'. **That is not a form, it is a
> permission.** And this is the matter where the source language
> interferes most: 'no longer', 'nobody', 'none at all' are three
> distinct turns in it, and a translator in a hurry will render them by
> three distinct calques with nothing to stop him."*

| you want to say | you write | warrant |
|---|---|---|
| **not** | ***`ni`* + verb**, and nothing else | ⚠⚠ **the postverbal reinforcer is FORBIDDEN** — bipartite negation (*ne… niht*, *ne… pas*) is an innovation of the 11th–13th c. |
| **no longer** | ***`ni`* V *`mair`*** | *Ni sprak hē mair* — a constant turn of the corpus. ⚠ `mair` is the comparative: the turn says "not any more", and the source language separates what Frenkisk does not |
| **nobody** | ***`ni`* V *`neoman`*** ~ ***`nihainan man`*** | `neoman` = `ni`+`eo`+`man`; ⚠ **one writes `eo`, not †`io`** |
| **none / no one …** | ***`nihain`* … *`ni`* V** — **the preverbal *`ni`* REMAINS** | negative concord is **licit and frequent**, attested in Gothic, OE, Norse, OHG and OS — *ni sah ik nihain* |
| **nothing** | ***`niowiht`*** — ⚠ gen. sg. ***`niowihtas`*** | settled 2026-08-18 by double enquiry |
| **never** | ***`nio`* … *`ni`* V** | `nio` "never", plus the concord |
| **no** (attributive) | ***`nihain`***, **declined like a strong adjective** | and `nainīg`, rated 70 % |
| **none of** (partitive) | ***`nihain`* + genitive plural** — *nihain mannō* | the Germanic partitive, not a calque |
| **nothing … but** | ***`ni` … `nibu`*** ~ ***`ak`*** | the negative exception |
| **no** (as an answer) | ***`nain`*** | |

⚠⚠ **THE LICENCE THE SOURCE LANGUAGE DOES NOT HAVE, AND WHICH YOU MUST
USE** — **`ni` + verb may OPEN the sentence, with an empty prefield**
(norm N5, negative licence V1): ***Ni sprak hē mair***, ***Ni mahta it
andres wesan***. *It is a constant turn of the corpus, and it is what
gives negation its bite.* ⚠ But with a heavy prefield — a nominal subject
carrying a relative — norm N8 takes over: **the kit does not say which
prevails when the two meet**, and translator N of the 44th round
stumbled exactly there.

⚠ **THE CONTRACTIONS — free of charge, and very high-yield for
authenticity**: ***`nist`*** (ni+ist — ⚠ the list also carried `nis`,
**abrogated by the ruling of 2026-08-26**), ***`nas`*** (ni+was),
***`nili`*** (ni+wili), ***`nait`*** (ni+`wait`).

⚠⚠ **TWO ABSOLUTE PROHIBITIONS**: **no negation without `ni`** (stage
III) · **no postverbal reinforcer without an argumental role** (stage
II). *These are the two most visible markers of a late date.*

| **neither … nor** | ***`ni`* V *`noh`* X *`noh`* Y** | the entry for `noh`: *"**1.** (conj.) nor — `noh … noh` 'neither… nor'"*, and the kit's **calibration text**, sentence 1: *nihain grāfio **noh** nihain liud* |

⚠⚠ **AND IT MUST BE SAID HOW THIS LINE CAME TO BE, BECAUSE IT IS THE
MOST INSTRUCTIVE FAULT IN THE FILE.** This table, written on 2026-08-21,
carried a **DECLARED GAP** here: *"the negative correlation of two terms
is recorded nowhere, and no witness is cited for it in the kit"*, with a
periphrastic workaround given as **conjectural**.
> **It was false.** The dictionary carries `noh` with **the exact
> gloss**, and the project's calibration text uses it in its **first
> sentence**.

⚠ **The table written to supply forms had invented a gap** — on the same
day, in the same table, and for the very matter translator M had flagged
as the most exposed. **Translator Q of the 45th round first gave up,
began building the periphrasis, then found `noh` by accident** while
rereading the Idiomatic section for something else entirely:
> *"Without a chance of reading, I would have written the periphrasis,
> declared it cleanly, and **my report would have been more flattering
> than my translation**."*

⚠⚠ **THREE CAUSES, AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS INATTENTION**:
· **the index had no entry for "nor"** — the most ordinary word of
  negation in the source language. Whoever looks for it by the normal
  path finds nothing, goes to the table, **and reads there that there is
  nothing**;
· **`deklar`, the 23rd control, did not go red**: its scope is bounded to
  slots of **inflection**, and this one is **lexical**;
· *"**Two broken instruments of navigation validate each other**"* — and
  it did not even take a translator to manufacture the gap.

---

### ⑧ USING OR NOT USING A DETERMINER

⚠⚠ **CALLED FOR BY TRANSLATOR P OF THE 45th ROUND, AND HIS ARGUMENT
RESTS ON A MEASURED COST**:
> *"On this text I took **six** decisions about articles — and **not
> one** was given to me as a written form: they all come out of a
> classification I had to make myself. The three-step procedure has
> seventeen sub-clauses, one of them **withdrawn the same day**, plus a
> calibration admitted to be unverified. **Nothing to copy, everything to
> interpret.**"*

⚠ **And this is the matter the kit itself names as interference site
no. 1 for the source language** — the proof being †*an theru naht*, a
mistake of article, recorded for weeks as a trap avoided.

| you want to say | you write | warrant |
|---|---|---|
| **a species, a generic** — "ravens fly" | **BARE noun** | *wolƀos sint grimme* |
| **a mass noun, an abstract** — gold, water, peace | **BARE noun** | STEP 1.3 |
| **a nominal predicate** — "he is king" | **BARE noun** | *hē ist kuning* |
| **a proper noun** | **BARE noun** | ⚠⚠ a determiner before a proper noun is **an immediately visible marker of a late date** |
| **a usual prepositional phrase** — at home, at the assembly | **BARE noun** | *te hūs*, *an thing* ⚠ *(`thing` does carry the sense "assembly", an old synonym of `mahal` — it was hidden in the etymological note and promoted to the gloss on 2026-08-21)* — ⚠ **never** †*bi nahti*, which is a Germanism. ⚠⚠ **AND "BY NIGHT" WAS REMOVED FROM THIS LINE ON 2026-08-21: see table ③.** It is not a prepositional phrase, it is an **adverbial genitive** — ***nahtas***. *This table used to write †`an naht`, that is, half of the calque †`an theru naht` it had been created to remove, on the very day the other table declared that "the WHOLE construction was wrong — not just the determiner". Reported separately by both translators of the 47th round.* |
| **a vocative** | **BARE noun** | STEP 1.6 |
| **a body part, a relative** whose possessor is obvious | **BARE noun** | STEP 1.8 |
| **the legal enumeration** | **BARE noun** | *of man swīn stilid* — anarthrous throughout, like the Latin *Pactus* |
| **with a possessive** | ⚠⚠ **NEVER a determiner** | *ira welo*, not †*the ira welo* |
| **a referent already named** (anaphora) | ***`the`*** | **this is the central use, the one that justifies the word** |
| **what one points at** (deixis) | ***`the`*** or ***`these`*** | |
| **the antecedent of a restrictive relative** | ***`the`*** | *the man the that swerd nam* — the most stable context |
| **an ordinal, a superlative** | ⚠⚠ ***`the`* is PERMITTED, NEVER REQUIRED** | ⚠⚠ **BOUND OF 2026-08-21, AND IT HOLDS FOR ALL SIX `the` LINES OF THIS TABLE.** Step 2 states a condition that is **necessary, never sufficient**: it lists the contexts where the determiner is **permitted**. ⚠ **BARE `aldisto` is the unmarked form, and the older one; the REGISTER decides.** — *This table used to print "you write → `the`", that is, an obligation. Three pairs of translators diverged here, two of them on `jungisto`.* |
| **a weak individualising adjective**, referent established | ***`the`*** | STEP 2.6 |
| **a determined moment of the narrative** — "that evening" | **accusative, no preposition, determiner kept** | *thena āband* |

⚠⚠ **WHAT WAS REMOVED FROM THIS TABLE, AND IT MUST BE SAID**: the line
"a **unique** — god, sun, moon, sky, earth, sea, day, night" was
**abrogated on 2026-08-21** by double enquiry. It was **empirically
false**: the *Heliand* writes `thiu sunna`, `thiu erða`, `an themu
mânon`, `an thene sêo`. **The determiner is not blocked by the LEXEME;
what is bare is the ADVERBIAL use and the GENERIC use.** *A rule that
files by type of noun where the corpus shows a property of construction
will go on being broken indefinitely.*

⚠ **DURATION WITH THE WORD FOR “ALL” — SETTLED 2026-09-06 by double
enquiry, both enquirers on the same three phrases**: `alla naht`, `allan
dag`, `allan wintar` (80 %/85 %, 80 %/80 %, 60 %/75 %). **Accusative, `all`
inflected STRONG, NO determiner, `all` before the noun.** The decisive
witness is Wulfila at Matthew 20:6, who renders ὅλην τὴν ἡμέραν — the
Greek article present — as `allana dag` and DROPS it, though he mirrors
the Greek article with `sa` everywhere else; the Wachtendonck psalter
writes `allan dag` for a Latin ablative *tota die*, and Tatian the same.
The two translators of the 45th round had `allan thena dag` against
`allan dag`: **the determiner is a symptom of the article's rise in the
ninth century, not an inheritance**. `allan thena dag` remains licit
only when `thena` is deictic — "all *that* day", a day already named —
and then it is not a duration phrase but an anaphora that happens to
last. ⚠ The dative plural, `nahtum endi dagum`, is not a rival: it is
the ITERATIVE "by night and by day" (Luke 2:37), a different meaning.

---


### ⑨ CROSSING TWO GOVERNMENTS

⚠⚠ **CALLED FOR BY TRANSLATOR Q OF THE 45th ROUND, AND IT IS THE MOST
UNCOMFORTABLE TABLE IN THE KIT: it sets out slots I do not know how to
fill.**
> *"The kit files magnificently by **single decision**: which case this
> verb takes, which preposition governs what, what the negative partitive
> is. **It never says what happens when two of those rules bear on the
> same phrase.** Three collisions in ten sentences, and not one is
> flagged anywhere. They are **invisible to the instruments by
> construction**: they are neither absent words nor unsettled uses —
> **every word is perfectly settled SEPARATELY.**"*

| the two rules that meet | what one writes | status |
|---|---|---|
| **NO INANIMATE CAUSER** + a plain TRANSITIVE with an abstract subject — “the law weighs the theft”, “the law holds the horse” | ⚠ **NOT ARBITRATED.** The ruling forbids an abstract in the AGENT position and prescribes the correlative conditional instead — but it is written for the CAUSATIVE, and these are ordinary transitives. Is the ban on the construction or on the role? | ⚠⚠ **UNSETTLED**, added 2026-09-01 by translator A of the 82nd round, who crossed it knowingly and reported it above everything else. *A rule written for one construction does not announce where that construction ends.* |
| **preposition with dative** + **negative partitive with genitive** — "of none of his men" | ***fon nihainemu sīnero mannō***: the quantifier takes the case of the preposition, **the partitive complement stays in the genitive** | ⚠ **UNATTESTED in the kit** — `nihainemu` does not appear in it once. **But the two translators of the 45th round wrote this form separately**, each declaring it a guess. *A convergence is not a warrant; it is a precedent, and it is worth more than a silence* |
| **verb with genitive of the thing** + **correlation `noh … noh`** — "he asked for neither gold nor silver" | ***noh goldas noh silubaras***: **each of the two coordinated terms carries the genitive** | ⚠ **UNATTESTED.** Both translators put the genitive on both terms, by two different routes |
| **verb with double government** + **complement clause** — "he swore to his wife that…" | the complement clause **occupies the accusative's place**, the dative of the person remains | ⚠ **UNSETTLED.** Norm N6 gives the PLACE of the complement clause, **never its case status**. The authorisation is borrowed from `biddan` (class V5), to which `swerian` does not belong |
| **imperative V1** + **clitic group** — "give him water" | ***Gif imu watar***: the clitic group **follows** the raised verb | ✓ **arbitrated** — table ② |
| **sententious register** (late verb) + **contraction `nist`** | ⚠⚠ **MEASURED 2026-08-30, AND THE SPLIT IS EXACT**: of the eight translators who met it, **four wrote `nist` and four wrote `ni ist`**. 4/4, twice over — the two pairs of one round split, and the two pairs of its replication split the other way. *A slot where eight readers of the same page divide down the middle is not an ambiguity of the reader: what they read is at fault.* ⚠ (This sentence was rewritten to carry no English noun ending in the letter `finales` reads as a dative singular. Standing among Frenkisk witnesses, such a word is taken for a form of the language — and naming the offending word in the explanation reproduced the fault, which is how this note came to be written twice.) ⚠ **the two contradict each other**: *…an ira welon nist* is grotesque, and `nist` in second position abandons the register | ⚠ **UNSETTLED**, reported by P |
| **negative V1 licence** (N5) + **heavy prefield** (N8) — a nominal subject carrying a relative | ⚠ the two norms are compatible and **neither has priority** | ⚠ **UNSETTLED**, reported by both translators of the 44th and the 45th. ⚠⚠ **AND NOW MEASURED, 2026-08-30**: eight translators met it, and **all eight opened the light-prefield sentence with `ni` + verb (N5) while giving the prefield to a heavy relative-carrying subject (N8)**. 8/8. That is not a ruling — nobody enquired — but it is the first observation this slot has ever had, and it points one way. Copies in `tours/`. |

⚠⚠ **WHY THIS TABLE EXISTS THOUGH IT SETTLES ALMOST NOTHING.** Because
**the cost has moved**. Q puts it better than I can:
> *"The kit has reached the stage where its isolated rules are sound and
> the cost has moved into their **intersections**. That is a good problem
> to have, and it is the one it does not measure."*

**A silent invention in an intersection leaves no trace** — no forge, no
block, no open slot, no divergence if both translators reason alike.
**This table is the only place in the kit where it becomes visible.**

⚠ **And the matching instrument is missing**, Q says so and I have no
remedy: *"no round of translation will report them as long as translators
are asked what they **failed to find** rather than what they
**assembled**."* **The question put to translators must change before the
instrument can be built.** ⚠ *(the word "brief" had to be avoided here:
`migro` reads it as the prior state of `brēf` — the project's metalanguage
collides with its language, and it is the fourth tool to stumble on it.)*

---

### ⑩ AGREEING AN ADJECTIVE — two entries, six slots

⚠⚠ **CALLED FOR BY TRANSLATOR R OF THE 46th ROUND, AND IT WAS BORN OF A
FALSE FORM THAT A TABLE OF THE KIT MADE HIM WRITE.** He had put
†*forhtu ist **hardira*** — from table ⑤, applied to the letter.
> *"†`hardira` is morphologically impeccable, table ⑤ gives it, the
> section filed by matter confirms it, and **nothing in my translation
> would have carried a trace**. I found the ruling only **by accident**,
> grepping for something else."*

**All six slots existed. Two of them were written only in § *Word formation*, where nobody goes looking for agreement** — and the generated
adjective block, which is the natural place, **certified "no open slot,
out of 17"**.

| | **ATTRIBUTIVE** — before the noun | **PREDICATIVE** — after `wesan`, `werthan` |
|---|---|---|
| **positive** | **WEAK if individualising**, strong otherwise — ⚠⚠ **NOT** "weak after a determiner", which is a rule of modern German, *logically impossible at 550* since the determiner is not obligatory there. The weak is **an apposed `-n-` stem**: *gōdo man* = "the man, the good one" | **STRONG** — *thia wāga wārun sō **hauha*** |
| **comparative** | **WEAK** — `-iro` | ⚠⚠ **BARE, uninflected, whatever the gender — AND WHATEVER THE NUMBER**: *forhtu ist **hardiro***, *stainā sint **hwītiro*** "the stones are whiter". ⚠ The predicative plural was brought here on 2026-08-28, **by parallel with the superlative**, which had received it on 2026-08-21 (*thesa berga sint allero hauhist*). *Two neighbouring series, only one extended to the plural: the other was not wrong, it was silent — and both translators of the 75th round had to extend it themselves.* **There is NO strong series of the comparative**: †*langiran*, †*langires*, †*langiremu* do not exist (ruling of 2026-08-14). Internal warrants: *thrim stapon **braidiro***, *grimmiro than…* |
| **superlative** | **WEAK** — `-ist` | ⚠ **REDUCED STRONG SERIES, and its mark is ZERO** — ⚠⚠ **AND THE PREDICATIVE PLURAL IS LICIT TOO, added 2026-08-21**: *thesa berga sint **allero hauhist***. *The two grids of 2026-08-15 had noted that the previous day's ruling had closed the gap only in the singular; this table had taken over Holthausen §350 b's bound **without its amendment of that same day**, and so made people GIVE UP WRONGLY in the plural.* — the reduced strong series admits only the nominative
singular of the three genders and the **accusative singular neuter** admit it (double enquiry at rank (a), 2026-08-15; Holthausen §350 b). A bare stem with no article, often with a partitive genitive plural — *these berg is allero bergō hauhist* |

⚠⚠ **WHY THIS TABLE HAS TWO ENTRIES AND NOT ONE.** The kit files degree
on one side (table ⑤) and agreement on the other (§ L'adjectif). **The
two rules bear on the same word and contradict each other in one slot out
of six**: agreement imposes the STRONG on the predicative, degree imposes
the WEAK on the comparative — *and so the predicative comparative has, by
their crossing, no licit form at all.* **It is that impossibility which
forced the ruling of 2026-08-14**, and it is what makes the two-entry
table necessary.


### ⑪ COORDINATING

⚠⚠ **CALLED FOR BY TRANSLATOR S OF THE 46th ROUND**: *"The kit has **no**
line at all on `endi`. Not the ten norms, not the table of questions, not
the tables of use. It is the most ordinary device in narrative prose, and
it is **entirely unsettled**."*

⚠ **CHECKED BEFORE WRITING, AND S OVERSTATES IT: the kit has FOUR,
scattered over four sections.** *Writing "there is nothing" would have
repeated, word for word, the "neither … nor" fault of the 45th round.*
**What is true is that nothing was gathered, and that what S needed — the
place of the verb in the second member — is indeed missing.**

| the question | the answer | status |
|---|---|---|
| ✓ **two `endi` in one sentence** | ⚠⚠⚠ **ABROGATED 2026-08-25 BY DOUBLE ENQUIRY — write them without fear.** The wording below is kept for the record and **prescribes nothing any more**: ~~`endi … endi` is a recorded CORRELATIVE~~ — "both… and", contradictory simultaneity — so a sentence with two `endi` is structurally ambiguous. ⚠ *Reported in the 56th round: the translator noticed only after writing, from the memory of a line in the § on connectives. This table, made to gather what was scattered, had gathered four out of five.* |
| **two subjects coordinated by `endi`** | ⚠ **PLURAL, whatever the order** — *thār Landerīk endi Ansoberht **sittiand***. **No agreement by proximity** | ✓ recorded |
| ⚠⚠ **two subjects under NEGATIVE correlation** — "neither X nor Y came" | ⚠ **UNSETTLED — the kit's two authorities contradict each other** | ⚠⚠ **BOUND ADDED 2026-08-21.** The line above read as universal; **the kit's calibration text puts the verb in the SINGULAR** under two negatively coordinated subjects — *nihain grāfio noh nihain liud it thī ni **binime***. A possible explanation: each `nihain` is a **distributive singular quantifier**, not a full subject. ⚠ **The two translators of the 47th round diverged here** — one followed the written form, the other the table. *It is the only ✓-ticked line in the kit that a check has found false.* ⚠ And the warrant is fragile: **golden rule no. 11 — the project's own corpus witnesses nothing** |
| **two coordinated pronouns** | ⚠⚠ **they CANNOT be clitic** — hence the stressed forms: *ik endi thū* "you and I" | ✓ recorded |
| **where does the second conjunct go if it is heavy?** | it **extraposes regularly**, like relatives and complement clauses (licences N9/N10, widened 2026-08-17) | ✓ recorded |
| **two nouns coordinated in the adverbial genitive** | each carries its ending — *dagas endi nahtas* | ✓ recorded |
| ⚠ **where does the VERB go in the second member?** | ✓ **SETTLED 2026-08-25** — see the ruling below | ✓ |
| ✓ **may the subject of the second member be elided?** | **YES, and it is the NORMAL regime**: only **4.7 %** of second members in the *Heliand* carry a full nominal subject (double enquiry of 2026-08-25). When the subject is the same, it is omitted and the verb comes **immediately after `endi`** — *The kuning haurda thia word thes bodon **endi swīgōda*** | ✓ **SETTLED** — ⚠ the slot still read OPEN here while the ruling closed it fifteen lines below; **both translators of the 61st round reported it** |
| ⚠ **may the determiner of the second noun be elided?** | — | ⚠ **OPEN** |
| ⚠ **does one `ni` bear on two coordinated clauses?** | ⚠ table ⑦ gives `noh … noh` for two **TERMS**, never for two **CLAUSES** | ⚠ **OPEN** |
| ⚠ **the clitic group of the second member** | — | ⚠ **OPEN** |

### ⚠⚠⚠ RULING ON COORDINATION — `endi` IS TRANSPARENT (2026-08-25)

**Double enquiry, two independent enquirers, corpora stripped and
counted.** They converge on the structure, and **both** refute the
analysis that made `endi` an occupant of the prefield.

> **`endi` IS NOT IN THE CLAUSE. It stands at position zero, and the
> clause that follows is built LIKE AN INDEPENDENT MAIN CLAUSE, with its
> prefield FREE.**

**What this settles, for the translator:**

· **`endi the fader nam that watar`** — the subject occupies the initial
  prefield. **This is licit.**
· **`endi thō nam the fader that watar`** — another constituent occupies
  it. **Licit as well.**
· **`endi nam the fader that watar`** — the prefield stays empty: this is
  **narrative V1**, which is an option of the language and **not** an
  effect of `endi`. **Licit too — and it is not `endi` that permits it.**

⚠⚠ **All three coexist in the same text, sometimes two lines apart.**
*Heliand* 3361 `endi Lazarus sat` against 3363 `endi lag the ōdago man` —
same sentence, two nominal subjects, two opposite orders.


**THE PROOF, AND IT PASSES NEITHER THROUGH LATIN NOR THROUGH THE METRE.**
The second enquirer measured, in the *Heliand*, the position of the
unstressed pronouns (Wackernagel's law):

| element in front | immediately followed by an unstressed pronoun |
|---|---|
| **`endi`** (coordinator) | **9.4 %** |
| `ac` (coordinator) | 8.0 % |
| `thō` (adverb, occupies the prefield) | **1.9 %** |

**Five times fewer clitics after `thō` than after `endi`.** A clitic can
lean immediately only if the place before the verb is still empty.
*Clitics carry no alliteration and translate no Latin: it is the one
argument in the file that no artefact pollutes.*

⚠ **AND ONE DISCREET FEATURE THIS AUTHORISES**: the clitic may lean
**right after `endi`** — ***endi im …***, ***endi ina …***. It is
characteristic, and the kit wrote it nowhere.

**Rated: 72 % and 78 %.** What stops it going higher, and both say so:
⚠⚠⚠ **there is NO continental Germanic narrative prose before 900.**
Rank (a) is an interlinear gloss whose order copies the Latin word for
word; rank (b) is alliterative verse; rank (c) is a verse-by-verse
translation. The first enquirer writes it without softening: *"any rating
above 80 % on this question would be, given the state of the witnesses, a
courtesy."*

⚠⚠ **AND THE METRICAL CONFOUND IS DEMONSTRATED, NOT SUPPOSED.**
*Heliand* 3361 carries alliteration in `l-` (*lîbes / lustun /
Lazarus*): the noun **had** to be in the first hemistich, hence before
the verb. 3363 carries vocalic alliteration (*allaro / armôdio /
ōdago*): the noun **had** to come after. **It is possible that the whole
V–S / S–V variation of the *Heliand* is metrical and not syntactic** — in
which case the percentages of the count measure nothing. Only the clitic
test survives that objection.

---

#### ⚠⚠ WHAT IS FIRMLY SETTLED — NEGATION (rated 85 % and 88 %)

**This is the one point where all four ranks converge, and where Old High
German DEPARTS FROM THE LATIN — which rules out a calque.**

> **① The preverbal `ni` IS REPEATED before each FINITE verb.**
> `ni quādun … endi ni skauwōda` — never a single `ni` over two finite
> verbs.
>
> **② But a single `ni` BEARS on two coordinated NON-FINITE verbs.**
> *Heliand* 5758 `ne farstelan … endi seggian` — two infinitives, one
> negation. ⚠ The second enquirer had left this case open for want of an
> apparatus; the first settled it by showing that these are
> **infinitives**, and confirmed it on *Heliand* 1375 and 3151.

> apparatus; the first settled it by showing that these are
> **infinitives**, and confirmed it on *Heliand* 1375 and 3151.
>
> **③ TWO NEGATED MEMBERS ARE NORMALLY COORDINATED BY `noh … noh`,**
> **not by `endi`** — but ⚠⚠ **`endi ni` DOES EXIST, and this clause was
> too strong in its first drafting (2026-08-25).** Both enquirers count
> **eight `endi ni`** in the *Heliand* (ll. 898, 1317, 1498, 1705, 1946,
> 1956, 3304, 4729), that is **0.9 %** of the `endi`, **and in each of
> them the negation is repeated**. So write `noh … noh` by default, and
> know that `endi ni … endi ni` is not a fault.
> ⚠⚠⚠ **THIS CORRECTION IS OWED TO THE TWO TRANSLATORS OF THE 61st
> ROUND**, who noticed separately that the example under point ① above —
> `ni quādun … endi ni skauwōda` — **contradicted point ③ ten lines
> below, in the same section, on the same date.** *A rule promulgated
> more loudly than its file gets contradicted by its own example.*
> The proper negative coordinator remains **`noh … noh`** (Old Du
> `nohne`, OHG `noh ni`, Goth. `nih`). *Tatian* 36,1: the Latin writes
> *et … non … nec*, the Old High German writes **`noh … noh … noh ni`**.
> **It does not copy: it restructures.**

⚠ And the *Heliand*'s minimal pair is mechanical: in the positive
*mid uuordun **endi** mid uuercun* (ll. 5, 2107); in the negative
***ni** mid uuordun **ni** mid uuercun* (ll. 1737, 1768). The same fixed
formula, four times, two regimes.

---

#### ⚠⚠⚠ WHAT IS ABROGATED — `endi … endi` IS NOT A CORRELATIVE

**The kit used to warn that a sentence with two `endi` was "structurally
ambiguous", `endi … endi` meaning "both… and". BOTH ENQUIRERS REFUTE
THIS.** Rated 80 % and 90 %.

· Repetition is **the normal regime**: 43 % of the coordinated sentences
  in the *Tatian* carry two `inti` or more; Gothic goes as far as **eight
  `jah` in a row** without anyone seeing a correlative in it.
· **The correlative exists and it is another word**: OS **`ia … ia`**
  (*Hel.* 354 `ia land ia liudi`, 2420, 4054, 4373) and the turn
  **OS *bēdiu … endi*** "both … and" — ⚠ a form of the WITNESS, not of
  Frenkisk, (*Hel.* 4639). OHG has `ioh … ioh`, and *Tatian* 170,6
  renders the Latin correlative *et … et* by **`ioh … ioh`, never by
  `inti … inti`**.


> **CONSEQUENCE: write two `endi` in one sentence without fear.**
> The warning cost the 60th round a whole structure — **both translators
> cut their last sentence in two** to avoid it, and the source text had
> three members where their Frenkisk has only two.
> *A false warning costs exactly what a false rule costs, and it leaves
> no trace.*

⚠ **THE REAL AMBIGUITY OF `endi` IS LEXICAL, AND THE KIT DID NOT SAY
SO**: `endi` is also the noun "**end**" (*Hel.* 267, 1324, 4729 `endi ni
kumid` — readable as "and does not come" or "an end does not come";
1780, 4455, in the dative: OS *endie*). That is where one must be
careful, not in the repetition.

---

#### ⚠⚠ *GAPPING* — THE TWO ENQUIRERS DIVERGE, AND ONE DOES NOT AVERAGE

**What they agree on** — and it is what governs practice: **continental
prose REFUSES the ellipsis of the verb and restores it.** The case is
direct: *Tatian* 89,2, the Latin practises gapping (*fregit et dedit
discipulis suis, **et discipuli ∅ populo***), and the Old High German
**inserts the verb**:
*inti gab sinen iungoron, **inti thie iungiron gabun themo folche***.
Three other repairs of the same kind (*Tatian* 7,4; 114,1; *Isidor* 9,1).
**Rank (a) repeats the auxiliary where the Latin did not require it**
(Ps 54,7 `ic fliugon sal … raston sal`; Ps 55,7).

**What they diverge on:**
· The **second** enquirer holds *Heliand* 2479–2482 to be "the
  centrepiece" — a true gapping, with no Latin model.
· The **first** sets it aside: *"in the digital text I consulted,
  `gangid` is **in brackets**, hence probably an editorial restitution.
  Reference to be checked against Behaghel–Taeger before any use."*


> **DECISION: do not practise gapping. REPEAT THE VERB.**
> ⚠ And know that this is not a prohibition of the language: it is the
> regime of prose, on a file whose only contrary piece is **disputed
> between the two enquirers**. Both translators of the 60th round had
> repeated the verb **assuming a prohibition the kit did not state**.
> They were right to do it, and wrong to believe it was written.

---

#### ⚠ THE SERIES OF THREE **OR MORE** — LOW RATINGS, AND BOTH REFUSE THE QUESTION

Neither of them keeps the hypotheses put to them. **There is no "A, B
**and** C" pattern: the final coordinator is not a Germanic rule, it is a
habit of the source language.** ⚠ English has exactly that habit, and its
style manuals go further, prescribing the comma before the final *and*.
The reflex is therefore stronger here, not weaker. ⚠⚠⚠ **AND THE RULE
HOLDS FOR ANY SERIES OF THREE ELEMENTS OR MORE, whatever its NUMBER and
whatever its NATURE** — clauses or phrases. A list of seven objects is
written in **complete asyndeton**: *thrī ohson, twē swīn, nigun skāp,
ainan speut, ainan skild, ainan ketil*. ⚠ Made precise on 2026-08-28: the
heading said "the series of THREE", and a translator read the prohibition
as bearing on triplets only — he wrote the source-language pattern over a
list of seven, while citing this very passage to justify a correct `endi`
between TWO phrases. *A rule whose heading puts a number on its case
reads as holding for that number alone.*

· **`endi` is not the device of narrative.** In the *Heliand*, `endi`
  makes 1.86 % of the words against 5.88 % in the translated *Tatian* —
  and **58 % of the *Heliand*'s `endi` coordinate not clauses but
  phrases.** The *Hildebrandslied*, the only untranslated continental
  narrative, has **four** `enti`, all between phrases, **none between
  clauses**.
· **The second enquirer names the device that is missing**: the particle
  **`thō` + verb + subject**. Measure: the *Tatian* opens **416
  sentences** with `thô` where the Latin offers `et` only 77 times —
  **~340 `thô` invented by the translator**, against not one invented
  `inti` in that position. *"When a continental speaker wants to say 'and
  then he said…', he does not say `endi`, he says `thō`."*
· **The first names the other half**: **asyndeton**, with Germanic
  *Variation* in apposition. *Hildebrandslied* ll. 4–5, 18, 63–67: the
  clauses follow one another bare.

> **INSTRUCTION: to carry a narrative forward, use `thō` + V2 and
> asyndeton. Keep `endi` for what it does most often — coordinating two
> PHRASES, or two verbs under a single subject** (*Hel.* 1291 `Sat im thô
> endi suuīgoda endi sah sie an lango`).


⚠ **Rated: 70 % and 85 % on the principle, 45 % and 65 % on the exact
form.** The first enquirer declares the gap: the *Markbeschreibungen* —
boundary descriptions in **untranslated** Old High German prose — would
have answered, and their URLs were dead. *"It is the most regrettable gap
of this enquiry."*

---

#### ⚠⚠ WHAT BOTH OF THEM HELD AGAINST THE COMMISSION

**I had them enquire into a rare case.** The second: in the *Heliand*,
only **4.7 %** of second members carry a full nominal subject. The first:
**58 %** of the `endi` do not even coordinate clauses. **The overwhelming
regime is `endi` + finite verb, subject omitted because identical** — and
that is precisely what the question did not cover.

*An enquiry well conducted on the wrong configuration returns a result
that is correct and useless.*

⚠ **AND A FLAW OF PROTOCOL, OF MY OWN MAKING**: the two enquirers worked
in the **same working directory**, and one of them reports that a file of
his was overwritten by the other. He regenerated and rechecked his
figures in a private directory. **The independence of the two enquiries
is therefore partly impaired** — I declare it rather than omit it. What
is reassuring: the first **destroys the second's centrepiece** (the
gapping of *Hel.* 2480), which contamination would not have produced.

⚠⚠ **THE PLACE OF THE VERB — WHAT WE HAVE, AND IT IS NOT A RULE.** The
**two translators of the 46th round wrote the same thing separately**:
> ***The kuning haurda thia word thes bodon **endi swīgōda**.***

verb **immediately after `endi`**, subject elided. **None of the ten
norms N1–N10 knows anything about coordination**: we do not know whether
`endi` opens a prefield (hence V2), whether it triggers *gapping*, or
whether it leaves the second member free. *It is a **convergent
precedent**, rated as such — and it is worth more than a silence, but it
is not worth a ruling.*


⚠ **And a NEIGHBOURING open slot, already declared elsewhere**:
agreement *ad sensum*. The kit settles the agreement of an apposed
participle **and** that of coordinated subjects, and **does not say**
whether agreement by sense is licit.

## Absolute rules

1. **Never a word outside the lexicon without marking it.** If a word is
   missing: derive it from Proto-Germanic through laws L1–L10, prefix it
   with an asterisk (*word) and report it at the end of your answer as
   an "unrecorded candidate". The authority of the lexicon lies
   elsewhere — you propose, you do not decide.
   **WHEN TO FORGE, WHEN NOT TO FORGE** — the complement of this rule,
   and it binds as tightly:
   · the **recorded word wins** if it covers the sense **exactly** — do
     not forge *skiphērro* when *hairro* already says "the master of the
     ship";
   · **forge** as soon as the recorded word **shifts** the sense, however
     slightly: *farloran wesan* "to be lost" does not say "missing at the
     muster" (at sea the two coincide, in law they do not) → **brestan**;
     *flōd* "flood" does not say the tide as a periodic regime →
     **gatīdi**;
   · **never forge** to avoid a periphrasis the Idiomatic section
     records: *hungar hebbian*, *sīn word geban*, *lātan faran* are the
     language, not makeshifts;
   · **test**: if the gloss of the recorded word cannot replace the word
     of the source text without changing what the sentence says, forge.
     Otherwise, use the recorded one.
   ⚠ **AND WHAT TO FORGE — a noun or a verb?** When it is a VERB that is
   missing, look FIRST for whether a **forged noun** combined with a
   **recorded verb** gives the sense: *laihan* "a loan" yields
   *te laihnē geban* "to lend" and *te laihnē niman* "to borrow" with two
   verbs that were already there. **The cheapest forge is the one that
   adds the fewest lemmas.** At the 7th independence test (2026-08-09),
   one forged that single noun, the other three verbs (*līhan, antlīhan,
   widargeban*): all well derived, but the divergence was born there, and
   the kit said WHEN to forge without saying WHAT.
   This clause was born of the second independence test (2026-08-09),
   where two translators diverged not by error but by **daring**: one
   forged where the other stretched, both defensibly. The rule now says
   which of the two is right, and when.

   ⚠⚠ **AND THERE ARE TWO. THE HIERARCHY BELOW IS THE ONE FOR FORM; FOR
   SYNTAX IT IS REVERSED.** Ruling of 2026-08-18, made on a double
   enquiry — **and both enquirers raised it SPONTANEOUSLY, at the head of
   their reports, without being asked.** They had been asked about seven
   governments; both began by saying that the ranking of witnesses did
   not suit the question.

   > **The rank of the LETTER is not the rank of GOVERNMENT.** The best
   > witness for form may be the worst for syntax, and here it is.

   **WHY.** Rank (a) is dominated by the **Wachtendonck Psalter**, which
   is an **interlinear** translation: its government follows the Latin
   word for word. *"A genitive there often proves the genitive of the
   Vulgate, not that of Frankish"* (enquirer R2). The *Heliand*, by
   contrast, is a long and free alliterative poem with an autonomous
   Germanic syntax. Otfrid and Notker are original works.

   **SYNTACTIC ORDER — for GOVERNMENT alone:**
   (1) the *Heliand* and the *Genesis* — original poetry, free syntax;
   (2) **Middle Dutch** — original legal and narrative prose, government
       noted lemma by lemma in the MNW. ⚠ **Too late for the letter,
       excellent for the construction**;
   (3) **Otfrid, Notker** — ⚠⚠ *original works, hence admissible HERE
       although rank (c) forbids them the letter.* This is the most
       counter-intuitive point of the ruling, and it is owned;
   (4) **Gothic**, a real witness to archaic valency, subject to the
       reservation about calquing the Greek;
   (5) **Wachtendonck** and the **Tatian** — close
       translations, **weak witnesses for government**;
   (6) **Merovingian Latin** — it gives the LATIN government of a
       Frankish word, never the Germanic one.

   ⚠⚠ **AND THE TEST THAT SAVES THE TRANSLATED WITNESSES — this is the
   real content of the ruling.** A close translation is not mute: it is
   mute **where it follows its model**. Where it DEPARTS from it, it
   bears witness. R2 gives the textbook case: in **Lk 23:2** the Greek
   κατηγορεῖν governs the **genitive** of the person, and Gothic answers
   with an **accusative**. A departure from the model cannot be a calque.

   > *In a translated witness, syntax that AGREES with the model proves
   > nothing; only syntax that DIVERGES is testimony. One does not
   > dismiss the psalter — one reads nothing in it but its departures.*


   ⚠⚠ **WHAT THE RULING COSTS, MEASURED THE SAME DAY — and the figure
   moves the problem elsewhere.** The dictionary carries **109** entries
   with a government. They break down thus:

   | warrant cited | number |
   |---|---|
   | rank (b), the *Heliand* — the best syntactic witness | **27** |
   | Gothic | **20** |
   | rank (a), psalter or Middle Dutch | **15** |
   | ⚠⚠ **no witness cited at all** | **66** |

   **The reversal touches only fifteen entries. Sixty-six rest their
   government on nothing whatever** — `biddan`, `keusan`, `geldan`,
   `fleuhan`, `gabeudan`, `dwingan`… **that is 61 %.**

   > *I spent the day wondering whether my witnesses were the right ones.
   > The measurement answers that in three cases out of five **there are
   > none**. A badly ordered hierarchy is a defect of method; a
   > government with no witness is a defect of fact, and it is three
   > times more common.*

   ⚠ **WHAT THIS RULING DOES NOT DO.** It does **not** touch the
   hierarchy of form, which stands entire and has been tested through
   thirty-six rounds. It does **not** restore Old High German for the
   letter: L7 holds. It opens a second order for one question only —
   government — and it declares that **the 66 entries with no warrant are
   an open slot, named and counted**, not a silent debt.

   **HIERARCHY OF THE STANDARDS** — for FORM, in this order, and not by
   choice:
   (a) **Old Low Franconian / Old Dutch** first: it is the direct
       descendant of Frenkisk, and the only line that is its own;
   (b) **Old Saxon** next, as a second witness, when Low Franconian is
       silent — which is often, its corpus being thin;
   (c) **Old High German** last, and **never for the form**: it underwent
       the second sound shift that L7 refuses. It gives the sense and the
       date, never the letter;


⚠⚠⚠ **THE RANK IS NOT THE SAME ACROSS DOMAINS** (2026-08-28, on outside
   criticism). A single global order makes one believe that a witness
   prevails in the same way on every question. **That is false, and the
   kit already knew it in three places that did not cite one another.**

   | domain | which prevails | why |
   |---|---|---|
   | **phonology** | **(a bis)**, then (a) | Merovingian Latin is **contemporary with the target**; Wachtendonck is three centuries later — ⚠ but see the reservation below |
   | **spelling** | **(a)** | a continuous Germanic text, written to be read in Germanic; Latin filters through habits of its own |
   | **morphology** | **(a)**, then (b) | Wachtendonck **actually declines**: the only witness in the area that gives paradigms in context |
   | **syntax** | **(b)**, then (c) | ⚠⚠⚠ **rank (a) is HERE THE WORST** — an **interlinear** gloss whose order copies the Latin word for word |
   | **lexicon** | rank (a bis) first, then (a) | the malberg glosses are **living vocabulary**, named by jurists describing real things |

   ⚠⚠ **PRACTICAL CONSEQUENCE, AND IT REVERSES A REFLEX**: on a question
   of WORD ORDER, **do not cite Wachtendonck as an authority**. Its
   government and its order are those of its Latin source.
   *The kit wrote this three times already without drawing the general
   consequence.*

⚠⚠ **CHRONOLOGICAL PROXIMITY IS NOT PHONOGRAPHIC FIDELITY.**
   The kit held that Latin scribes "have no motive to normalise a
   Germanism". That is too strong, and the enquirer on the diphthongs had
   said so as early as 2026-08-26 — without the remark being recorded
   anywhere.
   · **For `ai`, the premise holds**: Latin no longer had a living ⟨ai⟩
     digraph (⟨ae⟩ had been /ɛ/ since the 2nd c.). A Merovingian ⟨ai⟩
     **transcribes a foreign sound** — a clean witness: *Gais-*, *Rait-*.
   · **For `au`, it falls**: Latin had ⟨au⟩ and **was monophthongising
     it** in Gallo-Romance (*Appendix Probi*: "auris non oricla"). A
     Merovingian ⟨au⟩ may be a real /au/ **or a graphic hypercorrection
     for /o/**. **The witness is noised by Latin itself.**

   > **SEPARATE TWO QUALITIES THAT A SINGLE RANK CONFLATES: CHRONOLOGICAL
   > PROXIMITY and PHONOGRAPHIC FIDELITY.** Rank (a bis) has the first
   > without reservation; it has the second only where Latin has no sound
   > of its own to put in the place.
   ⚠ *This overturns no ruling already made — the one on the diphthongs
   rested on Ripuarian descent and on Braune's dating, not on the
   anthroponyms alone. But it changes what may be alleged next time.*

   (e) ⚠⚠ **OLD ENGLISH IS NOT A RANK — IT IS FORBIDDEN FOR THE LETTER,
       on the same footing as Old High German.** Added 2026-08-22, on a
       double audit of the lexicon.
       > **A**: *"The undocumented zone of your lexicon is filled with
       > **Anglo-Saxon poetry**. Five of the thirty lemmas audited —
       > `lagu`, `galdar`, `wōpian`, `gahwār`, `brordōn` — belong to the
       > **same register** and come from **the same source**. That is no
       > accident: **when rank (a) is silent, the hand goes looking where
       > the vocabulary is abundant and coloured**, that is, in the one
       > richly poetic old Germanic corpus."*
       > **B**: *"Your anti-Old-High-German discipline WORKS — twelve
       > lemmas out of thirty pass the L7 test by departing clearly from
       > OHG. The leaks come from elsewhere: from **Old English**, four
       > times out of six. **Your guard is on the wrong side of the
       > door.**"*
       ⚠ **Old English may serve as a comparative CONTROL** — it confirms
       that an etymon exists, it dates a sense. **It never supplies a
       form.** It is off the continent, off the line of descent, and its
       vocabulary is the most abundant: three reasons why the hand goes
       there, and three reasons why it must not.

       ⚠⚠⚠ **AND THIS WARNING BINDS AN ENGLISH-SPEAKING READER MORE
       TIGHTLY THAN IT BOUND A FRENCH ONE.** These lines were written for
       readers whose own language put no Old English within reach. Yours
       does. An Old English form will look familiar to you, and
       familiarity is precisely what this rule exists to distrust.

           *The leak came from the corpus nobody was guarding, because it
            was the one that felt easiest to read.*

       ⚠⚠ **EXPOSURE MEASURED 2026-08-22: 32 lemmas of the dictionary
       rest ONLY on Old English**, with no continental witness at all —
       `akus`, `gawādi`, `anud`, `alu`, `sālitha`, `unwiti`, `anst`,
       `abunst`, `gamund`, `skīn`, `hriuwan`, `galimpan`, `laus`,
       `gabāri`, `swehur`, `snora`, `swēso`, `ītwīti`, `antlūkan`,
       `ginōn`, `darōth`, `mornōn`, `eoman`… **They are NOT withdrawn —
       they are flagged.** *An audit is not a purge: it says where one is
       walking without a net.*


   (d) **a Latin loan** for Roman realities (measures, law, seafaring,
       writing).
   ⚠ **Rank (a) may be SILENT or TOO LATE** — one then goes down, and
   declares it. ⚠⚠ **But it is NOT silent merely because it carries a
   DIFFERENT formation: a different formation IS its answer.**
   ⚠ **AND WHEN THE TWO RANKS DIFFER ON GENDER**, not on form, rank (b)
   prevails: a change of gender is always late and traceable, whereas the
   form may be archaic on both sides. *wīti* "punishment" is NEUTER
   (OS *wīti*, a `-ja` stem like *rīki*, *beddi*) although MDu *wîte*
   became feminine. A translator in the 10th test REFUSED to forge the
   word for that reason alone — "hierarchy and letter contradict each
   other, and the kit gives no arbiter". It now gives one.
   ⚠⚠ **AND THIS CLAUSE CAN COLLIDE WITH THE PRECEDING ONE.** When rank
   (a) knows only a COMPOUND where rank (b) has a DERIVATIVE, the two
   clauses say opposite things: "a different formation is its answer"
   wants the compound, "rank (a) is silent on this formation, go down"
   wants the derivative. **Settlement: the function makes the derivative,
   the thing makes the compound.** A man defined by a FUNCTION takes
   **-io** even where rank (a) compounds — *stuurman* → **stiurio**,
   *veerman* → **ferio**; an object or a place takes the compound —
   *marcsteen* → **markstain**, *northfenstar*.
   At the 16th independence test (2026-08-11) the two translators cited
   THE SAME precedent (*stiurio*) to conclude, one *ferman*, the other
   *ferio*: the collision was real, and it was of my making.
   For "sow", rank (b) keeps the root noun *sū* and rank (a) knows only
   the `-ō` derivative (MDu *soghe*, Du *zeug*): that is not a silence,
   it is a choice, and it is **suga** that must be written. At the 6th
   independence test (2026-08-09), a translator declared rank (a) "silent,
   and late where it speaks" in order to go down to rank (b) — careful
   reasoning, and wrong, on the one forge of the text. Silent: Dutch
   knows only the compound *stuurman* and says nothing about the `-io`
   formation → one takes OS **stiurio**. Too late: *bevelen* lost its
   `-h-` well after 550 → one takes (a) for the sense and **bifelhan**
   (b) for the letter. Going down is not disobedience; going down in
   silence is.

   ⚠ This order is not decorative. On 2026-08-07, two independent
   translators armed with this kit forged the same word for "toll" from
   the **same etymon** and the **same law**, and arrived at two different
   forms — *tol* (Dutch standard) and *tolna* (Saxon standard) — because
   the list was read as an enumeration. It is a hierarchy. **tol** wins.
2. **Never a calque of the source language**: consult the turns of
   phrase first (§ Idiomatique).
   ⚠ **AND STAY CONSISTENT WITH YOURSELF.** When the same source word
   comes back in a text, render it by the SAME Frenkisk word, unless the
   sense changes. On a long text this is the first fault to appear: at
   the 12th independence test (2026-08-11, 331 words), a translator
   rendered "to build a bridge" as *timbrian*, then *gatimbrid*, then
   **makōda** — the same act three times, two verbs. No rule was broken;
   the text was incoherent. Reread yourself before handing in.
3. Every inflection follows the tables below.



## ⚠⚠ THE EIGHT GOLDEN RULES THIS KIT INVOKES

**Brought here on 2026-08-17. They were cited six times in the pages that
follow — "this is exactly the fault golden rule no. 12 names", "golden
rule no. 21 forbids deducing them" — and stated NOWHERE in this
document.** A translator armed with the kit alone was reading a pointer
to a text he did not have. Wordings reproduced verbatim.

| | |
|---|---|
| **no. 8** | *a ruling without its migration is worth nothing* — ⚠ **added 2026-08-19**: the kit invoked it without stating it, and a translator of the 39th round noted that the section created expressly to remove orphan pointers left two of its own |
| **no. 11** | *the project's own corpus witnesses nothing* — it applies the rules, it does not prove them. ⚠ Added 2026-08-19, same reason |
| **no. 12** | *a ruling note that narrates the correction DOES NOT STAND IN PLACE of the correction* |
| **no. 16** | *a blind test run from the inside measures its own documentation* — ⚠⚠ **BROKEN ON 2026-08-19, AND THE RULE WAS PRINTED ON THIS PAGE.** The 39th round was launched on the VERY text of the 38th, after a day of rulings had poured into the kit the translations of five or six of its ten sentences. Both translators saw it **before writing a line**, said so separately, and deflated their own marks from 3 and 3.5 to **2.5**. One writes: *"the kit optimised itself on its own test; the 39th round measured the kit's memory, not the vitality of the language."* **Corollary brought here: one NEVER retests on a text whose answers have been poured in, however little — a verification round demands a text the kit has never seen.** |
| **no. 18** | *an INSTRUMENT received from an enquirer submits to the same protocol as his conclusions* |
| **no. 19** | *a SILENT witness is not a DISSENTING witness* — a language that has undergone an **unconditional** change can say nothing about the earlier state. OHG made `þ > d` everywhere: it does not vote on `þ` |
| **no. 20** | *to promulgate is also to rewrite the EXAMPLES* — a rule promulgated without its examples is a rule without a language |
| **no. 21** | *two true rules chained together can produce a false form; it is ATTESTATION that stops a deduction, never the deduction itself* |

> ⚠⚠ **NO. 21 IS THE ONE THAT SERVES MOST, AND THE LEAST INTUITIVE.** It
> does not say to doubt the rules: it says that **the hierarchy of
> witnesses is there to STOP a deduction**, not to feed it. The case that
> produced it: L6 (`þ` preserved) plus no. 19 (OHG is silent) gave
> †`munth` by faultless reasoning. **Rank (a) writes `munt`.** Two sound
> rules, one false form.


## Sound laws L1–L10 (Proto-Germanic → Frenkisk)

⚠⚠ **THE TEN LAWS FIRST, THEIR RULINGS AFTER.** This section was reflowed
on 2026-08-16, after three attempts to separate the norm from its
narrative had failed. The cause was here: *"… rank (a) is mute) · L6 þ
kept, written th · "* — **the ruling ended and the law began on the same
line**. No tool can settle that; it had to be rewritten.
**Nothing was deleted: everything was moved.**

| | law | example |
|---|---|---|
| **L1** | loss of final `-z` | \*dagaz → **dag** |
| **L2** | ⚠⚠ **ABROGATED 2026-08-23.** It said `ai` → `ē`. The diphthong is **INTACT** at 550; the monophthongisation is later. *Its bound — the maintenance before `i`/`j` — falls with it: it was the exception to nothing.* | \*stainaz → **stain** · \*gaitiz → **gait** |
| **L3** | ⚠⚠ **ABROGATED 2026-08-23.** It said `au` → `ō`. Same reason, same date. | \*baumaz → **baum** |

⚠⚠⚠ **THE RULING ON THE DIPHTHONGS — 2026-08-22, DOUBLE ENQUIRY.
IT IS THE PROJECT'S LARGEST RULING, AND IT CHANGES NO FORM.**

**THE VERDICT, CONVERGENT AND IDENTICALLY ARGUED BY BOTH ENQUIRERS:**
> **`stain` (70 %) > `stein` (20 %) > †`stēn` (10 %).**
> **†`stēn` is the WORST of the three for a target at 550.**
> ⚠⚠ *Three forms IN COMPETITION. The pass of 2026-08-23 had corrected the
> third member to `stain`: the verdict then set `stain` against `stain`.
> **A form cited in order to be set aside keeps its own letter.***

**The argument, which both build on the same piece.** The onomastic
element `*auda-` "wealth" carries `au` **before `d`** — the context most
**favourable** to monophthongisation. And the Merovingian sources of the
6th–7th centuries constantly write ***Audovera***, ***Audoinus***,
***Audegisilus***, ***Baudegisilus***; the forms ***Odo*** / ***Otto*** do
not take hold before **~700-750**.
> *If the monophthong does not break through before 700 in its optimal
> context, it does not exist at 550 anywhere else.*
· **Toponymy confirms it**: `-haim` dominates in the 6th–8th c., `-heim`
  takes over in the 8th–9th, `-hem` in the 9th. **At 550: `*haim`.**
· **Manual dating** puts the two monophthongisations in the **7th–8th
  centuries** — *later than the target*.
· **Merovingian legal Latin** writes the diphthongs faithfully: `faida` <
  `*faihiþa`, `haistaldus`, `Gaiso`, `Radagaisus`.

⚠⚠ **WHAT THAT MEANS: NEITHER MY FORMS NOR THE ACCUSER'S.** At 550 one
should write ***stain · ain · haim · dail · hailag · baiþe · gaist · baum
· auga · haubid · rauba · galaubjan · kaupjan · daupjan***.

---


⚠ **TWO TRAPS THAT ONLY THE GLOSS COULD SETTLE**, and that no enquirer
could resolve without opening the lexicon:
- **`bautan`** "to thrust, to push" < `*bautaną` is **concerned** — but
  `bōta` / `bōtian` "fine, to amend" < `*bōtō` **are not**. *A treatment by
  family would have broken two words out of three.*

⚠ **AND ONE LEMMA WHOSE SPELLING CAME FROM ELSEWHERE.** The lemma was
written †`lēas` — that is the **Anglo-Frisian** form (OE *lēas*), where the
continent gives *lōs* (OS, OHG). *Its entry cited Old English as support*,
which rule (e) of 2026-08-22 forbids.
⚠⚠ **AT 550 THE QUESTION VANISHES**: both go back to `\*lausaz`, and
before any monophthongisation they **coincide in `laus`**. *The borrowing
of a spelling was real; it bore on a state later than the language.*
> ⚠ *Rewritten 2026-08-23: the pass had corrected **both members** and the
> sentence set a word against itself. A citation of a later state must keep
> its own letter.*

## ⚠⚠ WHAT THE PASS CARRIED AWAY WITH IT

**A correction of form is never made alone.** Correcting †`stēn` to `stain`
without touching the rest would have left the kit in the exact state it
forbids: **`ai` and `au` figured by name in the list of forbidden
sequences.** The pass therefore had, in the same movement, to:

| act | what falls |
|---|---|
| **L2 abrogated** | `ai` → `ē` describes a state **later** than 550 |
| **L3 abrogated** | `au` → `ō`, the same reason |
| **the bound of L2 abrogated** | it was the exception to L2; without L2 it excepts nothing |
| **`au`, `ai` leave the forbidden list** | they are henceforth the **normal** diphthongs |
| **`ei` re-enters it** | it had left only by the bound |

> ⚠⚠ **TWO OF THE TEN LAWS FELL ON THE SAME DAY**, and they were not
> false: they were **mis-dated**. *A sound law that is right for 800 and
> wrong for 550 is not corrected, it is re-dated.*

**What the pass did NOT touch, and why.** The corpus does not contain only
Frenkisk: it cites Gothic, Old Saxon, Old High German, and reconstructed
etymons that legitimately carry `ē` and `ō`. Three safety catches protected
them — the exclusion of the evidence files, the mask on citations, and the
arbitration by the longest lemma, which stops `lō` from swallowing
`lōthar`.

⚠ **And the preterite of `haitan` stays `hēt`.** Its `ē` comes from the
contraction of the reduplication `*he-hait`, not from a monophthongisation.
*It is not concerned by this ruling — it is simply not examined, and that
is an open case, not an oversight.*

the FEW** — free, immediate, it removes the underlying ambiguity; ②
**Nedoma & Düwel 2023** for the runes; ③ the ***Chartae Latinae
Antiquiores*** XIII-XIV, ~40 Merovingian diplomas **in the original**,
625-717, no copyist; ④ the **RICG I**, engraved epitaphs from **Trier**,
5th–7th c., **in the middle of the target basin**; ⑤ the entry **`hazier`**
in the CNRTL, to complete the dating doublet; ⑥ **Gysseling**

| **L4** | `eu` → `iu` **before `i`, `j`, `u`**; `eu` everywhere else | \*beudan → **biudis** ~ **beudan** |
| **L5** | umlaut `a` → `e` before `i`/`j` — ⚠ **does not cross a full syllable** | \*gastiz → **gast**, pl. **gesti** |
| **L6** | `þ` kept, written **th** | \*þeudō → **theud** |
| **L7** | **NO** second shift: `p t k` intact | **tehan**, **skip** |
| **L8** | short final vowels kept | **sunu**, **gebu** |
| **L9** | `ē¹` → `ā` | **māno** |
| **L10** | final hardening of the labial spirant: \*ƀ → **f** in absolute final position, **b** before a vowel and after a nasal | **wīf** / **wības** |

full statement and the enquiries that established them follow

he is applying — that is where the exceptions live, and they are many.

It is kept in the workshop and is **not part of this delivery**. ⚠ Nothing in it prescribes a form: it is the argument, not the ruling, and the rulings it produced are all here.

## The rulings on the laws

⚠ What follows is the file on each law: what the kit wrote that was false,
who reported it, on what witness, and on what date. **The two translators of
the 30th round credited this apparatus as the kit's most effective device**
— "it transmits the rule and its edge at the same time". It is not
decorative: it is what stops the faults from being made again.

### L2 — the bound before `i`/`j` — ⚠⚠ **ABROGATED 2026-08-23**

⚠⚠ **WHAT THIS BOUND SAID, AND WHY IT NOW SAYS NOTHING.** Promulgated
2026-08-15 after a double enquiry at rank (a), it excepted from L2 the words
carrying an `i`/`j` in the following syllable: `gait`, `hraini`, `haithin`,
`waitha`, `laidian`, `aigan` kept their diphthong there when all the others
went to `ē`.

**It was the exception to a law. L2 is abrogated; it excepts nothing any
more.** These six words carry `ai` like the rest — no longer by privilege,
but by the rule.

> ⚠ **WE KEEP THE TRACE, BECAUSE IT WAS RIGHT SIX DAYS TOO EARLY.** On
> 2026-08-16 this enquiry is the first place where the project saw a
> diphthong survive at 550. It took it for an exception and confined it to
> six words. *The ruling on the diphthongs discovered nothing: it
> generalised what this bound had found and shut away.*
>
> ⚠⚠ **AND IT LEFT A DEBT.** By separating the adjective `aigan`
> `\*aiginaz` from the verb `aigan` `\*aiganą`, it had undone a homograph
> — its entry congratulated itself on this: *"it was not a homograph, it was
> two words my law was confusing".* The law gone, the two words become one
> again. **They were re-merged on 2026-08-23.** *A distinction that hangs on
> a law falls with it.*

⚠ The detail of its application — which lemmas received it, the two
analogical levellings that contradicted it (`dailian`, `hailian`), the three
lemmas the grids had flagged (`gamaini`, `haisi`, `aik`) — was withdrawn
from the kit on 2026-08-23: **it served to apply a rule that is no longer
applied.**
### L4 — the diphthong, and the file that did not exist

⚠⚠ **L4 WAS AUDITED AT RANK (a) ON 2026-08-16, AND THE FILE THAT
THREATENED IT DID NOT EXIST.** The kit carried a note saying that Braune
§47 Anm. 1 "would call L4 itself into question". **It was an error of
scope, and the two enquirers reported it separately, text in hand** (2nd
ed., Halle 1891): the alternation whose coherence Braune denies is that
**between ⟨eu⟩ and ⟨eo⟩ — two spellings of one and the same lowered
member** — and not that between ⟨eo⟩ and ⟨iu⟩. The sentence that PRECEDES,
in the same note, says the opposite of what was feared: "der wechsel
zwischen eo und iu ist in den namen aus urkunden des 8. Jh. **schon
regelmässig vorhanden**".
⚠ **AND ITS EVIDENTIAL BASE IS UNTRACEABLE.** Braune adduces only a page
reference — "Jacobi, Beitr. z. d. gramm. 117". Neither enquirer could
identify the work, and one checked Braune's list of abbreviations: **it is
not there**. The kit was quoting this Jacobi verbatim over two pages; **the
quotation could not be verified and it is withdrawn**. *A handbook note
whose reference is untraceable has no value as data — and a quotation one
cannot reopen has no place in a grammar.*
⚠⚠ **THE REAL DEMONSTRATION IS IN BONNET, AND IT GOES THROUGH GREEK** (*Le
latin de Grégoire de Tours*, Paris 1890, pp. 144-145). The variation ⟨eu⟩ ~
⟨eo⟩ is observed on words **where no Germanic diphthong exists**: Εὐρώπη →
*Eorops* in ALL the manuscripts, εὐλογία → *eologias*, Θεοδόσιος →
*Teudosio* (Rome, 423), *Neoterius* → *Neuterius* (Rome, 390). A graphic
variation that occurs identically on Greek and on Germanic **encodes
nothing about Germanic**: it encodes the Gallo-Roman confusion of ⟨u⟩ and
⟨o⟩ in unstressed position. Bonnet concludes that in the Germanic names
"**eo is the true spelling**". Franck 1909 §37 Anm. already said "**Mangel
der westfränkischen Orthographie**", but that is a one-line assertion; **it
is Bonnet who proves it, and it is Bonnet who must be cited**.
⚠ **INDEPENDENT CORROBORATION, ANOTHER BRANCH**: Gallée, *Altsächsische
Grammatik*, 2nd ed. 1910, §102 Anm. 1, on the onomastic ⟨eu⟩ — "einige
dieser namen mit eu gehören wol der **allgemein verbreiteten konvention der
lateinischen Schreibung** an".

⚠⚠ **THE KIT'S CHRONOLOGY WAS FALSE IN ITS SECOND HALF, AND THE TWO
ENQUIRERS CORRECT IT IN THE SAME DIRECTION.** The kit gave the lowering as
"areal, continental, later than the migrations and under way in the 6th
century". **Wrong by some two to three centuries.** The lowering is not
peculiar to the diphthong: it is the **a-Umlaut**, exactly the rule that
makes \*u → \*o in a simple syllable (Braune §32), applied to the second
mora — Braune §47 says it word for word, "indem das u (ebenso wie einfaches
u §32) zu o wurde". And the a-Umlaut is **common to all North-West Germanic
and absent from Gothic alone**, hence earlier than the migrations: the
golden horn of **Gallehus**, around 400, already carries *horna* < \*hurną.
**One cannot hold that \*u → \*o is pre-migratory and that \*eu → \*eo
is of the 6th century: it is the same rule.**
The **raising** before *i/j/u*, for its part, is settled well before 550 —
the distribution is identical in OHG, OS and Old Low Franconian, hence
inherited and not areal, and Old Norse maintains it in another guise
(*bjóða* : *býðr*, *kjósa* : *kýss*). ⚠ One enquirer gives it as
Proto-Germanic, the other as post-Proto-Germanic with only 60 %
confidence: **the disagreement is declared, and it changes nothing at
550**, where both branches are in place.
⚠⚠ **THE RAISING ENVIRONMENT INCLUDES *u*, AND THE KIT HAD FORGOTTEN IT.**
Braune §47b: "wenn in der folgenden silbe sich ein i (j) **oder u**
befindet"; Franck §37: "bei i- **u**-Lauten". Control cases already in the
dictionary: *hiudu*, *fiur*, *thiustri*. **No lemma moves** — the
correction completes the statement, it migrates nothing.
⚠⚠ **THERE IS NO "ELSEWHERE" ENVIRONMENT, AND IT WAS THAT WHICH FORCED THE
KIT TO INVENT A SPELLING WITH NO WITNESS.** {i, j, u} against {a, e, o}
exhausts the vowel system: a third cell can only designate the absence of a
following vowel — closed syllable, final position, first member of a
compound before a consonant — **and that residue files with the LOWERED
member** (Gallée §102: "sonst zu eo, io, ia und ie, nl. **wenn kein vokal
folgte** oder wenn in einer folgenden silbe a, e oder o stand").
⚠ **IN THE RESIDUE, IT IS THE PARADIGM THAT DECIDES, NOT PHONETICS.**
Braune §47 Anm. 2: in the nominal inflection "der vocal des nomin.
durchgeht" — *diota* gives dat. *diotu*, not †*diutu*. So **the vowel of
the nominative runs through the whole paradigm of the noun**: *theud* keeps
*eu* in all its cases, and one does not write †*thiudu* in the dative.
Conversely, a monosyllabic stem in -*i*- takes the raised variant by
analogy with its obliques (Gallée §102 Anm. 2: *liud* beside *liudi*).
⚠⚠ **WE REFUSE ⟨io⟩ — BUT FOR A REASON OF DATE, NOT OF RANK.** The kit said
"an Old High German form, rank (c)". **That is false: Low Franconian and
Old Saxon write ⟨io⟩ as well** (Heyne 1873 §12,1 *lioht*; Gallée §102).
What condemns ⟨io⟩ is its DATE: Franck §37 — the lowering gives *eo*,
"woraus später, **im allgemeinen in der 1. Hälfte des 9. Jhs.**, io wird".
The distribution is confirmed by the sources: *eo* dominates in Isidor
(around 790-800), *io* is regular in the Tatian (around 830) and after.
**Three centuries too late for 550.**
⚠⚠ **⟨eu⟩ IS A CONVENTION OF NOTATION, DECLARED AS SUCH — AND IT IS THE
WEAKEST POINT OF THE LAW.** One enquirer recommends ⟨eo⟩ and observes that
⟨eu⟩ **rests on nothing but Latin onomastics, that is, on the corpus the
kit itself declares non-diagnostic**; the other recommends ⟨eu⟩ before
*i/j/u* and ⟨eo⟩ elsewhere, while giving the cell as **undecidable at
52 %** — "choose one and declare it as a convention, not as a result".
**The kit keeps ⟨eu⟩ and no longer claims to prove it**: the migration ⟨eu⟩
→ ⟨eo⟩ would cost 234 occurrences in 25 files to change how the language is
written and not what it is, five days after migrating the same diphthong in
the other direction. *Decision of the principal, 2026-08-16.*
⚠ **BUT WE DO WRITE THE ALTERNATION**, for consistency: a language that
writes *neman ~ nimis* must write *beudan ~ biudis*. It is the same rule.
It is **allophonic in 550, not phonologised** — the final syllables are
intact, the distribution stays predictable.
⚠⚠ **WHAT WAS WITHDRAWN FROM THIS LAW, AND WHY.**
· **The blocking clause before \*w is DELETED: it rested on an error of
  etymology.** There is no \*eu in *treuwa* — it is \*trewwō, Germanic
  \*e before a strengthened *w*, Holtzmann's law (Franck §41,2 and §71;
  Goth. *triggws*, OE *trēowe*). The word **never entered the \*eu
  series**: there was therefore nothing in it to block. Worse for the old
  clause: this secondary \*eu receives **exactly the same conditioning** —
  the *Heliand* regularly distinguishes *treuwa* (noun, ō-stem) from
  *triuwi* (adj., ja-stem), *hreuwan* from *hriuwig*, *euua* from *iuua*.
  **The contrast *treuwa* ~ *triuwi* is not a blocking: it is L4 working
  normally.** The forms *treuwa*, *untreuwa*, *gatreuwi* are right and do
  not move; it is the rule that falls, not they.
· **The Dutch doublets are WITHDRAWN as evidence.** The kit invoked
  *dierbaar : duur*, *Diets : Duits*, *kieken : kuiken*, *stieren :
  sturen*. Both enquirers demolish them: *Diets : Duits* is a **remodelling
  after German** *diutsch/deutsch*, and the split *ie*/*uu* is a **coastal
  Ingvaeonic dialect trait** (Debrabandere), the two members continuing the
  SAME raised etymon — hence not a conditioned alternation. ⚠ **And the
  warning holds beyond L4**: the Salians are precisely in the coastal zone,
  which makes this trait a live variable for the project and not an
  argument. *The same figure as the Salian hypothesis that died on the
  verbal plural.*

· **The argument from onomastic bias is BOUNDED, not withdrawn.** It holds
  for ⟨Theud-⟩ (\*þeudō, ō-stem, composition vowel *-a-/-o-*: a lowering
  environment, ⟨eo/eu⟩ predicted). **It does NOT hold for ⟨Leud-⟩** if the
  etymon is the *i*-stem \*leudi-: the composition vowel there is \*-i-,
  hence a raising environment, and ⟨Leudegisilus⟩, ⟨Leudastes⟩,
  ⟨Leudovaldus⟩ remain without ⟨iu⟩. **Do not present this argument as
  covering the whole corpus.**
**What stands, and it is the essential part**: Gregory writes *Theudericus
~ Theodoricus*, *Leuva ~ Leovigildus* for **GOTHS** — and Gothic has *iu*
EVERYWHERE without alternation (Braune §47: "welcher im Goth. überall iu
geworden ist"), and the Visigothic kings *Theudis* († 548) and *Theudigisel*
(† 549) are exact contemporaries of the target. **Merovingian Latin
therefore writes ⟨eu⟩/⟨eo⟩ for a CERTAIN Germanic /iu/.** ⚠ A double-edged
argument, and it must be owned: it destroys Braune's note as evidence
AGAINST L4, but it also destroys any evidence FOR ⟨eu⟩ at 550. To which is
added the paronymic attraction of Θεόδωρος, which imposes ⟨theo-⟩
independently of any phonetics.
⚠⚠ **A TRAP OF HOMOGRAPHY, THE 3rd OF THE DAY**: of 49 lemmas in *io*, **24
DO NOT COME FROM \*eu** and must not move — the agent nouns in **-io**
(*erbio, ferio, grāfio, stiurio, skenkio, raginburgio, fadurio*, < \*-jō),
the numerals (*fior, fiortho, fiortig*, < \*fedwōr), *io/nio/niowiht* (<
\*aiwa), *knio* and *thio/thionōn* (wa-stems), *willio* (< \*-jō),
*diobol* (Latin). **A blind replacement would have destroyed the agent
suffix and the *ferio* clause.**
(*þeudō → theud, but beudan → biudit; keusan → kiusit; fleugan → fliugit)
— a conditioning carried here 2026-08-07 (requisition R32): it held for the
verb and was stated only at the verb. It applies everywhere, including in
the plural of the i-stems (theud → thiudi) ·

### L5 — the bound of the full syllable

⚠ **L5 does not cross a full syllable**: when a *u* separates the root
vowel from the \*-j-, the umlaut does not apply — \*faðurjô- →
**fadurio** "paternal uncle", not †faderio (ruling of 2026-08-09, the two
standards agreeing by their silence: rank (b) writes *fadoro*, rank (a) is
mute)

### L6 — when the standard contradicts the law

⚠ **L6 and the standard can contradict each other**, and it is Verner's
alternation that decides: \*hlaþaną gives **hladan** (and not †hlathan)
because West Germanic generalised the voiced alternant \*hlaðaną. Rule:
when the Low Franconian or Saxon cognate carries *d* where the etymon
carries þ, it is Verner — follow the cognate. Reported 2026-08-07 by an
outside translator: "it is the only point where my two instruments
contradict each other".

### ⚠⚠ A BOUND ON L6 — the cluster `*-nþ-` hardens to `-nd-`

**Entered 2026-08-17, and found while attempting a migration the hierarchy
refused.**

L6 says "`þ` kept, written **th**". Applied mechanically, it requires
†*munth* for "mouth" — `*munþaz`, Goth. *munþs*. **And that is false.**

> **`*-nþ-` is not a simple `þ`: it is a CLUSTER, and it hardens to
> `-nd-`.** Whence **`mund`** "mouth", and not †*munth*.

⚠ **Rank (a) decides, and it decides against the reasoning**: the
Wachtendonck Psalter writes **`munt`**. It is one of the twenty-four
witness words of the Low Franconian deposit, and it agrees to the letter.
· ⚠ **And OE `mūþ` proves nothing against it**: it has `þ` only because the
**Ingvaeonic nasal spirant law** dropped the nasal there (`*munþ` > `mūþ`)
— **and the kit rejects that law for Frankish**. A witness that has
undergone a law one does not apply does not testify.

> **WHAT NEARLY HAPPENED, AND IT IS THE REASON FOR WRITING THIS BOUND.** I
> built an apparently solid case against `mund` — L6 on one side, and
> golden rule no. 19 on the other, Old High German `mund` being a **mute
> witness** since it made `þ > d` unconditionally. **Both premisses were
> right and the conclusion was false**, because the bound on the cluster
> was missing.
>
> *A chain of reasoning linking two true rules can produce a false form: it
> is the attestation that stops one, never the deduction.*

⚠ **And it is the hierarchy that worked**: rank (a) gives the letter, and it
gave it against me.
