### ⚠⚠ THE LEGAL ANCHORING — an instruction about PROPORTION

The two enquirers of the abstract grid put it above any dictionary entry:

> **"The only Frankish abstract known at FIRST HAND is that of law"** — the
> Malberg glosses of the *Pactus*, the Latin of the laws: *mallum*,
> *fredus*, *bannus*, *reipus*, *leudis*, *mundeburdis*, *chrenecruda*.
>
> **"If your lexicon is rich in affects and poor in procedure, it is
> INVERTED with respect to everything the corpus allows us to know."**

**MEASUREMENT MADE: the lexicon is NOT inverted.** 70 lemmas with a legal
sense against 40 with a psychological one — **a ratio of 1.75 in favour of
law**. ⚠ *The two enquirers were warning against a danger already avoided;
they could not know it, the protocol forbidding them to open the files.*

> **RULE OF PROPORTION, now written down**: the centre of gravity of the
> abstract is **juridical and social**; the psychological and moral zone
> stays **visibly thinner**. Every salvo of additions in the moral abstract
> is measured against the legal, and not against a list of notions from a
> modern language.

⚠ **And the reason is epistemic, not aesthetic.** The four witness
languages are **languages of translation** — Gothic calques Greek, the
*Heliand* and the *Isidor* calque Latin. **Their wealth in moral abstracts
is an artefact of translation.** The Frankish of 550 has neither a Bible
nor a scriptorium: **it must be poorer than they are, by construction.**

**What the attested corpus has already given** — and it has been worked to
the bone: *warg*, *raip*, *sakibaro*, *thungin*, *faihitha*, *morgangeba*,
*heriban*, *taskaga*, *liudwardi*, *theubwardi*, *liud*, *waddi*, *bann*,
*mundburd*, *wergeld*, *skilling*, *sunnia*.
· ⚠ **The hopeless cases stay hopeless, and that is a datum**: *achasius*
has no reading, and no lemma is drawn from it. *"An attestation is an
anchor only if it is LEGIBLE."*


### L10 — the final hardening, and its diagnostic

**L10 FINAL HARDENING OF THE LABIAL SPIRANT**: \*ƀ → **f** in absolute
final position, but **b before a vowel** (hence in all the obliques) and
**b after a nasal** (the cluster -mb is a stop, not a spirant) —
**wīf**/wības, **theuf**/theubas, **graf**/grabas, **līf**/lības,
**urlauf**/urlaubas, **gaskrif**/gaskribas, **half** but f. **halba**,
**self** but weak **selbo**, **kalf** but gen. pl. **kalbo**; *lamb* stays
*lamb*.
⚠ **The plural of the neuters has a ZERO ENDING**: *kalf* → pl. **kalf**,
*lamb* → pl. **lamb**, *werk* → pl. **werk**, *kind* → pl. **kind**. The
plural in **-ir** (†*kalbir*, †*lembir*, †*eier*) is **High German, rank
(c)**: rank (a) gives *kint*, *uuerk* without an ending, rank (b) files
*lamb* and *Calbesloge* "without a suffix", and the spelling *-er* is
labelled *spätahd.* — after the year 1000. The first Dutch *-er* is of
**1230** (*achte eyere*). Abrogated 2026-08-15, double enquiry.
⚠⚠ **HOW DOES ONE KNOW WHETHER A FINAL -f IS CONCERNED?** L10 aims only at
the *f* from **\*ƀ**; the *f* from **\*p** (Grimm's law) stays *f*
EVERYWHERE, obliques included. The diagnostic is German — the only language
that keeps the distinction, and which is questioned here about the ETYMON,
not about the form (rank (c) is licit for that): **Weib/Weiber → b**, hence
*wīf/**wības*** · **Hof/Höfe → f**, hence *hof/**hofas*** · *Laub/Laubes* →
b, hence *lauf/laubes* · *Schiff/Schiffe* → f, hence *skip/skipes*. Dutch
is of NO use here: it writes *v* in both cases (*wijven*, *hoven*), the
intervocalic voicing of *f* masking the distinction. A translator in the
check of 2026-08-13 wrote †*hobes* citing precisely *hoven*: here the usual
standard is the wrong witness.
⚠ **AND IT HOLDS FOR THE VERB**, the law being phonetic and not nominal:
pret. **starf** (sterban), **grōf** (graban), **skraif** (skrīban), **waf**
(weban), **skauf** (skeuban), **bilaif** (bilīban); imper. **skiuf!**. The
obliques and the plurals keep the b
⚠⚠ **RULING OF 2026-08-14 ON THE IMPERATIVE OF CLASS II**, and ⚠ **its
note was RECTIFIED on 2026-08-15**: it said "the kit carried †*skiuf!*
against *siuth!*, two incompatible imperatives". **That was false, and the
two forced grids reported it separately** — *skeuban* → **skiuf!** carries
exactly the same raising *eu* → *iu* as *seuthan* → **siuth!**. The two
forms are of the SAME type, the one the ruling retains. **`skiuf!` was
never faulty**; the note daggered it without the conclusion requiring it,
and the imperative of class II was left eleven hours with no prescribed
form. *A note on a ruling that contradicts the ruling amounts to an
abrogation by inadvertence.* A double enquiry at rank (a), and **the two
enquirers DIVERGED** — it is their disagreement that produced the answer.
· The Low Franconian corpus gives **three imperative sg. forms in *iu*** —
  ***gebuit*** (Wachtendonck Ps 67,29, *manda*), ***ūtguit*** (68,25,
  *effunde*), ***fliugh*** (Willeram 149,1, *fuge*) — against **one only**
  in *ie*, *niet* (glosses, a weak witness). And the alternation is
  INTERNAL: *ūtguit* (imper. sg.) against *ūtgietit* (imper. pl.) against
  *ūtgotona* (part.). Quak 1997 states it: "*in de gebiedende wijs treedt
  in het enkelvoud dezelfde wisseling op als in het enkelvoud van de
  praesens indicatief: farfiht, gif, **gebuit***".
· ⚠⚠ **BUT THE RAISING IS ANALOGICAL, NOT PHONETIC**, and it is the second
  enquirer who establishes it by **Wilmanns's test**: in West Germanic the
  final \*-i drops after a HEAVY syllable and is kept after a SHORT one;
  and *nim-*, *gib-* are SHORT — a desinential \*-i would have given
  †*nimi*, †*gibi*. **The positive control exists**: the presents in *-j-*
  do keep their *-i* (*biti*, *sizzi*, *sweri*). So the ending was NEVER
  \*-i, and the raising comes from the paradigmatic block "singular of the
  present". Corroborated by the *Heliand*, which gives **M *gef, help,
  teoh*** against **C *giƀ, hilp, tiuh*** — **in the same line**: *a sound
  law does not vary from one manuscript to another, an analogy does.*
· ⚠ **A NON-NEGOTIABLE CONSTRAINT**: the mechanism being ONE — the block of
  the present singular — **the choice must be the same for class II and for
  III(b)/IV/V**. A system with *skiuf!* but †*nem!* has no historical
  model. The kit already having *nim!*, *gif!*, *quim!*, *siuth!*, it
  follows the **attested Low Franconian type**, the one the psalter gives
  across the three classes at once — **and *skiuf!* belongs to it**: it
  carries the raising, like *siuth!*, *nim!*, *gif!*, *quim!*.
⚠ **A RESERVATION RECORDED, FILE OPEN**: the phonetically REGULAR form
would be *beud!* (bare stem, the "elsewhere" grade), and the second
enquirer gives the attested type only ~50 % for 550 itself — the levelling
being still UNFINISHED in the 9th century in Old Saxon. To which is added
Braune §47 Anm. 1: **the Frankish names of the 6th–7th centuries write *eu*
and *eo* with no consistent alternation**, which would call **L4 itself**
into question as an innovation later than 550. **Not examined, not
applied**: this is not to be settled in passing while correcting an
imperative.

(*sturbum*, *grōbum*, *gaskoban*), and the MEDIAL *b* of a compound as
well (*theubwardi*, like *obnē*). Extended 2026-08-11: promulgated two days
earlier ON THE NOUNS ONLY, it had left six strong preterites untouched —
the very fault it was correcting, made again on another class. **A sound
law is not promulgated class by class.** Promulgated 2026-08-09: it had
been applied BY HAND once, on *kalb → kalf*, and filed among "purged
doublets" — one occurrence of a law had been corrected instead of the law
being promulgated. Eight lemmas carried its trace, including a live doublet
(*wīb* AND *wīf* both recorded as "woman"), on which two independent
translators stumbled the same day without being able to decide.
Sequences forbidden in a Frenkisk word: **ei, uo, z, pf, c, v** — their
presence signals an error.
⚠⚠ **`au` AND `ai` LEFT THIS LIST ON 2026-08-23.** It is the most visible
reversal in the project: they are henceforth the **normal** diphthongs of
Frenkisk — *stain*, *baum*, *auga*, *ainfald*, *haitan*. A long vowel
`ē`/`ō` stays licit when it is **inherited** (*brōther*, *fōt*, *swōti*) or
**Latin** (*skōla*, *nōna*) — never when it continues an `*ai` or an `*au`.
⚠ **And `ei` COMES BACK INTO IT.** It had left on 2026-08-15 by the bound
of L2, which kept the diphthong as `ei` before a following `i`/`j`. **That
bound was the exception to a law we have just abrogated**: without L2 it
excepts nothing. *gait*, *hraini*, *laidian*, *aigan*, *waitha* carry `ai`
like the rest since the atomic pass.
⚠⚠ **THIS LIST WAS CUT IN TWO FOR A DAY**, and the two forced grids saw
it: in withdrawing *ei* I had left a parenthetic clause in the middle of
the sentence, whence **two incompatible lists** — the first without *uo*,
the second without *au* or *ai*. A reader applying the second let through
†*baum* and †*hlaidian*, which L3 and L2 forbid. **A parenthesis inside a
list doubles it instead of correcting it.**
⚠⚠ **AND THIS FORBIDS NO BORROWING: LATIN ADAPTS.** The list above aims at
Frenkisk words, not at the Latin words one brings in. Rule of adaptation,
applied four times in the dictionary and never stated until now: **c → k**
(*capitulum* → **kapitul**, *coquina* → **kokina**, *kyriakon* →
**kirika**, *schola* → **skōla**, *crux* → **krūki** (k even before i, like
*kirika*: the Romance palatalisation is later than the borrowing, and
neither *c* nor *z* can write it), *kalendae* → **kalenda**); **v → w** or
**b**; **z → t**; long vowels are written, and the Latin endings bend to a
Frenkisk class (a feminine borrowing MUST carry its class mark, cf.
*kirika* [n] against *mulīn* [ō]). At the 22nd test of independence
(2026-08-13) a translator concluded from the list that "borrowing is
literally unwritable" and that "the list of forbidden letters blocks a
whole class of Christian Latin borrowings". He was wrong about the
conclusion and right about the cause: **the rule was applied everywhere and
written nowhere.** And **no word ends in -b** outside a nasal cluster
(L10).



## FINAL SYLLABLES AT 550 — and why the kit had reduced them

⚠⚠ **A WORKSITE OPENED ON 2026-08-16 BY THE DATING AUDIT, AND IT IS THE
MOST SYSTEMATIC IN THE PROJECT.** A double enquiry at rank (a), then a
double enquiry on dating: all four agree on a single principle.

> **AT 550, VOWEL QUANTITY IS STILL DISTINCTIVE IN A FINAL SYLLABLE.**
> The great weakening of the finals (*Nebensilbenabschwächung*) is a
> process of the 8th–9th century. The kit kept the SHORT vowels — `sunu`,
> `gebu`, `dagum` — and had already reduced the LONG ones. **That is a 9th-
> century inflection set on a 6th-century vocalism.**

### What must be written, slot by slot

| slot | what the kit wrote | form at 550 | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| gen. sg. masc. of the `-a` stems | †`dages` | **`dagas`** | reconstructed, **high confidence** |
| dat. sg. of the `-a` stems | †`dage` | **`dagē`** | reconstructed, high |
| **instrumental sg.** | *absent* | **`dagu`**, **`gebu`** | reconstructed, high |
| gen. pl. | †`dago` | **`dagō`** | reconstructed, high |
| nom./acc. pl. of the `-ō` stems | †`geba` | **`gebā`** | reconstructed, high |
| dat. pl. of the `-ō` stems | †`gebum` | **`gebōm`** | reconstructed, high |
| gen. pl. of the `-ō` stems | †`gebono` | **`gebōnō`** ~ `gebō` | conjectured |
| present subjunctive sg. | †`gebe` | **`gebē`** | reconstructed, high |
| past participle and unstressed prefixes | †`gageban` | **`gageban`** | reconstructed, high |
| infinitive | `geban` | **`geban`** — unchanged | reconstructed, high |
| dat. pl. of the `-a` stems | `dagum` | **`dagum`** — unchanged | the vowel there is SHORT |

⚠⚠ **THE GENITIVE ARGUMENT IS THE SOUNDEST IN THE WHOLE FILE, AND THE TWO
ENQUIRERS GIVE IT SEPARATELY.** In Old English, *i*-mutation of *æ* gives
*e*. And the genitive is ***dæges*, not †*deges***; OHG is ***tages*, not
†*teges***. **So at the period when the mutation operated — 6th–7th
century — the ending contained NO front vowel.** It was `*-as`. The `-es`
of every attested language is a weakening **later than the umlaut**.
· Corroboration on Frankish soil, with a reservation: **Bergakker**
(Betuwe, ~425–475) carries *haþuþuwas*, read as a genitive in `-as` by
Quak, Looijenga and Mees. ⚠ **The reading is disputed in every one of its
words** — a support, not a proof.

⚠⚠ **AND BOTH OF THEM HELD ME BACK AT A POINT WHERE I WAS ABOUT TO
OVER-ARCHAISE.** **Do NOT restore `*-ai` in the subjunctive.** The
monophthongisation of `*ai` in a final syllable is **earlier than West
Germanic** — the Tune stone (~400) already writes, in runes, ON
*woduridē*. To write †*gebai* would be an archaism of two centuries.
*Repairing a dating that is too low by pushing it too high is not a
correction, it is the symmetrical error.*

### ⚠⚠ THE BOUND OF THE RULE ON FINALS — it holds ONLY for `-a` and `-ō`

⚠ *The table above is exact and **reads as if it were universal**. It is
not, and the error it induces is silent.* (Bounded 2026-08-17.)

> **`-as` and `-ē` continue `*-as` and `*-ai`, the endings of the `-a`
> STEM. The other classes never carried those endings.**
> The `-u` stem has `*-auz`; the `-i` stem has `*-īz`; the weak nouns have
> `*-en-`; the root nouns have no thematic vowel at all.
> **Applying the rule to them does not bring them up to date: it invents
> for them an inflection they never carried.**

| class | does the rule on finals apply? |
|---|---|
| **`-a`** (unmarked masc./neut.) | **YES** — it is for this class that it was established |
| **`[wa]`** `strō`, `snaiw`, `saiw`, `melo`, `smero` | **YES — a `-wa` stem IS an `-a` stem**, with a stem-final `w`. ⚠ **The kit was still writing `strawes/strawe` in its own grammar**: eighteen forms migrated that day to `strawas/strawē`, `snaiwas/snaiwē`, `saiwas/saiwē`, `melwas/melwē`, `smerwas/smerwē` |
| **`-ō`** (unmarked feminines in `-a`) | **YES** |
| **`[ja]`** | ⚠⚠ **YES — SETTLED 2026-08-17, a few hours after this line had declared it blocked.** A `-ja` stem is an `-a` stem, **and it has no `-i-` before the oblique ending**: `beddi/beddas/beddē`. The arbiter had been given since 2026-08-15, at rank (a) — see the arbitration, in the section on the paradigms. **84 lemmas migrated** |
| **`[jō]`** | ⚠⚠ **THE RULE, AND IT DISSOLVES THE QUESTION** (double enquiry, 2026-08-18): **the endings ARE those of the `-ō` stems, and the `-i-` is the STEM suffix standing before them.** There is therefore no contradiction with the masculine and the neuter: `hell-i-u` : `geb-u` ∷ `hell-i-a` : `geb-a`. · **Dative certain** (85–93 %): `bruggiu`, `helliu`, `sundiu` — four branches give the same form, Goth. `haljai`, ON `helju`, OS `helliu`, OHG `helliu`. · ⚠⚠ **THE GENITIVE IS SETTLED IN ITS TURN, on 2026-08-18, BY A SECOND DOUBLE ENQUIRY — AND IT WALKED THE CHAIN OF CITATION BACK TO ITS SOURCE.** The first enquiry put the genitive "one notch below" on the strength of Braune §209 Anm. 3, who makes the `-e` forms the oldest and `-ea/-ia` an analogical restoration. **The second read what Braune rests on.**

> **Hermann Paul, *PBB* 4 (1877), p. 344** — Braune's reference — **states no law at all.** He sets out **two competing analyses** and prefers the first only on the strength of **ONE SINGLE WORD**, *cumpurie*. **And his second analysis makes `-e` a CONTRACTION of `-ia/-ea`** — hence `-ia` the EARLIER state, the exact reverse of what Braune prints.


**The genealogy of the "law"**: Paul 1877 (a cautious observation, two explanations) → Kögel 1879 (promotes it to a *Lautgesetz*, and for OLD High German only) → Braune 1886 (generalises) → **Heidermanns 2018 (drops the word "law")**.

⚠ And Kögel, the law's inventor, **reads the `-ia` of manuscript Ra as the copyist's LIVING dialect**, not as an analogical restoration. *That reinterpretation is Braune's alone.*

Three converging supports: **Franck §52**, the grammar devoted to Franconian, gives the paradigm `sund(e)a` — with no `-e` — and calls the law *vielfach*, not *stets* · **Ringe & Taylor 2014** reconstruct PWGmc \*sibbjā > OHG `sippea`, **with no `-e` stage at all** · and the only unambiguous Frankish genitive in the whole documentation is **`sipbea` (Isidor)**, in `-ea`.

> **PROMULGATED: gen. sg. `-ia` (var. `-ea`).** `bruggia`, `hellia`, `sundia`. Confidence 60 % and 75 %. **Status: RECONSTRUCTED.**

⚠⚠ **AND IT MUST BE SAID WHAT LOW FRANCONIAN ACTUALLY GIVES, BECAUSE IT IS NOT THAT.** One enquirer went back over his own count of the Wachtendonck psalter on an untruncated text and made the list exhaustive: **`uuostinnon` (Ps 64:13) is the ONLY attested genitive singular of a `-jō` feminine, and it is in `-on`** — ⚠ a result **corroborated by an EXHAUSTIVE sweep** of every feminine in Köbler's dictionary: **twelve attested genitive singulars, all in `-on`, `-un` or `-o`** — Old Low Franconian *bedon*, *erthon*, *hulpon*, *stemmon*, *firingon*, *saldun*, *samnungun*, *heribergo* — **and the `-jō` feminine files in among them without distinguishing itself**. *No ending peculiar to the `-jō` stems appears anywhere in the corpus* — that is, they have already gone over to the WEAK declension. Nothing for *hella*, †*sunda*, *brugga*, *sippa*, *gerda*. **Any form of the type `sundia` in the genitive is therefore a reconstruction, never an attestation** — founded on OHG/OS comparison and on the one unambiguous Frankish genitive, `sipbea` (Isidor).

⚠ **AND A TRAP NOT TO BE MISSED, which the same check brought up**: the Low Franconian corpus DOES give a genitive in **`-ie`** — **`abulgie`** "irae", Ps 68:25 — but `ābulgi` is a **NEUTER in `-ja`**, not a `-jō` feminine. *The ending exists; it does not belong to that class.* The project carries both classes: do not carry one over to the other.

⚠ **And if `-e` is ever adopted, it must be adopted WHOLESALE** — §209 then imposes `-e` in the nominative, the accusative and the nominative-accusative plural too. *A genitive in `-e` beside a nominative in `-ia` would be a chimera no grammar supports.* · **The Wachtendonck psalter cannot decide**: its only forms, `hellon` and `uuostinnon`, have already gone WEAK — *a state of arrival, not a state of departure*, 350 to 400 years later. · ⚠ **The Saxon solution is NOT Ingvaeonic**: the dative `-u/-iu` stands squarely against OE `helle`, `sibbe`. On this point Old Saxon is on the side of Cologne and Fulda. And a **direct Low Franconian** witness confirms it: `salethu` glossing *tabernaculo*, dat. sg. in `-u` of a pure `-ō` stem. · ⚠⚠ **THREE CORRECTIONS TO THE KIT'S TEST WORDS**: `brugjō` is **the worst possible witness** (already weak in OS and ON, **absent from Low Franconian**) — take `hellja`, `sundja`; `*sunnjō` "sin" is **wrong**, it is `*sundjō`, and the `sunnia` of the *Lex Salica* is ANOTHER word, "lawful excuse"; `*hallj-` must be written `*hellj-`, primary mutation being already common. · ⚠ **And a warning about our own citations**: Franck's *Altfränkische Grammatik* **is not a Low Franconian grammar** — it covers Franconian in the Old High German sense (Isidor, Tatian, Otfrid). *There is no reference grammar of Low Franconian; there is nothing but van Helten 1902 and Quak 1981.* |
| **`[ī]`** | ⚠ **NO.** Its core is the deadjectival abstract type (`audmōdi`, `wirdi`, `menigi`, `wraithi`, `stolti`). ⚠⚠ **9 lemmas carry the `[ī]` mark** — the class overruns its core: `daupi` is deverbal, `linsi` is concrete. *The sentence said "8 lemmas" without saying whether it counted the TYPE or the MARK; clarified 2026-08-18 so that it could enter the register of verifiable assertions.* Its inflection has never been instructed |
| **`[i]` masc., `[nd]`** | ⚠⚠ **`[nd]` SPLITS IN TWO** (settled 2026-08-18, double enquiry: `friund`/`fīand` and `wīgant`/`weriand` do NOT have the same trajectory). · **Monosyllabic** — gen. sg. **`-as`** · dat. sg. **`-i ~ -e`, the only genuinely open slot** · **nom./acc. pl. WITH NO ENDING** — ⚠ *this is not an Ingvaeonic archaism: it is the continent that levelled later.* · **Disyllabic** — they pass to the **WEAK/participial** inflection, not to the `-a` stem: `neriando`, dat. sg. **`neriandin`**. ⚠ `weriand` is attested nowhere as a common noun: **conjectured**. · ⚠⚠ **ONLY THREE SLOTS DIAGNOSE** — gen. sg., dat. sg., nom./acc. pl. The gen. pl. `-ō` and the dat. pl. `-um` give the same form in both inflections since common Germanic: *a slot where both hypotheses predict the same form settles nothing.* · **`[i]` masc. remains open.** |
| **`[u]` `[n]` `[r]` `[rac]`** | ⚠⚠ **SETTLED 2026-08-18, DOUBLE ENQUIRY. There is not ONE chronology but FOUR, indexed on GENDER.** · **`[n]` weak: genitive in `-n`, never any transfer** — `Frankon`, `kirikun` (Goth. *hanins*, Ger. *des Hasen*). · **MASCULINE root nouns: `-es`**, the transfer complete before 550 — **`mannes`**, **`fōtes`**: the inherited genitive \*manz was becoming homophonous with the nominative after the fall of \*-z. · **FEMININE root nouns: ZERO ending** — **`naht`**, **`burg`**: `-es` is masculine/neuter, and no model of the same gender existed. · **Kinship `[r]`: ZERO** — **`fader`**, **`mōder`**, **`brōther`** — ⚠ *this is not resistance, it is the **regular** reflex of \*fadurz after the fall of \*-z.* · **`[u]`: the kit writes `sunes` AND DECLARES THE VARIATION.** It is the one point where the two enquirers hesitate (55 % / 25 %): if Franconian followed Saxon, the form at 550 would be **`sunies`**. ⚠ No witness dated before 700; **Bergakker** (425–475) gives `haþuþewas`, an `-a` stem — *the target of the analogy, not its date*. |
| **outside the classes** — `alu`, a `*-þ-` stem | **NO.** The lexicon declares it explicitly outside the classes; its entry carries `gen. alothes, dat. alothe` |

⚠⚠ **AND I MADE THE ERROR TODAY, ON THAT LAST WORD.** My automatic sweep
skipped `alothes` for want of identifying the lemma; **I migrated it by
hand to †`alothas`** without rereading the entry, which carried in that
same line *"a `-þ-` stem"*. Two forms returned to their state.

> *The automatic detection was right to refuse, and I corrected it.*
> **A tool that skips a case is not to be supplied by hand: one must
> first read WHY it skipped.** Nine times out of ten it skipped because
> the case was not to be treated.

#### ⚠ THE PREFIX IS `ga-` — but not every `gi-` is a prefix

*(Migration carried out 2026-08-17. ⚠ The filter goes at the START of a
migration: the first time it came after, and twenty cognates had been
corrupted.)*
⚠⚠ **This table prevents the reverse fault**: writing †`gagangan` for
`giengi`, or believing a `gibid` prefixed when it is not.

| set aside | why |
|---|---|
| `gisement` | a **French** word |
| `gift`, `giheizan`, `gilimpfan`, `gisuonen`, `giwizzi`, `gizeman`, `giefe`, `gimēnitha` | **cited witnesses** — ⚠ Gothic, for its part, carries `ga-` legitimately |
| ⚠⚠ **`giengi`** | **attested at RANK (a)** — Wachtendonck psalter, Ps 67:8. The preterite of `gangan`, not a prefixed form |
| ⚠⚠ `gibid`, `gibis`, `gibu`, `gibi`, `gildid`, `giuo`, `giuon` | **HAVE NO PREFIX.** Forms of `geban` and `geldan` produced by the raising `e → i`. `giuo`/`giuon` are likewise rank (a), in the Williram |
| `gietan` | **cited under a dagger** in the file that corrects it — see `gaetan` |

> ⚠⚠ **WHAT THE AUTOMATIC FILTER DID NOT SEE, AND I HAD TO REREAD BY
> HAND: `giengi`.** My filter knew `OHG`, `Goth.`, `OS` — it did not know
> **"rank (a) attests it"**, which is how THIS project names a witness.
> *A citation filter must know the vocabulary of the house, not only the
> abbreviations of the handbooks.*

⚠ **AND THE FILE ITSELF HAD TO BE EXCISED BEFORE THE MIGRATION.** It
cited `gigeban`, `giskrif`, `gisundi` in order to denounce them —
**exactly the strings that had to be replaced**, and nothing at the level
of the text distinguishes a citation from a live form. It was removed,
the rest migrated, then rewritten.
> *One does not filter what one can simply take out.* **A text that
> speaks of a form and a text that uses it are not separated by a
> pattern: they are separated by putting them in two files, if only for a
> second.**

⚠⚠ **AND THE DICTIONARY'S MIGRATION HAD ALSO LEFT A LIVE FORM**:
`seuthan` "to boil" kept `gisodan` in its principal parts, the sole
survivor in the whole lexicon. **A migration declared complete was
complete to within one form, and across both files at once.** *What a
migration lacks is almost never the rule: it is the count of its own
remainder.*

⚠ **A LEMMA TO BE ENTERED IN THE DICTIONARY**: `gathīhan` "to prosper, to
bring to a good end" is cited in the rule on personal substitutes and
**exists neither under `gi-` nor under `ga-` in the lexicon**, nor does
its simplex `thīhan`.


#### ⚠⚠ THE GENITIVE PLURAL `-ō` — for ALL classes

| class | form | status |
|---|---|---|
| `-a` stems | `dagō`, `mannō`, `skillingō`, `jārō`, `fiskō` | **promulgated** |
| `-i` stems | `gestiō`, `liudiō` | **promulgated** — the `-i-` is the THEMATIC vowel, not an ending: stem + `*-ǭ` |
| weak and `-ō` | `gebōnō`, `Frankōnō`, `garbōnō`, `bruggōnō` | **conjectured**, on the pattern of `gebōnō` |

⚠⚠ **DO NOT GO LOOKING FOR THE ENDING `-o`: IT BELONGS TO FIVE
CATEGORIES** — ordinals (`fiortho`), superlatives and comparatives
(`aldisto`, `betaro`), adverbs (`lango`, `ferro`), lemmas in `-o` (`melo`,
`māno`, `hairro`), and cited Latin. **A genitive plural is recognised by
being GOVERNED**: by a numeral (`tehan skok garbōnō`), a partitive
(`nihain mannō`), `thero`, or a genitive of price (`tehan penningō
werth`).

> *A pattern looks for an ending; a government looks for a function. The
> ending `-o` is shared by five categories; the government by one.*
> ⚠ **A table that teaches a correction carries the fault in its first
> column.** *A migration that runs through it destroys precisely what
> teaches it — without breaking anything a control knows how to see.*

#### The state of the entries' migration

**Rule promulgated 2026-08-16; the inflected forms in the entries stayed
in the 9th century until 2026-08-17.** The two translators of the 33rd
round reported it separately — *"the dictionary has not caught up with
the grammar, it has moved AWAY from it"* — with the argument that
decides:

> *"These are precisely the most useful entries — the ones that give an
> irregular genitive or dative — hence the ones a translator consults at
> the exact moment he cannot do without help."*

| | |
|---|---|
| labelled forms found | **77** |
| migrated, class certainly `-a` | **43** |
| left, `[ja]` stems | **11** — the slot blocked above |
| left, class not concerned | **21** |
| forms returned after my fault | **2** (`alu`) |
| forms from the kit's own grammar | **18** (the `-wa` stems) |

⚠ **Mechanisable because the forms are LABELLED**: `gen. *akkres*`. The
label gives the case, the case gives the ending. The third migration in a
row judged "not mechanisable" and which was — **by anchor, not by
pattern**. *A pattern searches in text; a label searches in a grammar.*

⚠⚠ **WHAT THE MIGRATION HAD TO UNDO IN ORDER TO FIND ITS LEMMAS**, and
every step is one of the kit's laws read backwards: **L10** (`lības` ←
`līf`, `gaskribas` ← `gaskrif`, `theubas` ← `theuf`), the **gemination**
(`hnappas` ← `hnapp`), the **syncope of composition** (`akkras` ←
`akkar`), and the alternation **th ~ d**. *A lemma is not read in its
inflected form: it is deduced from it, by undoing the laws in reverse
order.*

### ⚠⚠ The leak the audit had not seen: ALL the unstressed prefixes

> **`gi-` IS THE PRODUCT OF THE WEAKENING ITSELF.** Gothic has `ga-`, the
> oldest OHG (Isidor) has `ga-`, and `gi-` is the weakened form of the
> 8th–9th c. **At 550: `ga-`.**

The corollary is systematic and touches the whole lexicon:
**`ga-`** (not †`gi-`) · **`far-`** or `fra-` (not †`fir-`) · **`and-`**
or `ant-` (not †`int-`) · `bi-` · **`ur-`** or `uz-`.

⚠ **AND THE HOMOGRAPHY TRAP, FOR THE FOURTH TIME**: `gildid`, `gibid`,
`gibis` **have no prefix** — they are the forms of *geldan* and *geban*
that the raising produces. A blind migration would destroy them. **Always
collect with the context before replacing.**


⚠ A second point, declared: at 550 the prefix is probably **not yet
grammaticalised as a mark of the participle**. Gothic `ga-` is aspectual,
and OS as well as OE know *bundan* with no prefix. **It is therefore made
VARIABLE, not obligatory** — which agrees with the rule on the perfect:
if there is no grammaticalised perfect, it is partly because the
preverbal perfectiviser is still doing the work.

### The syncope of composition vowels — **SETTLED 2026-08-18**

**It is CONDITIONED by the weight of the first member.** Neither kept
everywhere nor lost everywhere: the disjunction itself was badly put.

> **R1 — HEAVY monosyllabic first member** (long vowel, diphthong, or
> short + 2 consonants) → **no vowel.**
> `skāphūs`, `halsberg`, `goldsmith`, `holtberg`, `markstain`, `wīngard`.
>
> **R2 — LIGHT monosyllabic first member** (short + 1 consonant) and
> `-i -u -n` stems → **vowel kept.** `heriban`, `siniskalk`.
>
> **R3 — POLYSYLLABIC first member**, or a compound of 4 syllables or
> more → **no vowel**, whatever the weight. `himilrīki`, `morgangeba`,
> `wintarmānōth`.
>
> **R4 — Strongly LEXICALISED compound** → no vowel, even under R2.
> Gothic shows it as early as 350: `guda-laus` but `gud-hus`.
>
> **R5 — The SUPPORT vowel is not the composition vowel.**
> *mariscalcus* renders `marhskalk`: the `-i-` there breaks a cluster
> `-rhsk-` unpronounceable in Latin. **It is not counted.** The same for
> the `-e-` of *rachineburgius*.
>
> **R6 — ONOMASTICS IS OUT OF SCOPE.** Anthroponyms keep the joint by
> analogical recomposition and by the convention of Latin scribes.
> **One never carries a form from a proper name over to a common noun.**

⚠⚠ **THE LEXICON WAS ALREADY OBEYING THIS RULE WITHOUT KNOWING IT.** Of
the **29** compounds whose first member is an `-a` stem ending in a
consonant — the only ones the rule concerns — the partition is
**perfect, 29 out of 29, no counter-example**: the **3** that carry the
vowel (`dagafrist`, `dagawerk`, `wegakrūki`) are the only 3 whose first
member is a light monosyllable; the other **26** are all heavy or
polysyllabic. **The ruling costs NO migration at all.**

> ⚠ **And that is precisely why it must not be boasted of.** Those 29
> lemmas were copied from compounds attested in OS and OHG. The partition
> does not confirm the rule: **it confirms that the standards observe it
> and that the copying was faithful.** An agreement one owes to one's own
> copying is not an independent verification.

### ⚠⚠ WHAT THIS RULING REVOKES — two errors, one of them the foundation

**1. The structural argument is WITHDRAWN.** The kit held the fall of the
composition vowel to be *"a particular case of the same weakening"* as
`gebōm` → `gebun` and `ga-` → `gi-`. **False** (two enquirers, 85 % and
90 %, the same reasoning): these are **three distinct prosodic
positions** — `ga-` **pretonic**, the composition vowel **medial
intertonic**, `-ōm` **final**. ⚠ Empirical refutation: the Gothic of 350
has already lost its short finals (`*dagaz` → `dags`) and keeps the
composition vowel (`weinagards`); the OHG of the 9th c. keeps the
**medial** `-a-` of `taga-sterro` when the **final** `-a-` has been gone
for centuries.

> **What is kept of it**: the cause is indeed common — the fixed initial
> stress. **A common cause is not a common rule, and a common rule is not
> a common date.** *It is that distinction the kit had crushed.*


**2. *Chlothacharius* was changing columns.** It had been entered under
syncope: that is a misanalysis. What falls in `*Hlōþa-hari` →
*Chlotharius* is the **intervocalic `-h-` of the SECOND member**, with
contraction — as in OHG *sehan* → *sēn*. ⚠ **The vowel that survives in
Chlo-tha-rius IS the composition vowel.** Control: `*Hlōdo-wald` →
*Chlodoaldus* → *Cloud*, loss of the `-w-`, the same figure. *The kit's
best witness to syncope was in fact witnessing its preservation.*

### ⚠ THE SLOT THE RULING DOES NOT CLOSE — a LIGHT `-a` stem

**It is exactly the slot of the lexicon's 3 lemmas**, and both enquiries
give it **undecidable** — H1 rates it **50 %**, "undecidable by
construction". The reason is a disagreement between standards:

| Witness | rank | form |
|---|---|---|
| OHG *taga-werc*, *taga-raud*, *taga-sterro* | (c) | **with** |
| OE *dæġ-weorc* | outside the area | **without** |
| Du *dagwerk*, *dagloon* | (a) | **without** |
| Du *dageraad* | (a) | **with** |

⚠⚠ **The rank (a) witness contradicts itself** — `dagwerk` without,
`dageraad` with — and so the hierarchy of witnesses cannot be invoked to
decide, as I first thought it could. **Rank (c) never gives the letter**:
*taga-werc* cannot decide on its own.

**Decision: the 3 existing forms are kept, they are not generalised, and
no new ones are forged in this slot without saying so.** For any new
compound with a light `-a` first member, **both forms are admissible** and
the translator notes which he took.

### ⚠ WHAT THE RULING COSTS — and what it gives back

It **gives back the most used device of word formation in the language**:
`holt` + `thak` → **`holtthak`** is clear by R1. ⚠ `stal` + `thak` stays
open — `stal` is a **light** monosyllable, the slot R2/R2bis leave empty,
and falling back on the analytic genitive (*that thak thes stallas*)
remains wise.

> ⚠⚠ **A RESERVATION ON THE RATING, NOT TO BE ERASED.** Both enquirers
> gave their references **from memory**, and flagged them themselves as
> UNVERIFIED. The rule rests on their convergence, not on checked
> citations: it is **solid as a form, weak as a reference**. And one of
> them supplies his own counter-example — *alodis* < `*al-aud`, a light
> first member, **without** a vowel. *An enquiry that supplies the pieces
> against its own conclusion is worth more than one that supplies none.*

⚠⚠ **WHAT THE RULE DOES NOT DO.** It is good **statistically** and
**nearly useless item by item** — 50–55 % on an isolated lemma, *"barely
better than chance"*. **It is applied to classes, never to decide a
single case against an attested standard.** ⚠ And Latin spelling attests
the **presence** of a vowel, not its **timbre**.

### ⚠⚠ THE VOWEL BEFORE FINAL `-r -l -n -m` — the third grey zone, settled

**"*mahle*, *mahlē* or *mahale*?"** — that is how the two translators of
the 33rd round put the question. **Answer: `mahlē`.** And the rule that
gives it explains at the same stroke a case the kit had merely recorded.


> ⚠⚠ **THIS IS NOT A SYNCOPE.** The vowel does not fall before the
> ending: **it was never there.** Proto-Germanic says `*maþlą`, `*akraz`,
> `*fuglaz` — **with no vowel**. Frenkisk writes `mahal`, `akkar`, `fogal`
> **because a consonant cluster cannot end a word**: the vowel is
> **anaptyctic**, born of the absolute final position.
> **As soon as an ending follows, the final is no longer absolute and the
> vowel has no reason to be there.**

It is the same mechanism as **L10** (`wīf` ~ `wības`): *an alternation
created by final position, not by the ending*. **And it turns the 33rd
round's question round: one should not have asked when the vowel FALLS,
but when it APPEARS.**

| type | behaviour | examples |
|---|---|---|
| **anaptyctic** — no vowel in the etymon | **disappears before any vowel-initial ending** | `akkar`/**`akkras`**/**`akkrē`** · `mahal`/**`mahlas`**/**`mahlē`** · `fogal`/**`fuglas`** · `taikan`/**`taiknas`** · `wagan`/**`wagnas`** · `nagal`/**`naglas`** · `regan`/**`regnas`** · `thegan`/**`thegnas`** · `hraban`/**`hrabnas`** · `wolkan`/**`wolknas`** · `wāpan`/**`wāpnas`** · `fōthar`/**`fōthras`** · `wedar`/**`wedras`** · `wundar`/**`wundras`** · `aldar`/**`aldras`** · `silubar`/**`silubras`** · `fingar`/**`fingras`** · `wintar`/**`wintras`** · `hungar`/**`hungras`** · `nebal`/**`neblas`** · `hagal`/**`haglas`** · `galdar`/**`galdras`** · `gīsal`/**`gīslas`** · `twīfal`/**`twīflas`** · `wehsal`/**`wehslas`** · `āthom`/**`āthmas`** · adj. `bittar`/**`bittra`** |
| **etymological** — the vowel IS in the etymon | **never falls** | `hamar` (\*hamaraz)/**`hamaras`** · `sumar`/**`sumaras`** · `bibar`/**`bibaras`** · `ebur`/**`eburas`** · `morgan` (\*murganaz)/**`morganas`** · `sadal`/**`sadalas`** · `stapal`/**`stapalas`** · `lakan`/**`lakanas`** · `drohtin`/**`drohtinas`** |
| **Latin loan** | ⚠⚠ **IT DEPENDS ON THE FORM BORROWED, NOT ON BEING A LOAN** — see the amendment below | `sestar`/**`sestaras`**, pl. **`sestara`** · `fenstar` · `mantal` · `plāstar` · `prestar` · `kopar` · `fiebar` · `apostol` · `diobol` — **but** `spēgal`/**`spēglas`** and `tegal`/**`teglas`** |

⚠⚠ **AND THIS RULE EXPLAINS THE `sestara` THE KIT HAD ONLY RECORDED.**
The kit carried *"the plural is therefore **sestara**, syncope blocked, as
in the example *sehs sestara alothes*"* — **a fact without its cause.**
The cause is here: *sestar* is a loan from `sextarius`, its vowel is
Latin, it was never anaptyctic. **There was no syncope to block.**
> *An exception recorded without its cause becomes an exception extended
> at random.* **This one covered eleven loans and only one had been
> named.**

⚠⚠ **AMENDMENT OF 2026-08-17, IN THE EVENING — AND IT IS THE 34th ROUND
THAT FORCED IT.** The "Latin loan" line said *"the vowel never falls"*.
**That is too strong, and three recorded entries already contradicted
it**: the kit wrote `spēgal → spēgle` and made that word **the explicit
model** on which `tegal` was adapted. A translator applying the new rule
condemns three old rulings; a translator following the entries breaks the
new rule.

> **THE RIGHT QUESTION IS NOT "IS IT A LOAN?" BUT "WHAT WAS BORROWED?"**
> Vulgar Latin had **already syncopated** these words before they were
> taken: the *Appendix Probi* corrects by name Lat. *"speculum non
> **speclum**"*, Lat. *"tabula non **tabla**"*, Lat. *"vetulus non **veclus**"*.
> **`spēgal` is not borrowed from Lat. `speculum` but from Lat. `speclum`** — hence
> with a final cluster, hence with anaptyxis, hence with loss before an
> ending: `spēglas`. The same for `tegal` < Lat. `tegla`.

| borrowed from | behaviour | examples |
|---|---|---|
| a Latin form **already syncopated** (the *Appendix Probi* type) | **anaptyctic — the vowel falls** | `spēgal`/`spēglas` · `tegal`/`teglas` |
| a **full** Latin form, whose vowel is stressed or protected | **the vowel stays** | `sestar` < *sextarius* · `fenstar` < *fenestra* · `apostol` · `diobol` · `kopar` · `prestar` |
| ⚠ **`esil`** < Gmc \*asilu- (via Lat. *asellus*) | **the vowel stays** — it is in the Germanic form, and OHG gives `esil`/**`esiles`** | `esil`/**`esilas`**, dat. **`esilē`**, pl. **`esila`** |

⚠⚠ **AND THE ENTRY FOR `esil` SAID THE OPPOSITE** — *"Gen. esles, dat.
eslē, pl. esla (syncope of the `-il` stem, as in †spēgal → †spēgle)"*. It
was right about `spēgal` and wrong about `esil`: **it extended to a
Germanic word a syncope that belongs to Vulgar Latin.** Corrected.
> *An analogy between two words that resemble each other is not an
> argument: `spēgal` and `esil` end alike and come from elsewhere.*


⚠ **THE TEST IS THE ETYMON, NEVER THE FORM.** `hamar` and `akkar` rhyme,
end in the same syllable, carry the same gender — and decline
differently. **The attestation column of the dictionary carries the
etymon: that is where one looks, not at the ending.**

⚠ **WHAT THE ATTESTED LANGUAGES BLUR, AND WHY THEY ARE NOT FOLLOWED
HERE**: OHG writes `ackares` **beside** `ackres`, OE `æceres` beside
`fugles`. **Levelling has begun, and it runs towards generalising the
vowel.** That is exactly the `dagas` argument: *what the languages attest
is later than the levelling; 550 is earlier than it.* One keeps the
distinction the etymon imposes.

### ⚠⚠ `gaetan` — the slot the migration broke

**BOTH translators of the 31st round reported it separately**, and they
are right: the kit wrote †**`gietan`**, participle of *etan*, and took the
trouble to explain that it was "the regular contraction of *gi-* +
*etan*".

> **In migrating `gi-` → `ga-`, the rule on finals DESTROYS that
> contraction and does not say what replaces it.** One of the two wrote
> †*gaetan* while reporting the gap; the other kept `gietan` unmigrated,
> noting that his text then carried **"a 9th-century form in the middle of
> 6th-century forms — exactly the fault the kit condemns about *hield*"**.

**Ruling: one writes `gaetan`, in two syllables, with no contraction.**
⚠ **And it is a CONVENTION, not a result.** The fate of an `-a` prefix
before a vowel-initial root is not established for 550: Gothic keeps *ga-*
before a vowel, OHG contracts. The full form is laid down because it is
transparent and prejudges nothing.

> *A migration that runs through a contraction cannot be merely a
> substitution of letters: it must say what becomes of the contraction.
> This one did not, and two readers out of two fell into the gap.*

### ⚠⚠ What remains UNDECIDABLE, and is not filled in

The four enquirers declare them, partly the same ones:
· **nom. pl. of the `-a` stems**: `-ōs` (an inherited archaism) or `-ā`
(generalisation of the accusative, Franconian consistency). **An open crux
of West Germanic morphology** — both are defensible, the mixture is not.
The kit writes `dagā` for Franconian consistency, **and declares it**;

  ⚠⚠ **THE RULING ON UNWRITTEN QUANTITY — 2026-08-21, 43rd round.**
  Translator L noticed that the register writes **`dagā`** and that three
  worked examples write **`daga`** — *"three short occurrences against one
  long; my whole sentence 3 depended on it; I wrote `wintra`, on the
  worked example, **against the table**"*. The second translator wrote the
  same form, by the same route.
  ⚠ **This was NOT a disagreement about reconstruction: it was a macron
  that had dropped.** The two forms are the same. **But the kit writes
  them differently, and a translator cannot know that.**
    ⚠⚠ **AND THE SOURCE OF THE CONFUSION IS LEGITIMATE, WHICH IS WHAT
    MAKES IT DANGEROUS**: rank (a) attests ***daga*** (nom. pl. Ps 72:10;
    acc. pl. Ps 54:24) — **with no macron, because NO manuscript writes
    quantity.** That spelling must stay bare: *it is a record, not a form
    of the kit.* **Every other occurrence carries the macron.**
    > **RULE: a form CITED from a witness is written as the witness writes
    > it; a form PRODUCED by the kit carries its quantity. When the two
    > look alike, one says which one is being read.**

  ⚠ **None of the controls of the time could see this.** `kollat` compares
  forms of equal length plus one letter; `daga` and `dagā` have the same
  length **and the same number of letters**. *A control that measures
  length is blind to the diacritic — and the diacritic is, here, the whole
  of the information.* **Whence test E**, added the same day;
· **gen. and dat. sg. of the `-ō` stems**: the handbooks do not cleanly
refer OHG *geba*/*gebu* and OE *giefe* to a single etymon;
· **gen. pl. of the `-ō` stems**: the `-ōn-` extension is continental and
of **unknown date**;
· **dat. pl. `-um` or `-am`**: Bergakker gives *kusjam*, read as `-am` by
Vennemann and Looijenga. If the reading holds, the rounding would be later
than 450 **in Franconian**. The reading is disputed: nothing is built on
it, but it is known;
· **1st sg. of the subjunctive** (`*-au`): the levelling on the 3rd is
complete everywhere, its date is unknown;
· **whether the participial prefix is obligatory** at 550.

### ⚠⚠ STATE OF THE CORPUS — a declared ruling

**The project's texts carry the state PRIOR to this audit** — †`dages`,
†`dage`, †`gageban`, †`gebum`. ⚠ **The dagger was added 2026-08-17**: this
note cited four abrogated forms WITHOUT marking them, and a reader could
have taken them for current. *A note that describes the old state must
mark it as old, or it teaches it.* The migration is not mechanisable: the
genitive `-es` cannot be told from a French word by a pattern, and the
prefix `gi-` cannot be told from the root of *geban* and *geldan*.

> **The rule prevails against the corpus. A text carrying the old state is
> a text to be migrated, not a witness.** And in accordance with golden
> rule no. 11, **the project's own corpus witnesses nothing**: it cannot
> therefore be invoked against the rule just laid down.

*A migration to be done text by text, with the context recorded. One does
not migrate blind what one cannot tell apart.*

## FOUR COMPLETE PARADIGMS, IN ONE BLOCK

⚠⚠ **THIS BLOCK WAS BORN OF THE 30th ROUND (2026-08-16).** Both
translators made the same observation: the verb's slots are scattered over
hundreds of lines, filed by date of ruling and not by matter. One had to
cross-check **three places 550 lines apart** to establish a single plural
imperative; the other did not find the plural of the weak preterite and
aligned it on the strong. **So the four paradigms are written out in full,
in one place.**
⚠⚠ **AND HERE IS THE ARBITER, WHICH WAS MISSING — 31st round,
2026-08-16.** This block said it prevailed "against any isolated line that
contradicted it", and the rule on finals said it prevailed "against the
corpus". **Each declared itself the arbiter of the other**, and the two
translators had to decide alone, before writing a single word.
> **A RULE PREVAILS OVER A TABLE. A table gathers what the rules
> prescribe; it prescribes nothing by itself.** This block prevails
> against an isolated line, never against a dated ruling that abrogates it
> by name.
· *"A translator who follows the examples and a translator who follows the
rule will produce two texts that do not resemble each other. That is not a
divergence of style, it is a divergence of language."*
· ⚠ **And the real fault was not the missing arbiter: it was promulgating
a rule without carrying a single worked example into the new state.** The
slots are now aligned. **TO PROMULGATE IS ALSO TO REWRITE THE EXAMPLES —
otherwise the rule has no language.**


⚠ It does not replace the rules: it *gathers* them. The rulings that
explain each slot stay where they are, and that is where one goes to find
out WHY.

### 5 — THE NOUN: the five declensions, slots filled

⚠⚠ **THIS BLOCK IS NO LONGER WRITTEN BY HAND — it is GENERATED from
`cases.md`, the register of slots (2026-08-20).**

The 41st round found four defects, and **three were in tables I had
written two hours earlier**. This one carried two: it re-declared as a
"gap" the feminine genitive the kit writes three times elsewhere, and the
`-i` paradigm it records in full. Translator G of the 41st round:

> *"An incomplete table is more dangerous than no table at all,
> **because it inspires confidence**. […] As long as the rulings pile up
> by date without the tables being **REGENERATED** from them, every new
> table will add a layer of truth that contradicts an older layer still
> legible."*

**`cases.md` is to the kit what `lexique.md` is to the dictionary: the
structured source of truth from which the table below is drawn.** One no
longer writes a slot here; one fills it there, and the table remakes
itself.
⚠ **It covers the NOUN only.** The verb, the adjective and the pronoun
still have hand-written tables, hence exposed to the same defect —
**declared, not hidden.**
