  (= Goth. *-aina*, IE optative \*-oi-n-), the *ē* being long because the
  diphthong is internal. For a state of **550** the long vowel is the
  right form; it is in the 9th–10th centuries that it shortens to *-en ~
  -an ~ -in*. **The kit was right and I was wrong.**
  ⚠⚠ **THE 2nd PERSON CARRIES THE -s, AND THE TABLE DID NOT SAY SO.** It
  wrote "-e in the sg." for all three persons, while writing three lines
  below, in the PRETERITE subjunctive, "1sg gābi, 2sg **gābis**, 3sg
  gābi". The *-s* of the 2nd person is the general mark of the language,
  and its only declared exception is the preterite-presents. **The forced
  grid ranked this gap first**: *thū* + present subjunctive was
  unwritable — every prayer, every protasis, every piece of reported
  speech in the 2nd person. Filled 2026-08-15, and rank (a) attests it
  directly: ***sīs*** three times in the Wachtendonck psalter, against the
  indicative *bist* — hence a 2 sg. subjunctive in *-s*, with no possible
  ambiguity.

· **wesan**: sg. sī / **sīs** / sī, **pl. sīn**;
· **dōn**: sg. dō / dōs / dō, plural **dōn** ⚠ (double enquiry at rank
  (a), 2026-08-15: the 3 sg. **duo** is attested twice and is the ONLY
  demonstrated slot, the indicative there being *duot*; the others are
  **homographs of the indicative** — *dōs* — or **of the infinitive** —
  *dōn*. The syncretism is real, it is not a gap: in Old Low Franconian
  **indicative and subjunctive are formally distinguished only in the 3rd
  sg.**, and elsewhere it is the Latin word alongside that identifies the
  mood. **The 2nd pl. exists nowhere in the corpus, for any verb** — a
  structural gap, not an accidental one);
· **preterite-presents**: sg. mugi, witi, skuli; **pl. mugin, witin,
  skulin**. Both translators wrote *sīn* and *mugin* with no table: they
  were right, and it was the table that was missing.

#### The non-finite forms, and the agreement of the verb

**The past participle in the PASSIVE is invariable**: *sia **ganerid**
wārun* "they were saved", *fon holtē **gawerkōd*** "of worked wood".


### ⚠⚠ THE SLOTS THE 36th ROUND FOUND EMPTY

**Twenty-eight decisions taken without the kit prescribing them**, by
each of the two translators. Translator A draws the distinction that
governs all the rest:

> *"A slot declared open makes the translator GIVE UP; a slot simply
> absent makes him INVENT WITHOUT KNOWING IT. The kit knows this
> mechanism perfectly — it writes it itself — and to this day draws only
> one consequence from it."*

**Four slots are filled below because the kit's own rules already decided
them. Three are DECLARED open** — one does not fill a slot by guessing,
which is precisely what is held against silence.

#### 1. THE SINGULAR IMPERATIVE OF CLASS I — the slot was empty

⚠⚠ **There was not ONE form in the whole kit.** The file devotes forty
lines to arbitrating the raising `eu → iu` in the imperative of class
**II**, and gives `nim! gif! quim!` (III b/IV/V), `siuth! skiuf!` (II),
`gang! stand! wes!` (VI/VII), **`hefi!`** (`heffian`, strong with a `-j-`
present — ⚠ with its `-i`, and **`f`, not †`hebi!`**: the ruling on
`heffian` is promulgated, but until 2026-08-26 it lived in the file on
consonant alternation, where nobody looks for an imperative), `hauri!
wardo! hebi! **segi!** dō!` (weak; ⚠ **`segi!`**: `seggian` belongs to the
closed annexe of `hebbian`, which degeminates outside the `-j-` forms —
`segis`/`segid` → `segi!`, as `hebis` → `hebi!`. — ⚠⚠ `hebi!` is
`hebbian` "to have", **not** `heffian`) ⚠ *(†`habe!` withdrawn 2026-08-21
— the `-ēn` class no longer exists)*.
Class I — `rīdan`, `skrīban`, `bītan`, `stīgan`, `līthan`, `blīkan`,
`glīdan`, `snīthan` — had nothing.

> **PROMULGATED: the sg. imperative of class I is the bare stem of the
> present singular, which keeps its `ī`** — `rīdan`, present `rīdis`,
> gives **`rīd!`**; likewise **`stīg!`**, **`bīt!`**, **`snīth!`**,
> **`glīd!`**.
> ⚠ And **`skrīf!`** by **L10**, the `*ƀ` hardening in absolute final
> position exactly as in `geban → gif!`.

**Status: DERIVED, not attested** — but the **two translators of the 36th
round derived it separately and arrived at the same form**, each
reporting that the kit did not carry it. *Two independent derivations
that converge are not worth an attestation; they are worth more than a
rule written by one hand.*

#### 2. THE PRETERITE SUBJUNCTIVE OF `unnan` — the one preterite-present forgotten

The kit gives the other six — `mahti`, `wissi`, `skoldi`, `woldi`,
`thorfti`, `aihti` — and forgets `unnan`, **which is the very verb of the
formula of seigneurial grant**. Translator A needed it for "what you have
granted me", that is, for the most formal sentence in the text.

> **PROMULGATED: `unda` → pret. subj. `undi / undis / undi`**, on the
> recorded pattern `wolda → woldi`, `skolda → skoldi`.

⚠ **Translator B wrote `undes`** — the WEAK preterite 2 sg. in `-es`,
which is the indicative. The divergence is not about the ending, it is
about the **mood**: `unnan` being listed nowhere among the
preterite-presents, B treated it as weak. *A class one does not declare
gets guessed — and gets guessed differently by different readers.*


#### 3. THE NEGATIVE IMPERATIVE — the form existed, the rule was missing

The 36th round's text carried **two prohibitions** (*"change nothing"*,
*"do not write to him"*) and the kit had no rule at all. Both translators
wrote `ni` + imperative, and A found on his own the example that
justifies it, buried in the file on the oar: ***ni lāt thīn rōthar
faran***.

> **PROMULGATED: the prohibitive is `ni` + IMPERATIVE**, the unmarked
> turn.
> ⚠ The negative jussive subjunctive is not excluded; it is simply not
> the ordinary form.

*The kit possessed the form and did not teach it: it lived in an example,
not in a rule. A translator does not imitate the rule, he imitates the
example — provided he stumbles on it.*

#### 4. ⚠ THREE SLOTS DECLARED OPEN, and not filled

**(a) The genitive of `niowiht`.** Both wrote `niowihtas`, and both gave
it in medium or low confidence: `niowiht` is a compound of `wiht`,
**which has no dictionary entry** and which is an `-i` stem in part of
Germanic. The question is the CLASS of `wiht`, not the ending. ⚠ And it
is not idle: `biddan` governs the **genitive** of the thing, so "I ask
nothing of you" is **unwritable** without it. **A safe route meanwhile:
`niowihtas`.**

**(b) The place of `thes` in the clitic group.** The kit promulgates the
internal order — *"nominative first, then accusative, then dative"* —
⚠⚠ **AND THE QUESTION WAS BADLY PUT — ruling of 2026-08-18, double
enquiry.** The kit was looking for a RANK for `thes` in the clitic group.
Both enquirers answer that there is not ONE genitive but **THREE**, and
that they do not behave alike:

| genitive | behaviour | confidence |
|---|---|---|
| **adnominal** ("the house *of my father*") | **NEVER clitic**, stays inside its noun phrase | 0.92 / 0.85 |
| **`thes`, adverbial** ("thereof, on that account") | ⚠⚠ **this is NOT a clitic** — it is the genitive of the DEMONSTRATIVE, a full word, stressable, which **occupies the INITIAL field** | 0.85 / 0.75 |
| **governed by the VERB** (the argument, the real "of it") | the only candidate for the clitic group — and it is **`is`/`es`**, genitive of the personal pronoun | 0.60 |

⚠⚠ **THE TEST THAT SEPARATES THE FIRST TWO IS METRICAL, AND IT IS HARD**:
in Old Saxon verse, `thes` **ALLITERATES**. *A clitic cannot carry
alliteration.* The metre of the *Heliand* is here an empirical test that
no reasoning replaces.

⚠ **The RANK of the adverbal genitive remains UNDECIDABLE** — 0.45 and
≤ 0.35. The reason is structural: **verbs governing a genitive are mostly
bivalent**, the genitive being their only object; a cluster of four
clitics one of which is a genitive is attested nowhere. *One does not
settle an order on data that do not exist.*

⚠ **And a reservation on the order itself**: in Old English, **the
hierarchy of PERSON outranks that of CASE** — a 1st- or 2nd-person
pronoun precedes a 3rd-person one whatever its case. C1 sees this as the
most profitable addition to the rule. Open slot, named.
and promulgates separately that `thes` is **"exactly the French *en*"**, a
property of the whole class of impersonals. **It gives it no rank at
all.** Both translators placed it on their own authority, and B notes
that the kit's examples contradict each other: *`mī ist naud`* puts the
dative first, but an oblique clitic **cannot** occupy the absolute head
⚠⚠ **— EXCEPT BEFORE AN IMPERSONAL VERB, AND THE EXCEPTION IS ADDED
2026-08-21.** The affect section gives ***mi thunkid***, ***mik
hriuwid***, ***mī langōd***: **oblique in absolute head position, nine
times out of nine.** The prohibition and the section contradict each
other, and the kit did not know it — *because all its impersonals are in
the FIRST person, and the conflict shows only in the third.* Translator S
of the 46th round wrote ***Imu thūhta***, following the form against the
prohibition, and reports it:
> *"The same mechanism as the collision `thō gaf hē it imu`, arbitrated
> in table ② — **but that one was seen.**"*
⚠ **One writes the oblique first before an impersonal**: that is the form
attested in all three branches (Goth. *þugkeiþ mis*, OE *mē þyncþ*,
OHG *mih riuwit*). *The prohibition bore on the ARGUMENTAL clitics of a
personal verb; the experiencer of an impersonal is not in that case — it
is the only nominal argument of its clause.*
according to the rule of clitics. **The kit manufactures this problem
itself without seeing it.**

**(c) ⚠⚠ THE DUAL: `unser` or `unker`?** **A clear divergence in the 36th
round.** A wrote `unser mōder`, B `unker mōder`. B invokes the dual,
pointing out that the kit promulgates it **with "two brothers" as the
canonical example of obligatory use** — and the message of the 36th round
is precisely addressed by one brother to another. ⚠ If B is right, A
committed a fault the kit made it obligatory to avoid. **But the kit gives
the dual only in a TABLE OF PRONOUNS**: nothing says that `unker` is used
attributively, or whether it agrees. *A form promulgated as obligatory
whose syntax is not given is a delayed trap.*


### ⚠⚠ NINE GOVERNMENTS THE GLOSS PROMISES AND THE ENTRY DOES NOT SUPPLY

**Case opened 2026-08-18, named, counted, not settled.**

The ruling on the default government declared its own condition of
validity: *"one deviant NOT recorded turns the ruling into a TRAP, since
the translator will be entitled to believe in the regular case."* The 35th
round found the first breach in eight hours (`thurfan` + genitive). The
36th round gave **the control that counts them** — translator A put it in
one sentence: *"sweep the glosses containing 'to', 'of', 'in' and check
that the lemma carries its mention."*

**The count is nine**, and the 16th control `rektio` keeps them:

| lemma | what the gloss promises | status |
|---|---|---|
| `manōn` | to remind **of** | acc. + gen.? — reported by BOTH translators |
| `wārōn` | to establish the truth **of** | gen.? |
| `tredan` | to tread **on** | ⚠ acc. **or** dat. — the gloss carries two senses |
| `tōdōn` | to join **to** | dat.? |
| `wrōgian` | to lay a complaint **against** | acc. + gen. of the grievance? |
| ~~`warian`~~ | ⚠⚠ **NO LONGER EXISTS** — a doublet of `werian`, deleted on the evening of 2026-08-18 | the cell was not a government, it was one lemma too many |
| `hurtan` | to knock **against** | dat. or a preposition? |
| `mōtan` | to be allowed **to** | ⚠ **an artefact of the gloss**: + bare infinitive |
| `hrampōn` | to creep **along** | ⚠ **an artefact of the gloss**: a phrase of the glossing language |

⚠ **TWO OF THE NINE ARE DEFECTS OF THE GLOSS, NOT OF THE VERB** — the
glossing language promises there a government Frenkisk does not carry. *A
gloss is a translation, not a definition: it can lie about the syntax of
the word it renders.*

⚠⚠ **AND THE CONTROL SEES ONLY WHAT THE GLOSSING LANGUAGE BETRAYS.** The
six governments guessed at by translator B bore on the **recipient** and
the **source** — to whom one writes, from whom one learns. **No gloss
announces that**, and `rektio` is blind to it by construction. *The ruling
on the default settles the first argument; the second and the third have no
rule at all.*

**Pending the enquiry: the explicit preposition is the safe road** for
these nine, as the analytic genitive is for the composition vowel.


Promulgated 2026-08-07 (requisition R11) on the four models the kit already
carried without drawing the rule from them.
**Participle of the passive: INVARIABLE.** *sia ganerid wārun* "they were
saved", and not †ganerida. It is a **predicate**, not an epithet: the
agreement rule for the apposed participle does not reach it.
**Agreement of the verb with coordinated subjects**: the verb goes into the
**plural**, whatever the order (*thār Landerīk endi Ansoberht
**sittiand***). No agreement by proximity.
**Substantivised infinitive** (neuter a-stem): that wesan "being, essence",
gen. wesanas, dat. **wesanē** — not to be confused with the gerund **te
wesanne**, which exists only after *te*.
Comparative -iro, superlative -ist (declined weak). ⚠ **The LEXICALISED
comparative in -aro NEVER syncopates**: *aftar → **aftaro***, *oba →

#### The compound tenses

**Active perfect and pluperfect**: `hebbian` + past participle, the
participle **invariable** (hē habda mik bifangan "he had overrun me");
verbs of motion and of state: wesan + participle (hē was kuman). Not to be
confused with the state passive (wesan + part. of a transitive verb).
⚠ **The perfect of a GENITIVE-governing verb** — *I have forgotten your
name*, `ik hebbiu thīnes namon fargetan` (84th round): the genitive
stays and the participle stays invariable, **by analogy, NOT ATTESTED**
(read 2026-09-07: no *habda* + participle of OS *fargetan* or *gihuggian*
in any text; the simple tenses keep the genitive, Hel. 3604 *fargâtun
godes rîkies*, 4998 *gihugde thero uuordo*, against one accusative,
2506 *that he it gehugda*).





### Word formation

**Nominal composition** productive (head on the right): transparent
occasional compounds admitted, above all in poetry (kennings) — ābanddrōm
"evening dream", northfenstar "north window".
**Productive preverbation**: far- (loss), bi- (around/completely), **ga-**
(completed), **and-** (**receptive AND separative** — andfāhan "to
receive" but andfleuhan "to escape", andbindan "to unbind"; the separative
value promulgated 2026-08-04), wither- (against), and the **directional
preverbs ūt- (out), inn- (in), nithar- (down), up- (up), thurh-
(through)** + a recorded verb, with a transparent sense: farlātan "to
leave", bislotan "shut in", ūtgangan "to go out", nitharstīgan "to go
down".

⚠⚠ **TWO CORRECTIONS OF THE 35th ROUND ON THIS ONE LINE.**
· **It wrote `gi-` (completed)** — and `untfleuhan`, `untbindan` — three
  months after the ruling of 2026-08-17 that promulgates **`ga-`**. It
  escaped because **a prefix CITED BARE escapes the detector**, which
  requires three letters after it. *The list that teaches the preverbs was
  teaching the one the kit had abrogated.*
· **The DIRECTIONAL preverbs were missing**, although the dictionary
  carries six compounds of them — `ūtfaran`, `ūtgangan`, `ūtgeutan`,
  `inngangan`, `nitharstīgan`, `thurhborōn`. **BOTH translators of the 35th
  round reported it separately**, and one put an asterisk on `*ūtrinnan`
  "out of caution" for want of the clause. *A device the lexicon uses six
  times and the rule does not name forces the translator to doubt what the
  kit is doing before his eyes.*
**Diminutive -ikīn** (neuter) productive: hōdikīn "little hood",
thingikīn "small object".
⚠⚠ **ARBITRATION BETWEEN A PRODUCTIVE SUFFIX AND THE STANDARD**
(2026-08-10): a suffix declared productive says what is done **when the
standard is silent**. **When the standard attests ANOTHER formation for
THAT lemma, the standard wins** — it is the same clause as for *suga*: a
different formation IS its answer. Whence **blīthskepi** "joy" (rank (a)
MDu *blijschap*, like the recorded *friundskepi, bodoskepi*) and not
†*blīthitha*, regular but contradicted; and **rīdāri** "horseman" (rank
(a) MDu *ridere*) despite the entry for *stiurio* reserving -āri to the
trade. At the 11th test of independence both translators wrote *blīthitha*
following the general rule, and one named the contradiction: "rank (a)
does not say †blijte, it says blijschap". He was right, and two of my
rules were contradicting each other.
⚠⚠ **DEVERBAL NOUN: -unga OR ga- + ROOT?** (2026-08-11) Frenkisk has both,
and the rule has been **in the dictionary all along**, without exception
across eleven lemmas:
· ***-unga*** (feminine) names the **PROCESS**, the operation in the doing
  or as a regulated procedure — *lathunga* "the summons", *dailunga* "the
  partition as a regulated operation", *wahsunga*, *hrōrunga*, *mōtunga*,
  *wārunga*;
⚠ **AND THE SUBSTANTIVISED INFINITIVE, A THIRD ROAD.** *that bigraban*
"the act of burying" costs no lemma and states the BARE ACT; the suffix
states the regulated operation, the one that has a rite and a duration.
For a burial it is **bigrabunga**: one does not say "after the act of
burying" but "after the funeral". **If the act has a SOCIAL form, the
suffix; if it is bare, the infinitive suffices.** The two translators of
the 21st test diverged exactly there, one forging, the other
substantivising, both citing a correct clause.
· ***ga-* + root** (neuter) names the **RESULT**, the thing produced —
  *gaskrif* "the writing", *gasprek* "the interview", *gabod* "the order
  given", *gabed* "the prayer", **gaswel** "the swelling".
Whence *gaswel* for what is treated, and *swellunga* for the swelling in
progress; *garotōd* for what is rotten, *rotunga* for the putrefaction in
progress. The two translators of the 20th test diverged exactly there,
each of them right for a different sense.
⚠⚠ **-itha OR -haid? THE CRITERION** (2026-08-11): Frenkisk has TWO
suffixes of deadjectival abstract, and they divide the ground between them according to
the standard, without exception in the ten recorded lemmas —
**a Dutch form in *-te* calls for *-itha*** (*hoogte* → **hauhitha**,
*breedte* → **braiditha**, *diepte* → **diupitha**);
**a Dutch form in *-heid* calls for *-haid*** (*hardheid* → **hardhēd**,
*mildheid* → **mildhēd**, *schoonheid* → **skaunhaid**, *vrijheid* →
**frīhēd**, *waarheid* → **wārhaid**).
This criterion was not written down: BOTH translators of the 17th test
**deduced it from the dictionary, separately and identically**, and one
noted "this is an inference, not a written rule". Two independent readers
drawing the same rule from the same data is the best proof that it is
right: it is written now.
**Deadjectival abstract -itha** (feminine) **productive** — it is THE
suffix that makes a noun of quality on an adjective: *hauh → **hauhitha***
"height", *braid → **braiditha*** "breadth", *deup → **diupitha***
"depth" (⚠ **L4**: the *-i-* of the suffix imposes *iu*), *māri →
māritha*, *faih → faihitha*, *haunian → haunitha* ⚠ (and not †*haun*, which is the NEUTER "hen": the cross-reference was dead). ⚠ The dictionary also
carries three abstracts in **-i** (*wraithi* "anger", *stolti* "pride",
*gasundi* "health"): these are **lexicalised survivals, kept without being
imitated**. Settled 2026-08-09 at the 5th test of independence, where the
two translators forged "breadth" on the two patterns, each citing internal
evidence equally true — the lexicon did not say which was alive. Rank (a)
says it: Dutch still forms *hoogte, breedte, diepte, lengte, sterkte,
warmte* on \*-iþō, and the type in -ī is fossil there.

⚠ **AN EXAMPLE OF A PRODUCTIVE RULE IS NOT A LEMMA — UNLESS IT NAMES AN
EVERYDAY THING.** The forms cited below to show that a suffix is alive
(*hornag*, *hason*, *thingikīn*…) are FORMABLE: the reader does not need an
entry to make them again. But those that name a thing one speaks of every
day do have one, failing which the reader looks for them, does not find
them, and asterisks them as forgeries. That happened at the 17th test
(2026-08-11) with ***lairāri***, cited here as an example and absent from
the dictionary — **the fifth occurrence of the invisible type-word**, after
*frenkisk*, *dag*, *tunga* and *geba*, and the first in this shape: no
longer the head of a paradigm, but **the example of a rule**.
**Adjectives in -īg/-ag** productive (mahtīg, skuldīg, hornag "horned",
blādrig). **Adverbs in -o** productive on any adjective (ferro, lango,
sāfto, wīdo, endilōso "infinitely") — promulgated 2026-08-04.
**Adjectives of material in -īn** productive on any noun of material:
guldīn "of gold", silubrīn "of silver", purpurīn "of purple", wullīn "of
wool", līnīn "of linen", holtīn "of wood", īsarnīn "of iron". They decline
like any adjective — strong without a determiner, **weak according to
REFERENCE** — see the register of the ADJECTIVE, the only place where the
rule is written. ⚠⚠ **This passage carried "weak after the article" until 2026-08-20**, that is, the **revoked** rule, in a section the entry table does not flag. Reported by translator J of the 42nd round, **on the very day the register proclaimed that "there is now only one place where the rule is written"**: *"it is the defect the kit congratulates itself on having eliminated, two hours after eliminating it"*;
"declined strong" in the first drafting meant "of the vocalic type", not
"never weak" (clarified, R36). Promulgated 2026-08-07 (requisition R21) —
announced once without being carried out: the ruling had been written in
the prose of the lexicon, which does not reach the kit.
**markstain** "boundary stone", and not †markastēn. The linking `-es-`
holds for strong stems in `-a` and for personal names; an `-ō` stem
takes no linking vowel at all.
The two translators of the 10th test wrote *markstain* and both reported it
as contrary to the letter of the rule: they were following the standard
(MDu *marcsteen*), and they were right.
With a genitive joint **Johanasdag**, formed exactly like the days of the
week (*Wōdanasdag*, *Thunarasdag*) and like *Hrōdashēm*. Prefer the
compound when the feast serves as a DATE MARKER (*air Johanasdagē* — ⚠ *the example carried †`air themu Johanesdage`: **three faults in eight words** — the joint in `-es-`, the dative in `-e`, a determiner after `air`. Corrected at the 49th round*
"before St John's day"), the periphrasis when one is speaking of the saint
himself (*te themu wīhon Hlōdowalde*). The two translators of the 8th test
each took one of the two turns, and neither was wrong.
⚠⚠ **THE SCRIBAL TABLE, AND ITS INVERSION** (carried into the kit
2026-08-13 — it lived in `phonologie.md` §4 and had NEVER reached the
portable file, so that two translators had to infer it from a single
example). **Measured on 102 ORIGINAL deeds earlier than 800** (deposit 13,
TELMA) — how the Latin scribe writes Frenkisk:
· the **macrons drop** (the scribe does not write length);
· **hl-/hr-/hn- → chl-/chr-/chn-** (Chlodovius, Chrotcharius) — in the
  majority, not exclusive: Hl- and Hr- survive;
· **initial h- → ch-** (Chilpericus);
· **initial w- → Vu-** (Vulfoleudus, Vualdramnus) — ⚠ **never gu-**: zero
  occurrences in 321,000 characters;
· **medial w → v, and often NOTHING between vowels** (-owald → -oaldus);
· **k → c, sk → sc**; **syllable-final h → ch**;
· **-rht → -rcth** (Dagobercthus 633, 64 occurrences against zero -rcht).
⚠ **TO RESTORE A NAME TRANSMITTED IN LATIN SPELLING, ONE INVERTS THIS
TABLE — NO MORE, NO LESS.** It is a rule, not a case-by-case matter:
*Clodoaldus* → **Hlōdowald** (ch- restored, medial w restored, length
restored); *Chramnulfus* → **Hramnulf**; *Ansbercht* → **Ansberht**;
*Bertrada* → **Berhtrada**; *Frodobertus* → **Frōdoberht**; *Gundoinus* →
**Gundowin**. ⚠⚠ **AND THE RECORDED ELEMENT OVERRIDES THE SCRIBE'S LETTER.** When the
inversion table does not decide — because the scribe writes two spellings
for one sound — it is **the form of the onomastic element that decides**.
*Chlodo-* and *Chlotho-* write the SAME element, and the corpus proves it:
**Micro-tables**: syncope of the unstressed vowel of the POLYSYLLABIC stem
in -al/-an/-il **and -ar** before a vocalic ending — ⚠⚠ **and before a DESINENCE only, never before a derivational SUFFIX**: `īdal` gives `īdla` in the accusative plural, but `īdalōn` "to empty" keeps its `-a-`. *The contrast `īdla` ~ `īdalōda` is not a contradiction: the inflected form syncopates, the derived one does not* (nagal → pl. nagla;
spēgal → **gen.** spēglas; luttil → acc. luttlan; wagan → dat. wagnē;
mahal → dat. mahlē; **akkar → pl. akkra; watar → dat. watrē**), including
in the second member of a compound (raubofogal → gen. pl. raubofoglo).
⚠ **AND ON THE SUBSTANTIVISED ADJECTIVE THE TWO OPERATIONS COMBINE**:
syncope of the stem AND an ADJECTIVE ending, not a noun one — *undar twēm
**ublēm*** "between two evils" (from *ubil*, strong dat. pl. in -ēm like
*gōdēm*). At the 9th test of independence (2026-08-09) one wrote *ubilēm*
(right ending, syncope forgotten), the other *ublum* (syncope done, noun
ending): **each held one half, neither had the form**. It is the sign that
a correct rule without a worked example is transmitted only by halves.

⚠⚠ **RULE RECAST 2026-08-18 — AND IT WAS AN ERROR OF MECHANISM, NOT OF
BOUND.** The two enquirers of the double enquiry on `aran` say so
separately, at **90 %** each, and in the same terms:

> **My "syncope rule" was the rule of ANAPTYXIS stated backwards** — and
> stating it backwards made it applicable to vowels that were never
> inserted.

⚠ **The clue that betrayed the origin, and that I had before my eyes**: my
exception clause bore on the **composition of the consonant cluster**
("except after two distinct consonants"). That is the signature of an
anaptyxis rule. *A genuine syncope is never conditioned by the nature of
the cluster preceding it: it is conditioned by the WEIGHT of the root
syllable.*

**THE THREE RULES THAT REPLACE THE OLD ONE**

> **R-0 — THE GOTHIC CONTROL TEST, prior to the other two.** A medial vowel
> is ETYMOLOGICAL if Gothic (failing that, Old Norse) shows it; it is
> **ANAPTYCTIC** if Gothic shows a bare consonant cluster. ⚠⚠ **Gothic
> does not know West Germanic anaptyxis** — it writes `maþl`, `fugls`,
> `akrs`, `tagl`. So a vowel it carries is inherited.
> · `*maþlą`: Goth. **`maþl`** → the `-a-` of `mahal` is **anaptyctic**
> · `*asaniz`: Goth. **`asans`**, gen. `asanais` → the `-a-` of `aran` is
>   **ETYMOLOGICAL**
>
> **R-a — ANAPTYXIS (insertion).** A supporting vowel is inserted in a
> cluster of obstruent + sonorant **in absolute final position or before a
> consonant**, and there only. Before a vocalic ending the conditioning
> disappears: **it does not drop, it never appears.**
> → `mahal` ~ `mahlē` · `fogal` ~ `fogles` · `akkar` ~ `akkrē`
>
> **R-b — SYNCOPE (deletion).** An unstressed **etymological** medial
> vowel is deleted before a vocalic ending **if and only if the preceding
> syllable is HEAVY**.
> → `haubid` ~ `hōbdes` (heavy, deletes) · `unker` ~ `unkres` (heavy,
>   deletes) · **`aran` ~ `aranē`** (LIGHT, keeps)
>
> **R-c — OUTPUT FILTER.** Neither R-a nor R-b may produce an illicit
> cluster. ⚠ **It is my old clause "two distinct consonants", demoted from
> master rule to safety catch** — which is what it always was.

⚠⚠ **THE MINIMAL PAIR THAT PROVES THE DISTINCTION IS OBLIGATORY**, given
by C1 in the rank (b) witness:

| form | origin of the `-a-` | before a vocalic ending |
|---|---|---|
| `tekan` "sign" < `*taikn` | **anaptyctic** | `teknes` — vowel **absent** |
| `aran` "harvest" < `*asaniz` | **etymological** | `aranē` — vowel **present** |

**Two words identical on the surface, opposite behaviours.** No
phonotactic rule — weight, number of consonants, syllable structure — can
tell them apart, since they are identical. *A rule that does not know the
ORIGIN of the vowel cannot be correct.*

⚠ **Two secondary tests, readable without etymology**: the **quality** of
an anaptyctic vowel **floats** from one manuscript to another
(`fogal`/`fogel`/`fugal`, OS `burg`/`burug`) whereas an etymological vowel
is stable; and an anaptyctic vowel **does not count as a syllable** in the
alliterative line — `burug` scans as a monosyllable. *The speakers
themselves did not treat the two objects alike.*

⚠⚠ **AND A WARNING ABOUT DATE THAT I MUST CARRY.** C2 points out that the
syncope of **etymological** medial vowels is a LATE fact — 10th–11th
century in High German — and that **`/a/` is the most resistant vowel of
all**: syncope begins with `i` and `u`. In 550, the Frankish onomastics of
the Latin sources show unstressed vowels **still full**: *Chlodovechus*,
*Childericus*, *Ragnacharius*. **R-b is therefore, at that date, at best
nascent** — a third road to `aranē`.

**BOUND** (2026-08-09, kept as R-c): syncope applies **only if the root
ends in ONE consonant** — or a geminate, which counts as one. It is
**BLOCKED after a cluster of two distinct consonants**, which would produce
three: **morgan → morganē** (and not †morgne), **sestar → sestarē** (and
not †*sestra*, a faulty example in the first drafting) — ⚠ the plural is therefore **sestara**, syncope blocked, as in the example *sehs sestara alothes*. ⚠⚠ This counter-example had been corrupted on 2026-08-14 by a **GLOBAL sweep** that rewrote †*sestra* as *sestara* even inside the bound that forbids it: the rule then rejected the form it prescribes. **A sweep without context corrupts the rules first, because rules CITE the faulty forms.**.
The two translators of the second test wrote *morgne* out of obedience and
both put it at the head of their doubts.
**And it applies whatever the consonant**, including a *b*: *oban* "oven" →
dat. **obnē**. The two translators of the 4th test wrote *obne* and both
gave it as doubtful, fearing that L10 might touch it: it does not — L10
settles the absolute final, not a *b* before a consonant.
Worked examples: *mit themu **wagnē*** "with the wagon", *an themu
**mahlē*** "at the moot", *fon themu **watrē*** "of the water", *sehs
**akkra*** "six fields" — but *an themu **morganē***, the cluster *-rg-*
blocking.

⚠ **This example is MORPHOLOGICAL: it shows syncope blocked, it prescribes
no construction.** *an themu morganē* is the INDEFINITE moment ("of a
morning"); the DETERMINED morning — that of the day just spoken of — stays
in the accusative with no preposition, ***thena morgan***, by the rule of the three uses
of time. The two translators of the 29th round diverged exactly there,
**one citing the rule, the other the example**; the gloss "in the morning"
that stood here was the cause, and it is withdrawn.
OUT OF SCOPE: monosyllables, where the vowel belongs to the root (dal, sal,
stal, fal → dalē, salas, nitharfalla); the **strong past participles in
-an** (gaslaganan, gabrokanan, gafanganon, farloranon — never
†gaslagnan); the substantivised infinitives (wesan → dat. wesanē). Drafting
tightened 2026-08-04: the wide version captured the whole past participle
of the corpus;
pres. subj. of the -ian verbs: -ie (haurie), the contraction -e admitted
after a dental cluster (festian → feste).
⚠ Syncope applies ONLY before a genuinely vocalic ending. The plural of a
neuter a-stem being identical to the singular, there is nothing to
syncopate before: **aimbar → pl. aimbar** (like word, jār), and not
†ēmbra.
**Joint of composition**: after a weak stem, the linking vowel -on-/-un-
(māno → mānondag; sunna → sunnundag; hnakko → hnakkonstad). After a STRONG
stem, and notably on a **personal name**, the joint is the **genitive of
the first member**: Wōdan → Wōdan*es*dag, Thunar → Thunar*es*dag, and
likewise Hrōd*es*haim. ⚠⚠ **THIS CLAUSE SAID "NEVER †-as-", AND IT IS ABROGATED
ON 2026-08-16, AT THE 31st ROUND** — found by a translator, not by a
control. It asserted that "the genitive of the -a stems has been -es since
550, the -as of Bergakker being the state of 425". **The double enquiry on
final syllables overturns it, and by an argument that owes nothing to
Bergakker**: the i-umlaut of *æ* gives *e* in Old English, and the genitive
is *dæges* and not †*deges*, the OHG *tages* and not †*teges* — so the
ending contained no front vowel when the umlaut operated. **The `-es` is
the LATER weakening, not the state of 550.**
· ⚠ **The kit therefore invoked Bergakker AGAINST `-as`; the enquirers
invoke it FOR.** *A disputed inscription can serve both parties: that is
the sign that it proves neither, and that the argument must come from
elsewhere.*
· **The joint is therefore `-as-`**: Hrōd → Hrōd*as*haim.
· ⚠ **But the ATTESTED place names carry `-es-`** — and that is coherent:
they come to us from charters of the 7th–9th century, hence **later than
the weakening**. The attested spelling is that of the documentation, not
that of 550. *We write the reconstructed form and we know why the charters
carry another.*
**obaro***, *nithar → **nitharo*** — and the recorded **betaro** proves it,
otherwise the lexicon would carry †*betro*. The micro-table of syncope aims
only at the nominal STEMS in -al/-an/-ar, not at the comparative suffix.
⚠ **DO NOT CONFUSE THE ADVERB AND THE ADJECTIVE** in the suppletive
degrees: *gōd* and *ubil* have an ADVERB (**bet**, **wirs** — *hē dōt it
bet* "he does it better") AND an ADJECTIVE (**betaro**, **wirsiro** — *ain
betaro man*, *thiu wirsira saka*). The adverb cannot be an epithet, the
adjective cannot modify a verb; and for "to see worse", a noun, it is
something else again — *mair ubiles*, partitive genitive. Three uses, three
forms. The kit gave only the adverb, which left "neither worse nor better"
without a way out when the two words are epithets.
**L5 DOES NOT APPLY HERE**: the *-i-* of the degree umlauts nothing — *lang
→ **langiro**, **langista***, *hard → **hardiro***, and not †lengiro. The
same levelling as in R33; the question was put at the 5th test
(2026-08-09) by a translator who had got it right without knowing why.
⚠⚠ **THE COMPARATIVE HAS NO STRONG INFLECTION, AND IN PREDICATE POSITION
IT STAYS BARE** (ruling of 2026-08-14). The two forced grids showed that it
otherwise had **no licit form at all**: the agreement rule imposes the
STRONG form in the predicate (*thia wāga wārun sō hauha*), and the
comparative is declared always WEAK. **The kit already carried the answer
in its own examples** — *thrim stapon **braidiro***, *grimmiro than…*: a
BARE, uninflected form. **As an epithet it is weak; as a predicate it is bare.** There is no
strong series †*langiran/langires/langiremu*.

⚠⚠ **THE SUPERLATIVE, FOR ITS PART, HAS A STRONG SERIES — and it is
exactly the predicative cell** (double enquiry at rank (a), 2026-08-15).
Holthausen §350 b: only the **nominative singular of the three genders**
and the **neuter accusative singular** admit the strong inflection of the
superlative; §352 c adds that it is used "with the genitive plural of a
noun". Braune §264 Anm. 1 states it as a West Germanic law. **Its mark is
ZERO**:
· **as a predicate: the BARE stem, without an article**, often with a
  **partitive genitive plural**: *these berg is **allero bergō hauhist*** "this
  mountain is the highest of all" — of the type *allaro cuningo
  **kraftigost*** (Heliand 973, after the copula *bist*), *allaro thingō
  **egislīcost*** (2613);
· **after a determiner: WEAK**: *an themu **hauhiston** himilē* (type
  Heliand 418);
· ⚠ **and not †hauhista**: the *-a* of the psalter's superlatives
  (`hōista`, `afrista`) is the citation form used for an oblique case —
  the glossator puts a nominative where the Latin called for a
  dative.
· ⚠⚠ **IN THE PREDICATIVE PLURAL, the bare stem holds as well** — *thesa
  berga sint allero hauhist* "those mountains are the highest of all". The
  strong series of the superlative is **defective**, not inflected: it has
  only one form, and that form serves for singular as for plural, just as
  the predicative comparative (*braidiro*) is invariable in number. **The
  two grids of 2026-08-15 noted that the previous day's ruling had closed
  the hole only in the SINGULAR**: Holthausen's bound §350 b describes the
  cells where the superlative *inflects* strong, it does not say that the
  bare stem is forbidden elsewhere — and the type *allaro barno bezt* has
  precisely no inflection to carry.
⚠ **The conflict the kit thought it had did not exist.** The two forced
grids were right to report the empty cell, but the cause they assigned it
— "there is no strong series of the superlative" — was false. Rank (a)
already carries one: ***furist*** "princeps", a bare nominative.


### Onomastics

**Borrowed proper names**: a-stems by default (pl. Silena, dat. pl.
Silenum, Harpium, Satirum).
**Hagionyms and saints' feasts** (2026-08-09): the saint's name is a
borrowed proper name (a-stem: *Johan*, gen. *Johanes*, dat. *Johanne*).
Two turns, both licit: **thes wīhon Johannes dag** or the compound
⚠ **WHICH MEMBER GOES IN FRONT: THE NOUN, NOT THE VERBAL STEM**
(2026-08-11). When both a noun AND a verb of the same root are recorded,
the compound is built on the **NOUN**: *laira* + *kneht* → **lairkneht**
"apprentice", not †*lernkneht* on *lernōn*; *blāsan* has no noun, whence
**blāsbalg** on the verbal stem, for want of better. The reason: nominal
composition takes NOUNS, and rank (a) confirms it lemma by lemma
(*leerknecht* on *leer*, not on *leren*). The two translators of the 17th
test both composed — they agreed on the device — and diverged on the head
member, for want of this rule.
⚠ **ZERO JOINT after a first member in -ō**: *marka* + *stain* →
**Chlod- 492 occurrences, Chloth- 404** across the nine Merovingian
volumes, almost even — the scribe's letter says nothing. The recorded
element is **hlōd-** "fame", so *Chlothacharius* → **Hlōdohari**, not
†*Hlōthahari*. At the 24th test of independence (2026-08-13) both
translators wrote *Hlōthahari*, inverting the table to the letter, and
both put this point at the head of their doubts: they were right to doubt,
and the rule was missing. **Order of operations: (1) the recorded element,
if there is one; (2) failing that, the inversion of the table; (3) never
the common lemma (R52).**
The restoration touches the SPELLING and never the lemma: R52 goes on
protecting *Wulf-* against *wolf*, *-ramn* against *hraban*, *Rad-*
against *rād*. **Latin spelling: we render it. Onomastic element: we keep
it.**
**Germanic masculine personal names in -hari**: the second member inflects
like the ja-stem *heri* — dat. **Gundharē**, **Baldharē** (on *beddi →
beddē*, *rīki → rīkē*), and not †-harie nor †-harī. The two translators of
the 4th test wrote *Gundhare* and both gave it as "the most fragile point
of the translation, and it is at the head of the greeting". It is so no
longer.
**Germanic feminine personal names**: the nominative keeps the transmitted
onomastic form, apocopated (**Adalhild**, **Hrōdhild**), and the obliques
follow the ō-stem of the second member — gen. and acc. **Adalhilda**, dat.
**Adalhildu**. The rule was missing on 2026-08-09 and the two translators
reconstituted it identically while declaring it absent; it is now written.
**Phonology of the onomastic compounds** (requisition R52): a personal name
is a FROZEN compound, transmitted for three generations by the time it is
written; its members **are not bound** to reflect the current state of the
corresponding lemma. Whence Wulf- beside the lemma wolf, -ramn beside
hraban, Rat- beside rād, Amal- with no lemma at all. It is not a fault and
the linter must not count it as one: the elements of names go to their own
table, never to the lexicon.


### The prepositions, the governments and the complements

**Government of the prepositions that had stayed mute** (requisition R8):
⚠⚠ ***in* — the one preposition R8 FORGOT** (reported by the two forced
grids, 2026-08-13): **+dat** at rest, **+acc** in motion, by the general
rule of the prepositions of place — **+dat at rest, +acc in motion** — which the kit states here for the first time, ⚠ **for it was invoked without being written**; the kit wrote it "in — in", with no
government, although R8 was promulgated to fill exactly that hole and dealt
with *innan*, its compound. Its two internal uses confirm it: *in themu
middē* (dative), *in hertan haldan* (accusative of direction, the heart
being that towards which one carries).
**umbi (+acc)** "around" (umbi thena gard) — figuratively "concerning";
**innan (+dat)** "within" (innan themu tūne), **(+acc)** if one enters it;
**ūt (+dat)** "out of" (ūt themu spīkare); **oba (+dat)** "above",
**(+acc)** in motion. General rule: the place where one is → dative, the
place where one is going → accusative.
**SECOND TEST OF INDEPENDENCE** (2026-08-09) — two fresh translators
converged at **89 %** word for word on a narrative, and stumbled at the
same nine points. Here they are, settled.
**Plural of *man***: nom./acc. **man** (INVARIABLE, as in Old Saxon), gen.
**mannō**, dat. **mannum**. The analogy fōt/fōti + L5 would give †menni: it
does not apply, *man* being a root stem with a geminate. The two
translators wrote *twēne man* against the analogy and for the standard —
they were right.
**Accusative of time**: duration and the moment are marked by the **bare
accusative**, without a preposition — *thena āband* "that evening", *thrī
dagā* "for three days", *thia naht* "that night". There is no
distance-marking demonstrative: the article alone suffices, or **air
(+dat), a preposition** "before" — ⚠⚠ **AND THE NOUN STAYS BARE**, ruling
of 2026-08-21: ***air dagē*** "before day", ***air dōmas dagē*** "before
the day of judgement". ⚠ *Frenkisk letter: rank (b) carries the reduced
endings of 830; at 550, `-as` in the genitive and `-ē` in the dative.* The
two enquirers note separately that in the *Heliand* **`air` is NEVER
followed by a determiner before a noun of time**. **The warrants: ll. 4333,
4694, 4999** — three complements of time after `air`, **all with a bare
noun**. ⚠ *The Old Saxon forms are not copied out here: the kit is
distributed on its own, and a letter of 830 set before a translator's eyes
gets copied.* ⚠ †*air theru midnaht* is withdrawn: the determiner is
faulty **and** the lexeme `midnaht` is attested at no rank.
   ~~*air themu
dagē*. To be distinguished from the conjunction *air than* (+subj.). Its
absence forced the two translators to recast two sentences as clauses: the
sense came through, the concision did not.
**Two relatives in one clause**: the second one **extraposes**, in every
register, law included — *that ain saiw, the hrōpid, nihain saiw ni sī, the
slahid*. R28 keeps the relative before the verb; stacked, two relatives
make the sentence unreadable, and Germanic usage throws the second one out.
**lātan faran (+acc)** "to let go, to release" — the idiom of letting go of
a hold, not to be rendered by *farlātan*, which is to leave a place: *ni lāt
thīn rōthar faran*.
**SYNTAX OF RELATIONS** — promulgated 2026-08-07 after the test of
independence. Two outside translators stumbled at the same points, and
their common diagnosis was that the kit covered the words and not the
**relations between the words**. Here they are.
**Predicative possession — "to belong to, to fall to"**: there is no verb;
one uses the **predicative genitive** with *wesan* — thiu **halbu** **ist
thes mannes** "the half is the man's / falls to him", that fehu **ist thes
abbatas**. It is the common Germanic turn and it is licit: the kit attests
the adnominal possessive genitive (Godes sunu), and the predicate is only
its predicative position.
**Distributive "per"**: **bī (+dat)** — twēne penningā **bī themu mudde**
"two deniers a modius", ain penning **bī themu fōthre**". The turn *fon
gahwilīkemu mudde*, which the two translators built for want of a rule, is
intelligible but heavy, and *gahwilīk* remains a PRONOUN with a partitive
genitive (thero gahwilīk), not an epithet.
**Verbs of teaching: DOUBLE ACCUSATIVE** — *lairian* takes THE PERSON and
THE THING in the accusative: *hē lairit **thena kneht** thia laira*. The
two translators of the 5th test diverged for want of a written government,
one putting the person in the dative.

**Dat. sg. of *man***: **manne** (*te themu aldon manne*) — the kit gave
only the gen. pl. *mannō* and the dat. pl. *mannum*, and the two
translators of the 12th test had to infer it. The root stem is invariable
in the NOM.-ACC. PLURAL only, not in the singular.
**Plural of *naht***: **INVARIABLE** — *sibun dagā endi sibun **naht***. It
is a root stem, like *man*: the table of the i-stems would give †*nehti*,
and it does not apply here (the same reason as for *man*, and the
dictionary already flags the dat. sg. with a zero ending).
**Genitive plural of *man***: **mannō** (root stem, like fōt/fōti but with
an etymological geminate \*mann-) — nihain **mannō** sīnero.
**Dative of disadvantage** (requisition R48): verbs of dispossession, of
withdrawal and of injury construe a dative of the injured person, without a
preposition — the man **nam mī** twē muddi kornas "the man took two modii
from me"; likewise `biniman`, `stelan`, `brekan`. ⚠ **This line wrote `beniman` until 2026-08-30**, a spelling absent from the lexicon: the ruling named a verb no tool could look up, and the entry for `biniman` prescribed the opposite government for six days without anything going red. The counterpart of the dative
of the experiencer (mī thunkid, mik lustid).

## VERNER'S ALTERNATION, AND THE REDUPLICATION OF CLASS VII

Double enquiry of 2026-08-16, opened by the dating audit.

### The alternation is ALIVE at 550, and the argument is unanswerable

Confidence **0.95** and **0.97** — it is the best established point in the
whole verbal system.

> **Modern Dutch still carries it: *verliezen / verloor / verloren*,
> *kiezen / koos / gekozen*, *vriezen / gevroren*, *snijden / gesneden*.**
> The direct descendant of Frankish keeps the alternation **fourteen
> centuries later**. There is no scenario in which it would already be
> levelled in 550 only to be re-regularised afterwards.

⚠ **Gothic is of no use here**: it has **levelled** the alternation in the
strong verb (*tiuhan/táuh/táuhum/táuhans*), keeping it only in the
preterite-presents. It is an isolated innovator, not a witness to the
continental state.

⚠⚠ **AND IT IS MORPHOLOGISED, NOT PHONOLOGICAL.** The Indo-European accent
conditioning disappeared centuries earlier: at 550 it is a fact of
**lexical morphology**, stored verb by verb.
> **Therefore: it is productive NOWHERE. We keep it where it is inherited;
> we NEVER invent it by analogy.** The default is the alternation, and each
> levelling is declared individually.

### The five pairs of alternants

| voiceless | voiced (Verner) | in Frenkisk |
|---|---|---|
| `*f` | `*b` | **f ~ b** — *heffian / hōf / hōbum / gahaban* |
| `*þ` | `*ð` | **th ~ d** — *werthan / wurdum / gawordan* |
| `*h` | `*g` | **h ~ g** — *teuhan / tugum / gatogan* |
| `*hʷ` | `*gʷ` | **h ~ w** — *līhan / liwum / galiwan* |
| `*s` | `*z` | **s ~ r** — *keusan / kurum / gakoran* |

### ⚠⚠ THE TRANSVERSAL DIAGNOSIS — it is worth more than the list of faults

> **The kit applied Verner faultlessly everywhere the alternant is the
> plain VOICED counterpart of the same consonant — `h ~ g`, `s ~ r`. It
> failed everywhere the alternant CHANGES PLACE OR MANNER: `þ ~ d` and `hʷ
> ~ w`.**

*It was a defect of PROPAGATION, not of doctrine* — the proof being that the
kit already applied the hard rule `*hʷ → w` correctly to *līhan*.

**The audit commissioned on that diagnosis found exactly two verbs**, and
all the other stems in `*þ` were right (*quethan*, *werthan*):

| | the kit wrote | corrected |
|---|---|---|
| `snīthan` | †*snithum*, †*gasnithan* | **`snidum`**, **`gasnidan`** |
| `līthan` | †*lithum*, †*galithan* | **`lidum`**, **`galidan`** |

⚠ *snīthan* carries **the same alternation** as *werthan* and *quethan*,
which the kit was treating correctly. **It was not an error of knowledge, it
was an internal inconsistency** — and that is why an audit was worth more
than two piecemeal corrections. ⚠ **`līthan` had even been declared a
"gap" to the enquirers: it existed, and it was wrong.** *One does not know
one's own lexicon.*


### `sehan` — corrected, with a declared divergence

**Both enquirers condemn the participle †*gasehan***: it is the **Old High
German** form, in which the *h* of the present has been restored by
analogy. The kit was importing, without knowing it, the 10th-century High
German state into a 6th-century Low Franconian system.
> **`sehan / sah / sāwun / gasewan`**

⚠⚠ **BUT THEY DIVERGE ON THE PRETERITE PLURAL, AND THE DIVERGENCE IS
INSTRUCTIVE.**
· The **second** gives **`sāwun`** [attested]: OS *sāwun*, OE *sāwon*, and
OHG still carries *gisëwan* beside *gisëhan*. A simple rule: **`*hʷ → w`
everywhere**.
· The **first** gives **`sāgum`** [reconstructed] by a finer rule: `*ǥʷ`
gives **g before u** (preterite plural) and **w before a** (participle),
whence the parallel `sāgum : gasewan :: ligum : galiwan` — and he then
corrects *liwum* to †*ligum*.

> **We follow the SECOND**: he has the attestation, the first has an
> internal reconstruction he himself classes **[R] and not [A]**, admitting
> that *"no attested language keeps the four forms intact"*. And the second
> **leaves `liwum` untouched**, which the kit already carried. *Between an
> elegant rule and a witness, we take the witness.*

⚠ The first enquirer's rule stays **written here** because it may be right:
each language levelled, but **in a different direction**, which guarantees
the antiquity of the unlevelled state and at the same time deprives us of a
direct witness.

### ⚠⚠ THREE VERBS WERE MISFILED — and the label is more dangerous than the form

**`fleuhan`, `skehan`, `bifelhan` have NEVER alternated** in continental
Germanic. Their forms were right; it was the rubric that was false.
> *"A reader who applies the rubric by analogy will produce monsters."* An
> error of labelling costs nothing so long as the lexicon is the source of
> truth — **it becomes toxic the moment the rubric is read as a generative
> rule.**

⚠⚠ **AND `fleuhan` / `bifelhan` ARE AN EMPIRICAL REFUTATION OF THE
INSTRUMENT ABROGATED THE DAY BEFORE.** Old English has **g** — *flugon /
flogen*, *fulgon / folgen* — against **h** in OHG, in OS and in Gothic.
> **Had the "check by Old English" been applied, one would have introduced
> †*flugum*, †*gaflogan*, †*bifulgum*, †*bifolgan* into a continental
> system, AGAINST the agreement of three witnesses.** Golden rule no. 18
> receives its experimental verification here.
· ⚠ And the second adds the key: the two forms in *g* in Old English each
have **an obvious and immediate contaminator** — `flogen` beside
`flēogan` "to fly", `folgen` beside `folgian` "to follow".

### The CLOSED list of legitimate levellings at 550

We level nowhere else.
· **`fāhan / fēng` and `hāhan / hēng`** — the *-ng-* of the voiced alternant
is generalised to the whole preterite throughout **all** West Germanic,
against Gothic *faifāh*. A pan-West-Germanic levelling, earlier than any
attestation;
· **`hladan`** — *d* generalised to the **present** everywhere (OE *hladan*,
OHG *hladan*);
· **`skehan`** — *h* everywhere, and **it is not even a levelling**: there
never was an alternation;
· **`fleuhan`, `bifelhan`** — *h* generalised on the continent. ⚠ **A
declared convention**, confidences 0.78 and 0.58: the handbooks of
Proto-West-Germanic often reconstruct *g*. **We diverge knowingly.**
· **`slahan / slōg` and `thwahan / thwōg`** — ⚠⚠ **the two enquirers
recommend the OPPOSITE of each other.** The first wants `slōh`/`thwōh` (the
etymological form, OE *slōh*, 0.80); the second wants to **keep
`slōg`/`thwōg`** (the attested continental state — OS *slōg*, OHG *sluog*,
Du *sloeg* —, 0.55 on the date).
> **We keep `slōg` / `thwōg`, and we declare it as a convention** — not out
> of preference, but because **it is the areal criterion the FIRST enquirer
> invokes himself** for `fleuhan`: *"your area is continental, OHG and OS
> agree against OE alone"*. He did not apply it to `slahan`. **We apply his
> criterion against his conclusion.**

### The gaps filled

| | infinitive | pret. sg. | pret. pl. | participle |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| to choose | **keusan** | **kaus** | **kurum** | **gakoran** |
| to go, to pass | **līthan** | **laith** | **lidum** | **galidan** |

⚠ **`keusan` was empty, and it is the emblematic verb of Verner's
alternation.** Both enquirers give it identically (0.93 and 0.95).
⚠ **`wesan`** keeps `was / wārum` — *s ~ r*, the liveliest alternation in
Germanic, kept down to German *war / waren*.


⚠⚠ **AND THE SECOND ENQUIRER WAS WRONG ABOUT `heffian`, FOR WANT OF
KNOWING L10.** He recommended †*hōb* in the singular, "because the
continent keeps the *b*". The kit writes **`hōf / hōbum / gahaban`**: it is
`*ƀ → f` in absolute final position and `b` before a vowel, **exactly the
pattern `wīf / wības`**. His correction broke the law it invoked.
> *An enquirer writes in the language of his sources; the kit writes in its
> own — and this time the gap told in the kit's favour.*

⚠⚠ **AMENDMENT OF THE 35th ROUND: THE FORM WAS RIGHT, THE REASON WAS
WRONG.** Translator A pointed out that **L10 does not apply to `heffian`**,
and he cites the kit against itself: *"L10 aims only at the `f` from \*ƀ;
the `f` from \*p stays `f` EVERYWHERE"*. And **`*hafjaną` is cognate with
Lat. *capiō*** — its `f` comes from \*p, not from \*ƀ.

> **The alternation `hōf ~ hōbum` IS Verner's, not L10's.** And rank (a)
> confirms it in the present: Du **heffen**, with `ff`.
> **So, FOR `heffian` "to lift": imperative `hefi!` and 3rd sg. `hefid`**,
> not †`hebi` / †`hebid`.
  ⚠⚠ **THE NAME OF THE VERB WAS ADDED ON 2026-08-21, AND IT WAS MISSING —
  WHICH MADE THIS RULING DANGEROUS.** It forbids †`hebid` without saying
  which lemma it is speaking of. And **`hebid` is the CORRECT form of the
  3rd sg. of `hebbian` "to have"**, given in its entry (*hebbiu, hebis,
  hebid*). Translator P of the 45th round: *"two near-homographic verbs, a
  ruling that does not write which one it aims at, and a formal prohibition
  bearing on the correct form of its neighbour. **I was one click from
  writing †`hē thurst hefid`.**"*
  > **RULE: a prohibition that does not name its lemma forbids for all its
  > homographs.**

⚠ **BOTH were wrong, and not in the same way**: the enquirer wanted †`hōb`
in the singular, which is false; the kit refused it for a reason that is
false too. *Being right against a false argument does not make one's own
argument right — and it took a translator, three weeks later, to say so.*

### ⚠⚠ CLASS VII: THE AUDIT WAS WRONG, AND IT WAS WRONG INSTRUCTIVELY

**The contracted preterites are RIGHT at 550. We change nothing** (0.85 and
0.88). The contraction is **North-West Germanic**, common to ON, OE, OS and
OHG — hence well earlier than the split, hence well earlier than 550.
*"Writing †hehait would be a Gothicism laid over West Germanic."*

> ### ⚠⚠ AND THE KIT ALREADY CONTAINS REDUPLICATION: IT IS CALLED `fēng`, `hēng`, `geng`.
> That `-ng-` is not a nasal infix: it is the **residue of a reduplicating
> preterite**, contracted and gone opaque — `*fe-fanh-` → `*feanh` →
> **`fēng`**; `*he-hanh-` → **`hēng`**; `*ge-gang-` → **`geng`**;
> `*he-hait-` → **`hēt`**; `*le-lōt-` → **`lēt`**.
>
> **The auditor took a PHONOLOGICAL RESULT for a MORPHOLOGICAL ABSENCE.**
> *To say "no reduplicating preterite" without qualification is to confuse
> the absence of a morpheme with the absence of a history.*

⚠ **The reproach nonetheless keeps a real core**: *"a system at 550 with
strictly no residual irregularity in its class VII is a system too regular
to be a language."* It is a defect of **realism**, not of reconstruction —
but under probationary discipline, realism counts.

**We therefore licence ONE relic, and one only:**
> **`heht`**, preterite singular of *haitan*, a **variant of the formal
> register** beside `hēt`, which remains the ordinary form. Plural `hehtum`
> if it is used.
> ⚠ **Do NOT give it a complete paradigm**: it is a frozen lexical item, not
> a productive model. To invent a subjunctive †*hehti* for it is to invent
> more than one reconstructs.

⚠⚠ **AND ITS ATTESTATION IS DECLARED UNVERIFIED.** The first enquirer gives
*heht* as attested in the *Heliand*; **the second could not confirm it** and
warns: *"if the Old Saxon attestation falls, the relic has only an
Anglo-Frisian support left, and you should then set it aside."* **This form
is therefore CONDITIONAL, and the condition is written down.**
· Confidences: 0.70 and 0.50. It is the most fragile form in the kit.

**What is NOT imported**: `leort`, `reord`, `leolc`, `ondreord` are
**exclusively Anglo-Frisian** relics, two of which carry specifically
Anglo-Frisian consonant treatments. And above all **not** `lailōt`,
`saizlēp`, `staistáut`:
> *"To import them would be a GOTHICISM — that is, exactly the fault
> reproached to the check by Old English, but committed with Gothic. Gothic
> is the most divergent branch: it is precisely the one from which nothing
> is to be imported without a West Germanic witness."*

⚠ **The origin of ē² is NOT settled here, and that is deliberate.** One
school derives it from the contraction of the reduplication; another
(Jasanoff) makes of it an autonomous formation that competed with and then
ousted the reduplicating type. **The two hypotheses converge on the same
practical recommendation** — a contracted norm plus one marked relic. *The
kit does not decide, and writes that it does not decide: it is exactly the
case where a declared non-decision is sounder than an arbitrary
arbitration.*


### ⚠⚠ TWO FALSE ALARMS, AND THEY CAME FROM MY OWN BRIEF

The second enquirer reported, **outside his mandate**, two defects he said
were "more costly than your two questions together". **On checking, neither
of them exists.**

· **"Your system is inconsistent on `*ai`: you write `snaith` but
`haitan/haitan` in a hesitant doublet."** — The doublet **does not exist**:
the lexicon carries only `haitan`. **I had written it that way in the
brief.** And the eleven class I preterites are perfectly consistent:
*skraif, waip, bait, baid, bilaif, staig, snaith, hrain, hnaig, waik, laih*
— **eleven `ai` out of eleven**.
> ⚠⚠⚠ **THIS ANSWER WAS FALSE — THE ENQUIRER WAS RIGHT.** The preterite
> singular of class I does come from `*ai`; eleven mutually consistent `ē`
> were the same fault eleven times over. *Corrected 2026-08-23.* **A
> perfectly regular series can be regularly false: internal consistency is
> not a dating.**
· **"Your ending `-um` is the High German generalisation; Old Saxon has
`-un`."** — The kit has the plural **apportioned `-um / -ut / -un`**, which
is the **ternary Franconian plural** that the dating audit had precisely
confirmed as correct. The enquirer read my citation form as a single
ending.

> **A BADLY BRIEFED ENQUIRY MEASURES THE BRIEF.** It is the exact
> counterpart of golden rule no. 16: *a blind test conducted from the inside
> measures its own documentation.* Here an outside expert, competent and in
> good faith, produced two rigorous diagnoses **of a system I had described
> to him badly**. ⚠ **And he had no means of noticing, since the protocol
> forbids him to open the files.**
>
> *The prohibition that guarantees the enquirer's independence also forbids
> him to check what he is told. That is the price of the arrangement, and it
> has to be paid by writing exact briefs.*


## THE ABSTRACT — SIX MECHANISMS, AND THE SOURCE LANGUAGE HAS ONLY ONE

Double forced grid of 2026-08-16. **The two enquirers say the same thing and
say it against me**: I was looking for which WORDS were missing, and the
want was one of FORM.

> **"The source language NOMINALISES an experience; Germanic CONJUGATES a
> process on an oblique experiencer. Without this rubric, your translators
> will produce THE SOURCE LANGUAGE IN GERMANIC DRESS: sentences whose
> grammatical subject is the person, whereas the language of 550 put the
> affect in the subject and the person in an oblique case."**

### ⚠⚠ 1. THE IMPERSONAL VERB — that is where affect lodges

**The most underrated mechanism, and both enquirers put it first.** The
inner state is not named: it is conjugated, with the person in the
**accusative** or the **dative**.

| turn | sense | warrants |
|---|---|---|
| **mik hriuwid** | I regret | OHG *mih riuwit*, OE *mē hrēoweþ* |
| **mik urthriutit** | it wearies me | Goth. *usþriutan*, OHG *irdriozan*, OE *āþrēoteð* |
| **mi thunkid** | it seems to me | Goth. *þugkeiþ mis*, OE *mē þyncþ*, OS *thunkian* |
| **mik lustid** | I feel like | OE *mē lyst*, Goth. *lustōn* |
| **mik skamōd** | I am ashamed | OE *mē sceamað*, OHG *scamēn* |
| **mi is laith** / **leof** | it is hateful / dear to me | *laith* attested everywhere |
| **mi is wai** | I am wretched | recorded |
| **mi is naud** | I am in need | recorded |
| **mi galimpit** / **gatimit** | it suits me, it befits me | OHG *gilimpfan*, *gizeman*, OE *gerisan* |

⚠⚠ **`skamōn` — AN ARBITRATION, the two translators of the 32nd round
having stumbled on it.** The rubric gives the impersonal `mik skamōd`; the
dictionary gives `sik skamōn` + genitive, that is, **the person in the
nominative**, the structure the rubric declares un-Germanic. One wrote the
form the entry condemns; the other used both in two different texts,
*"having no arbiter. There must be one."*
> **BOTH ARE LICIT, AND THEY DO NOT SAY THE SAME THING.**
> · **`mik skamōd thes`** — impersonal: the shame COMES UPON one, it is
> undergone;
> · **`ik mik skamōn thes`** — personal reflexive: one feels shame and owns
> oneself its subject.
> **Old Germanic attests both turns for this verb**; it is the source
> language that has only one cell. ⚠ **In the gnomic register and for an
> affect that falls upon someone, the impersonal; for owned shame, the
> reflexive.**


> **RULE: every entry for an affect carries its GOVERNMENT SCHEMA as a
> property of the lemma.** A dictionary that gives only nouns makes people
> write the source language.

⚠ **That is the answer to "to look (like)" and "to pretend"**, which the
source language turns into nouns and Germanic conjugates.

### 2. THE COMPOUND IN `mōd-` — the main channel of the psychological abstract

**"Pride" is not a derivative, it is `ubarmōdi`** — OHG *ubarmuot*, OS
*obarmōd*, OE *ofermōd*, and Gothic says *hauhhairtei* "high-heartedness".
The device is the same.

**Available second members**: **`mōd`** (heart-courage-humour, recorded) ·
**`hugi`** (thought) · **`sebo`** (mind) · **`herta`** (recorded) ·
**`ferah`** (life, soul) · **`willio`** · **`sin`**.
· **`ubarmōdi`** (n.) pride, hubris · **`audmōdi`** (n.) humility — OS
*ōdmōdi*, OE *ēaðmēdu*, OHG *ōdmuotī*.

> **It is a PRODUCTIVE SCHEMA, not a list.** Every time a moral abstract is
> called for, **the first answer to try is not a suffix, it is a
> compound.**

### 3. THE COMPOUND WITH A CONCRETE FIRST MEMBER

*"Where the source language has a learned simplex abstract, Germanic
compounds two concrete words."*
· **`fehugīrī`** "cattle-greed" = avarice (Goth. *faihufrikei*);
· **`manslahta`** "man-slaying" = murder;
· **`elilendi`** "other-land" = exile AND misery (recorded);
· **`wergeld`** "man-payment" — **attested in Frankish Latin**.

### 4. ⚠⚠ THE NEGATION `un-` — THE device of moral abstraction

> **The source language opposes distinct roots — wisdom / folly, measure /
> excess. GERMANIC DERIVES THE VICE FROM THE VIRTUE BY NEGATION.**

*untreuwa* (recorded) · *unwiti* · *unmet* · *unēra* · *unreht* ·
*unmildi* · *unthank* · *unfrōdi*.

> **RULE: X / un-X are paired systematically.** ⚠ *Forging a new root for a
> vice is the reflex of the source-language speaker and the commonest
> typological misreading.*

### 5. THE PREFIX `ga-` IS A FORMER OF ABSTRACTS

*ga-thuld*, *ga-mund*, *ga-witi*, *ga-thank*, *ga-wald*, *ga-laubo*,
*ga-burd*, *ga-siuni*, *ga-bārida*. **A schema, not a list.**
⚠ *"If you do not have this prefix declared as productive on the
abstracts, you have a structural hole — graver than any of the ten
words."*

### 6. THE INHERITED DEVERBAL ABSTRACT IS IN `-t`, NOT IN `-unga`

⚠⚠ **`-unga` is the PRODUCTIVE suffix; the NATIVE stock is in `-t` / `-þ`**
(PGmc `*-tiz`, `*-þiz`) — and it is that stock which survived into Dutch:
*daad, macht, geboorte, komst, gunst, lust, dood, vaart, dracht, vlucht,
jacht, gift*.
· Recorded or carried: **maht, dād, tīd, lust, thurft, anst, gaburd, list,
gamund, gathuld, dugunth, urkundi**.
> **A language of 550 has a great deal more `*-þiz` than `*-ungō`.** The
> type in `-t` is **not productive**: it is a closed series, to be listed
> word by word.

### 7. THE SUFFIX `-nissi` WAS MISSING

The third great abstract suffix of West Germanic, and it was absent from
the inventory. Goth. **`-assus`** (*ibnassus*, *ufarassus*,
*þiudinassus*) · OE **`-nes`**, *the most productive suffix in all Old
English* · OHG and OS **`-nissi`** · Du **`-nis`** (*geheimenis, kennis,
erfenis*).
⚠ **RESTRICTED PRODUCTIVITY, AND DECLARED**: its western explosion is
**correlated with the Christian vocabulary of translation**. At 550, in a
barely Christianised Salian setting, its degree of productivity is
**undecidable**. We enter it; we do not exploit it beyond the
deadjectival. Status **[R]**.

### ⚠⚠ 8. THE "BLOCKED" STATUS, WHICH WAS MISSING

> *"The BLOCKED category is the one you most lack, and it is the one that
> will stop your translators from endlessly reforging what you have decided
> to set aside."*

**General clause: the absence of a regular derivative from the dictionary
does NOT mean it does not exist in the language; only a BLOCKED entry
forbids it.**


| blocked | why |
|---|---|
| †`lobunga` | **the primary noun `lof` occupies the cell.** An inherited primary abstract blocks the derivation |
| †`dolitha` | ⚠ **no Germanic language derives the abstract of "mad" from the adjective.** They all say "un-knowing": `unwiti`, `unfrōdi` |
| †`latitha` | ⚠ **the trap**: `-itha` on `lat` would give "slowness, sloth" (OHG *lazzī*), **not delay**. Delay is `frist`, the act of delaying is `lettian`. *Three notions, three forms* |
| †`farthreutandi` adj. | **"tedious" does not exist as an adjective** in old Germanic. One says `mik urthriutid`, or the recorded adjective `laith` + dative: *hē ist mi laith* |
| "specious" | a Latinism of 1650. Rendered by an **antithetical concrete juxtaposition** — *fagar ūtan, fūl innan* — or `laus skīn`. **A free phrase, never an entry** |
| "honourably" | a social ideal of the 17th century with no counterpart. Three turns according to the sense: `sōthlīko`, `rehto`, `mit treuwōn`. ⚠ **To be displayed as UNTRANSLATABLE** |
| "air" (bearing) | an idiom of the 16th–17th century. Noun: `gabāri`. Verb: impersonal `thunkid` |
| "to differ" (be unlike) | ⚠ **Germanic does not verbalise difference, it predicates it**: *sint ungilīk*, *is ander* |
| "duration" | ⚠⚠ **none of the four witnesses lexicalises it.** One says `lang` + a noun of time, or `aldar`. ⚠ **And one does NOT borrow *durare***: OHG *dūrēn* is much later and would carry us from the 6th century to the 9th |

### ⚠⚠ 9. THE RULING ON `-itha` IS REQUALIFIED AS A CONVENTION

**Both enquirers say that I inverted the state of 550**, and the second
makes it "the weightiest point in the report":

> **"You declared FOSSIL the suffix that was ALIVE, and PRODUCTIVE the one
> that was not yet."**

In Old High German — which is in part Franconian — **the type in `-ī` is
the dominant deadjectival and massively productive**: *hauhī, tiufī,
breitī, lengī, wārī, guotī, scōnī, reinī, frōdī*. Gothic does the same in
`-ei`. The reversal in favour of `-te` is a Middle Dutch fact, **six to eight
centuries after the cut**.

> **The ruling is NOT ABROGATED, it is REQUALIFIED.** It becomes a
> **declared convention [C]**: *"Frenkisk anticipates the Low Franconian
> generalisation of `-itha` at the expense of `-ī`. A downstream choice,
> owned, not reconstructed."*
· ⚠ **And the type in `-ī` ceases to be "fossil"**: *wraithi*, *stolti*,
*gasundi* are an **inherited layer still alive in restricted
distribution** — which is what they in fact were.
· ⚠ **The cost is stylistic and it is real**: *"your language will sound
like late Old Dutch everywhere the deadjectival abstract appears"*. We know
it and we own it.
· ⚠ Corollary: **`braiditha` is [C]** (OE *brǣdu*, OHG *breitī*), while
`diupitha` and `hauhitha` are **[A]** through Gothic.

### ⚠⚠ 10. THE BIAS THAT COMMANDS ALL THE REST

> **"Your four witness languages are languages of TRANSLATION. That bias
> inflates the abstract."** Wulfila's Gothic calques the Greek; the
> *Heliand*, the *Isidor*, the *Tatian* calque the Latin. Goth.
> *garaihtiþa* and Goth. *ajukdūþs* are **manufactured by a translator** for
> Mediterranean concepts.
>
> **A SALIAN FRANKISH OF 550 MUST BE POORER IN MORAL ABSTRACTS THAN GOTHIC
> AND THE HELIAND, BY CONSTRUCTION.** No Frankish Bible, no scriptorium.

**The only Frankish abstract known at FIRST HAND is that of LAW** — the
Malberg glosses of the *Pactus*, the Latin of the laws: *mallum*, *fredus*,
*bannus*, *reipus*, *leudis*, *mundeburdis*, *chrenecruda*.

> ⚠⚠ **"If your lexicon is rich in affects and poor in procedure, it is
> INVERTED with respect to everything the corpus allows us to know."**

**The centre of gravity of the abstract is juridical and social; the
psychological and moral zone must stay visibly thinner.** It is an
instruction about **proportion**, and it is worth more than any entry.

⚠ **And the same criticism reaches my MEASUREMENT**: I had tested coverage
against a grid of 64 notions **in the source language**. *"Measuring a
language of 550 against a list of abstract nouns from a modern language
guarantees that you will find gaps that are not gaps. The inverse control
is the only valid one."*


## Syntax — the ten norms N1–N10

⚠⚠ **N2 TO N8 WERE PHANTOMS UNTIL 2026-08-16.** The kit announced "norms
N1–N10" and named only **N9** and **N10** — which are in any case
LICENCES, not norms. A translator of the 30th round reported it. The other
eight did exist, stated in prose and without numbers: they are numbered
here, with nothing changed in what they say. ⚠ **N9 and N10 keep their
numbers**, which the kit cites elsewhere.

| | norm | scope |
|---|---|---|
| **N1** | **V2 in a main clause** — the finite verb occupies second place, the prefield carrying a single constituent | ⚠ **A TENDENCY**, not a rule (2026-08-16) |
| **N2** | **verb FINAL in a subordinate clause** — after *that, of, the, thō, thār, sō, unt that* — ⚠ **and COORDINATION INSIDE THE CLAUSE, ATTESTED 2026-09-07**: `that` is NOT repeated; `endi` alone carries the second verb, the subject unrepeated, the verb final and in the same mood — Hel. 5860 *that sia ina quelidin endi an crûci slôgin*, 5601 *that thu mîn gihuggies endi an helpun sîs*, 882 (both readers 85 %); the 84th round's blockage is closed: `that sia air ūhton ni fōrin endi … ni quāmin` | ⚠ **A STRONG TENDENCY**, not a rule (2026-08-16) |
| **N3** | ***hwanda* "for" keeps MAIN-CLAUSE order**, verb in second position — ⚠⚠⚠ **and `hwanda` is at POSITION ZERO, like `endi`: it does not count in the reckoning.** The first constituent is what follows it, and the verb comes after: *hwanda **mīn brōther beut** mik*, not †*hwanda **beut** mik mīn brōther*. ⚠ Made precise 2026-08-28: the rule said "second" without saying **second with respect to what**, and two translators diverged — for the second time on this rule. *If what follows `hwanda` is a complete main clause, `hwanda` stands outside it.* | an exception to N2 |
| **N4** | **the legal protasis "Of hwē"** — ⚠⚠⚠ **RECTIFIED 2026-08-28: the verb stays FINAL.** This line said "early verb" and **had no example of its own**, while the kit's two worked protases are verb-final — *of man swīn stilid*, *of ain man sīn rōthar lātid* — and the ruling on the licences lays down that **the bound of the final verb holds for LAW**. *An exception with no example, contradicted by the examples beside it, is not an exception: it is a line that has outlived its own refutation.* ⚠ The second rule of the scale contradicted by its own examples this week, after N3. | — |
| **N5** | in a main clause, *ni* + verb may **open the sentence**, prefield empty — this is the negative V1 licence, detailed below | licence |
| **N6** | **a COMPLETE clause governed by the verb is placed AFTER it**, in every register (R28) | absolute |
| **N7** | **a relative governed by a NOUN stays BEFORE the verb** of its main clause (R28) | ⚠ **A STRONG TENDENCY** (2026-08-17) |
| **N8** | **a heavy phrase carrying a relative goes to the PREFIELD**, and the main clause stays V2 | ⚠ **A TENDENCY** (2026-08-17) |
| **N9** | **LICENCE, in verse**: a heavy constituent — two words or more, or a prepositional phrase — may follow the final verb (poetic extraposition, the *Heliand* type) | licence |
| **N10** | **LICENCE, in REASONING prose** (treatise, demonstration): the same for a heavy complement of cause, means or comparison, or a dative government | licence |

⚠⚠ **AND IN NARRATIVE AND IN LAW, NO LICENCE: THE FINAL VERB IS STRICT**
(ruling of 2026-08-17, **amended the same evening**).

> ⚠⚠ **THE TRANSLATOR OF THE 34th ROUND REPORTED THAT THIS RULING
> CONTRADICTS THE DOUBLE ENQUIRY FIFTEEN LINES ABOVE IT**, which *"asks
> for the extraposition licences to be WIDENED, not restricted"*. **He is
> right, and it is golden rule no. 12 committed by me**: I laid down a
> general rule to fill a local silence, without seeing that the enquiry
> said the opposite.
>
> **ARBITRATION: the bound holds for LAW, it falls for NARRATIVE.**
> The *Pactus* is paratactic and its verb is final; the *Heliand* and
> narrative prose extrapose heavy constituents, and the enquiry attests
> it. A narrative that refuses extraposition produces relatives the kit
> itself calls "unreadable".
>
> *A licence bounded to two registers remains a prohibition in the
> others — but one does not decide a register by symmetry: one decides it
> on what that register attests.* N9 holds **in verse**, N10 **in
reasoning prose** — and the kit said nowhere what happens elsewhere, that
is, in the two registers one translates in most. The question had been
open since the 30th round, phrased *"widen?"*.
> **A licence bounded to two registers is a PROHIBITION in the others,
> and it must be written in both directions.** *A translator who reads
> "licence in verse" does not conclude on his own "therefore never in
> narrative": he concludes "therefore perhaps elsewhere too".*

⚠⚠ **THIS SENTENCE REPEATED, FOR TWO DAYS, A RULE THAT THE ARBITRATION
TWENTY LINES ABOVE HAD ALREADY AMENDED** — corrected 2026-08-19, at the
**third** report (translator A of the 38th round, then both of the 39th,
separately).
**THE STATE IN FORCE, AND THERE IS ONLY ONE**: the bound of the final
verb **holds for LAW**; it **falls for NARRATIVE**, where N9 and N10 are
open — *a narrative that refuses extraposition produces relatives the kit
itself calls "unreadable"*.
  ⚠ A translator of the 39th round puts a figure on what that sentence
  cost: *"my ten sentences are narrative, nearly all of them carry a heavy
  relative, and **the word order of six sentences out of ten depends on
  which of the two I follow**."*

    *A rule amended whose original wording survives twenty lines below
    is not amended: it is doubled. And it is the categorical version the
    reader keeps, because it is the sharper one.*

⚠ **N3 HAS ALREADY MADE TWO TRANSLATORS DIVERGE**: an example in the kit
cited *hwanda* with a final verb, against its own rule, on 2026-08-09.
"That single contradiction made two translators diverge the next day —
one obeying the rule at the cost of a turn that sounds worse, the other
obeying the example."

**Declension of the interrogative** (2026-08-09, it was missing): **hwē**
(who) / acc. **hwena** / gen. **hwes** / dat. **hwemu**; neuter **hwat**,
gen. *hwes*, instr. *hwiu*. On the pattern of the article. Whence *furi
**hwena** sprak hē?* "before whom did he speak?". The kit declined the
article and the demonstrative, never the interrogative, and both
translators of the 9th test had to improvise it.
Negation: preverbal ni, concord **LICIT AND FREQUENT, NOT OBLIGATORY**
(the "obligatory" is abrogated 2026-08-16 — see below: on this point the
auditor was wrong) (nihain man NI sprak; spoken contraction nist = ni
ist).
**Negative V1 licence**: in a main clause, "ni" + verb may open the
sentence with an empty prefield (Ni mahta it andres wesan "it could not
be otherwise"; Ni sprak hē mair) — a constant turn of the corpus,
declared 2026-08-04 to lift a false infraction against V2.
Narrative hinge: thō… thō (subordinate with final verb, resumption V2).
**Mood of the indirect question** (2026-08-09): after *of* "whether"
introducing a QUESTION (not a condition), the **subjunctive** — *man
frāgōd, of the aith thena man **bindē*** "one asks whether the oath binds
the man": the question is open, the indicative would close it. Not to be
confused with *of* as a protasis, which keeps the indicative (*of ain man
sīn rōthar lātid*). Both translators of the 9th test diverged for want of
a rule.
**Jussive of the verbs in -ōn**: their present subjunctive is in **-o**,
homophonous with the imperative (†skauwoe is impossible) — *skauwo the
raginburgio thriu thing* "let the judge look at three things".
**FREE VARIATION — what the kit declares EQUIVALENT** (2026-08-09).
Two independent translators converged at 89 %; part of the remainder was
not error but **licit choice**, and it is better said than left to look
like a fault:
· **the filling of the prefield** — V2 admits in front the subject, an
  adverbial or a complement, at the writer's choice: *Sia fōrun an thia
  habana mit themu flōdē* = *Mit themu flōdē fōrun sia an thia habana*.
  Both are V2, both are right;
· **correlative thō…thō or main clause first**: *Thō sia that stath
  farlētun, thō was the saiw stilli* = *The saiw was stilli, thō sia that
  stath farlētun*;
· **an (+acc) and innan (+acc)** before a place one enters: *an thia
  habana* = *innan thia habana*;
· **a heavy phrase carrying a relative** — R28 wants it before the verb
  of its clause, N9/N10 refuse to extrapose it in narrative: it therefore
  goes to the **prefield**, and the main clause stays V2. *Furi that word,
  that hē thia naht gageban habda, **hēt** Waldramn twē pund wahsas
  beran.* Recasting it as a causal — *Waldramn hēt… beran, **hwanda** hē
  habda thia naht sīn word gageban* — says the same thing and is licit;
  the prefield keeps the nuance "in discharge of" better. ⚠ **hwanda
  keeps MAIN-CLAUSE order**, verb in second position, as the list of
  exceptions above states: this example was first cited here with a final
  verb, on 2026-08-09, and that single contradiction made two translators
  diverge the next day — one obeying the rule "at the cost of a turn that
  sounds worse", the other the example;
· **the place of nihain in a partitive**: *Nihain thero thrītig mannō ni
  brast* = *Thero thrītig mannō ni brast nihain* "of thirty men not one
  was missing". The article before a partitive genitive governed by a
  numeral is licit, not obligatory: *thero thrītig mannō* = *thrītig
  mannō*;

· **fractions are counted FORWARD, not backward**: "one and a half" is
  ***andarhalf*** — literally "the second by half", that is one full unit
  plus half of the second (*andarhalf man* "a man and a half"); likewise
  *thriddehalf* "two and a half". This is not a Romance calque, it is the
  Germanic reckoning, and the kit had none;
· **twice as much as**: ***twifald sō filu sō*** — the exact counterpart
  of the recorded *half sō filu sō* (*that ist twifald sō filu sō ain deur
  berid* "that is twice what a beast carries");
· **the year before**: ***that jār air*** (accusative of determined time +
