⚠ And G2 gives the counter-example that reassures: for mockery, **Gothic
has the ACCUSATIVE** where Old High German has the genitive. *My suspicion
sometimes ran the opposite way from the one I feared.*

### ⚠⚠ THE FIVE VALENCY CLASSES — and why these are not forty decisions

**Ruling of 2026-08-18, in answer to a defect measured the same day.**

The ruling on the syntactic hierarchy had noted that **dozens of recorded
governments cited no witness at all**. The first count gave 66, the second
47 — *my measuring pattern was forgetting Old High German and Dutch* — and
inspection removes a further **seven that are not verbs**: `akkar`,
`Bagak`, `haim`, `wirs`, `aftaro`, `Frīun` — nouns, adverbs, a place name,
whose mention of DECLENSION had been taken for a government.

> ⚠ *Three counts, three figures, and the defect was each time in the
> instrument. A number one has not been able to reproduce is not a
> measurement: it is an alarm.*

**Forty verbs remain. And they do not call for forty witnesses: they fall
under FIVE INHERITED VALENCY CLASSES**, documented for the whole of old
Germanic. It is the argument the two enquirers of the government batch used
themselves for `wārōn` — *the government follows from the derivational
class, not from a citation* — and it is the only one the project's
discipline allows when the witness is missing.

⚠⚠ **AND IT IS ALSO A WARNING, GIVEN BY ENQUIRER R2:**

> *"These are not N independent decisions: it is **ONE SINGLE decision**,
> applied N times. If you rate each one separately, you will give yourself
> the illusion of N convergent confirmations where there is only one
> hypothesis."*

**We therefore write the classes, not the verbs.** A false class carries
off its ten verbs at once — that is the risk, and it is declared rather
than diluted.

| class | government | verbs of the kit |
|---|---|---|
| **V1 — memory, affect, privation, mockery** | **GENITIVE** of the thing | `fargetan`, `gahuggian`, `thenkian`, `skamōn`, `klagōn`, `hlahhian`, `skernōn`, `sunnian`, `biniman`, `warnian` — and the adjective `ful` "full **of**" |
| **V2 — service, following, pleasing, befalling** | **DATIVE** of the person | `thionōn`, `hlosōn`, `galīkōn`, `andwordian`, `galingan`, `gateman`, `skehan`, `langōn` (impersonal) |
| **V3 — ordinary transitive** | **ACCUSATIVE** | `nerian`, `forhtian`, `wardōn`, `lathōn`, `mahalōn`, `hrīnan` — ⚠ **already covered by the ruling on the default**: their mention is redundant, not faulty |
| **V4 — thanking, filling, lacking** | **DATIVE** pers. + **GENITIVE** thing | `thankōn`, `fullian`, `brestan` |
| **V5 — asking, accusing, choosing** | **ACCUSATIVE** pers. + **GENITIVE** thing | `biddan`, `keusan`, `klōn` — and the two settled that day, `manōn` and `wrōgian` |

⚠⚠ **HOW THE OBJECT OF EACH CLASS IS RELATIVISED** — a migration of the
ruling on the two-member relative (2026-08-19). **This is where the
deadlock lay**: until that day a rule promulgated two thousand lines away
declared `the` invariable, and **the objects of the 54 verbs above were all
unrelativisable**. The relative now takes the case the verb assigns *inside*
the relative clause.

| class | relativisation of the object |
|---|---|
| **V1** — gen. of the thing | ***thes** hē fargat* "what he forgot", headless; with a head *that word, **thes** hē swīgōda* |
| **V2** — dat. of the person | *the hairro, **themu** hē thionōda* "the lord he served" |
| **V3** — acc. | **BOTH members are licit, and the bare particle is the UNMARKED choice** — *that word, **the** hē sprak*; the inflected demonstrative (*thena the…*) lifts an ambiguity or marks insistence. ⚠⚠ **Member ② is REQUIRED as soon as the relative carries an oblique case governed inside — and "oblique" means the GENITIVE and the DATIVE, not the accusative**, which is the default object case. *(The word was defined nowhere; clarified 2026-08-19 at the third report.)* |
| **V4** — dat. pers. + gen. thing | according to which of the two is relativised: *the man, **themu** hē thankōda*, or ***thes** hē imu thankōda* |
| **V5** — acc. pers. + gen. thing | likewise: *the man, **thena** hē bad*, or ***thes** hē ina bad* |


⚠ **The particle may REINFORCE the inflected member** — *thes the*, *themu
the* — it does not replace it. And for V1 the **headless** turn is the most
idiomatic: *thes hē tharf* "what he needs". **Do not force an antecedent the
language does not ask for.**

**The authority for these classes is ONE, and it is named**: Behaghel,
*Deutsche Syntax*, vol. I, Heidelberg 1923, the section on the object in
the genitive — ⚠ **pagination NOT VERIFIED**, and both enquirers give it
with the same reservation. It is the work that will decide whether the
reconstruction of the object genitive around 550 is tenable.

### ⚠ WHAT THE CLASSES DO NOT COVER — the isolates, named

· **`fleuhan`** — the simplex is intransitive; it is the **prefixed**
  `antfleuhan` that governs the dative. Two entries, one government.
· **`hladan` / `andhladan`** — **accusative of the CONTAINER** + `mit` +
  dative of the content. A construction of its own, outside the classes.
· **`gabeudan`, `rādan`, `sakian`, `wrekan`, `folgōn`, `galaubian`** —
  each carries **two declared governments**, hence two uses. They fall
  under no class, they fall under a division of sense, and each will have
  to be examined for itself.

### ⚠⚠ WHAT THIS RULING DOES NOT SETTLE, AND IT MUST BE WRITTEN

**It does not supply the witnesses.** It says that forty governments rest
on five hypotheses instead of forty, which is at once more honest and more
fragile: **the day V1 falls, ten lemmas fall with it.**

And it says nothing about the **vitality of the object genitive around
550**, which is the underlying question: in all attested West Germanic, the
object in the genitive is **already in retreat** before the accusative and
prepositional phrases. V1, V4 and V5 depend on it entirely — **eighteen
verbs out of forty.**

> *The real deliverable is not "verb X → genitive", it is "verb X → a set
> of frames, with weights". The kit still writes a cell where the witnesses
> show an alternation.*

**An open case, named and quantified**: eighteen verbs hanging on a single
question of dating.

He was right, and **the kit answered badly**. Out of 173 recorded verbs, 36
carried a government; the other 137 were not *mute by choice*, they were
mute **for want of a statement**. One cannot mark 137 lemmas "+
accusative": the measure would rise, the language would learn nothing, and
the kit would gain two thousand tokens of noise.

> **PROMULGATED: a transitive verb WITHOUT a mention governs the
> ACCUSATIVE, and an intransitive verb without a mention has no object.
> The absence is no longer a silence: it is a DECLARATION.**
>
> **Corollary, and it is the corollary that does the work: every verb that
> governs ANYTHING OTHER than the accusative carries its mention
> OBLIGATORILY.** The list of deviants is therefore closed by construction
> — if a verb is not on it, it is regular.

⚠ **THIS IS NOT A CONVENIENCE, IT IS A REVERSAL OF THE BURDEN.** Before:
the translator had to guess what the kit had not said. After: it is the kit
that must have said everything that deviates, and its silence binds it.

*A dictionary cannot state everything. But it can state what its silence
means — and then the silence stops being a hole and becomes a piece of
information.*

⚠ **The bound, and it is real**: this ruling is worth exactly what the
completeness of the list of deviants is worth. Every deviant found later
strengthens it; **one deviant NOT recorded turns it into a trap**, since
the translator will be entitled to believe in the regular case.

⚠⚠ **AND THE FIRST BREACH WAS FOUND THE SAME DAY, BY THE 35th ROUND.**
Translator A pointed out that **`thurfan` "to need" carried no mention** —
hence the accusative by this ruling — whereas it governs the **genitive in
all four witnesses**, and the kit writes `mī ist naud` **(+gen.)** three
hundred lines further on. *The first hole, and on a modal verb of high
frequency.*


The audit that followed found **two more**: **`haurian`**, which governs
the accusative in the sense "to hear" and the **dative** in the sense "to
obey", and **`frāgōn`**, which takes the accusative of the person and the
**genitive** of the thing. All three are now recorded.

> *A ruling that declares its own condition of validity invites one to put
> it to the test. This one was tested in eight hours, by someone who had
> only the kit — and the breach closed the same evening. That is the normal
> regime, not an incident.* That is why non-accusative governments are now
recorded with their STANDARD, and not deduced.

### The four governments

**`sprekan` + addressee: `te` + dative** — *hē **sprak te im***.
⚠⚠ **AND THE BARE DATIVE IS NOT MERELY WORSE, IT IS AMBIGUOUS.** Old Saxon
makes massive use, with verbs of speaking, of an **ethical dative
coreferent with the SUBJECT**: *thō sprak im eft…* "then X spoke again (to
himself)". †*sprak im* will therefore be read "**he** spoke, he did", not
"he spoke **to them**". The Tatian, moreover, constantly renders the Latin
dative of *loqui* by *zi* + dative **even while calquing** — had it had the
bare dative, it would have kept it.
· **Distribution by verb, to be remembered**: *seggian, quethan,
andwordian, gabeudan* → **bare dative**, without ambiguity, since the
content is the object · *sprekan* → **te + dative**, because it is a verb of
speech ACTIVITY, not of transmission.
· *sprekan mit* + dative = "to converse with". ⚠ The distinction "to speak
to" / "to speak with" is **available but not systematic**: do not make a
binding rule of it.

**Verb of warning + complement clause: `that … ni` + subjunctive, final
verb** — *wardōd iu, **that** gī that erbi **ni** sellien*.
Germanic has **no dedicated conjunction** of the Latin *ne* type: only
Gothic has *ibai* + optative, with no continental counterpart. The pattern
is the Tatian's, *uuartet thaz ir … ni …*.
⚠⚠ **`warnōn` IS TO BE SET ASIDE, AND BOTH ENQUIRERS SAY SO**: in old
Germanic the verb means "to equip, to provide, to be on one's guard"; the
sense "to warn" is a **late English and German** development. To use it
would be to import from the modern languages.
⚠ *hōdian* is **disputed between the two** — one proposes it with genitive
government, the other refuses it as "a verb of shepherds and sentries",
physical guarding and not moral warning. **Cell declared: we write
`wardōn`, on which the two agree.** One of them points to an even safer
option, *sehat that gī … ni …* "see that you do not…", resting on Goth.
*saiƕiþ ei* and OHG *sehet thaz*.

**`wīsian` + dative of person + complement clause** — *hē **wīsda im
that**…*, bare dative, final verb. A regular double government, no
difficulty.
⚠ **THE MOOD IS MEANINGFUL, NOT MECHANICAL**: **indicative** if the content
is taken as a fact by the narrator, **subjunctive** if it is reported
without being vouched for. It is a useful opposition, not an agreement.
⚠ **A TRAP OF HOMOGRAPHY REPORTED BY AN ENQUIRER, AND IT IS ALREADY IN THE
KIT**: *taugian* "to make see" would be better than *wīsian* for ostension
— but the kit carries *that niowiht, thār hē fon **gatogan** ist*, where
*gatogan* is the participle of **teuhan** "to draw", not of *taugian* "to
show". **"drawn" and "shown" would look dangerously alike in writing.** The
cell is left to *wīsian*, the trap being declared.

**`galingan` impersonal: GENITIVE of the thing** — ***thes** galingid iu*,
"you will bring **it** off".
⚠⚠ **`thes` IS EXACTLY THE PARTITIVE PRONOUN "of it"**, and that is the
cell that was missing. The pattern of the Germanic impersonals is **dative
of the person + genitive of the matter** — *mih lustid **thes***, *mir
wirdit **thes*** — and it is a property of the whole class, not of any one
verb.
· correlative with a subordinate clause: ***thes** … **that*** — *thes
galingid iu, that gī…*, the genitive announcing the complement clause.
· ⚠ **`an` + dative is to be set aside**: a modern turn, calqued on German.
· ⚠ **A declared reservation**: the verb *galingan* itself is of **rank
(c)**, the two enquirers being unable to warrant it either in Saxon or at
rank (a) — 45 % each. We keep it for the coherence of the lexicon, knowing
what we do. Better attested personal substitutes, if one wants safety:
*gathīhan* "to prosper, to bring to a good end".


### The temporal conjunctions

⚠⚠ **GERMANIC DOES NOT POSSESS A CLOSED LIST OF CONJUNCTIONS: IT MAKES
THEM**, on the pattern `[adverb or demonstrative phrase] + relative
particle (sō / the / that)`. The three forms the kit already carried — *sō
lango sō*, *air than*, *unt that* — are three instances of the same
mechanism. **To produce others is therefore not to invent: it is to apply
an attested generative rule.**

· **"as soon as" → `sān sō`**, indicative, final verb. ⚠ One of the two
points to a better attested form which, however, **does not carry
immediacy**: *sō hwan sō* "at the moment when". We keep *sān sō* for the
sense, declaring that it is reconstructed by proportion (Goth. *sunsei*, OE
*sōna swā*, OHG *sār sō*).
· **"after" → `sīthor`**, indicative. ⚠⚠ **BUT THE HELIAND MOST OFTEN MAKES
DO WITH `thō` + PRETERITE**: *old Germanic massively UNDERSPECIFIES
posteriority*, the order of the clauses being enough. **Do not over-equip
the kit with a tool the language uses only sparingly.**
⚠ And *sīthor* is **ambiguous between temporal and causal** — "since (the
time when)" → "since (because)", like Eng. *since*, Ger. *seitdem*. **That
is a fact of the language, not a defect: do not try to eliminate it.** For
pure posteriority, *aftar thiu that*.
· **"while" → `thē hwīla the`**, literally "the time that": accusative of
duration + relative particle. **Germanic has no simple conjunction of
simultaneity.** It is the direct root of Du *terwijl* and Ger. *dieweil*.
⚠ *mit thiu that* exists too, but it is **punctual-circumstantial**, not
durative: "as, while".
· **"as long as" → `sō lango sō`. THE DISTINCTION DOES NOT EXIST.** Both
enquirers are categorical: Germanic does not grammaticalise the difference
between an observed duration and a conditional one. It goes through the
**mood**, not through a conjunction. **The cell is empty in the LANGUAGE,
not only in the kit — nothing is being invented.**

⚠⚠ **THE MOOD AFTER A PROSPECTIVE TEMPORAL CLAUSE: PRESENT INDICATIVE**,
and it is a single rule that replaces several special cases.
> **THE GERMANIC SUBJUNCTIVE MARKS NON-REALISATION, NEVER TEMPORAL
> SUBORDINATION IN ITSELF.**
Whence: *air than* calls for it, because the event is **necessarily not yet
come about** at the reference point; prospective *unt that* calls for it,
because it sets a **targeted bound** with a final value. "As soon as" does
not call for it: the event there is **posited as bound to happen**, and
Germanic having no future, the present indicative carries what is to come.
⚠ **Anteriority in the future is rendered by ASPECT, not by mood** — to
solve a problem of aspect by a means of modality would be a category
mistake. Recommended form, the best attested: the **resultative** *wesan* +
participle — *sān sō the akkar **gasnidan is***. The active perfect
*hebbian* + participle is possible but still semi-grammaticalised at 550.
*Of the two translators, the one who used the indicative was right; the
other was not wrong, but he wrote a marked form.*

### The adverbial relative — it exists, and it opens a whole series

⚠⚠ **YES, OLD GERMANIC HAS AN ADVERBIAL RELATIVE**, and the translator who
improvised *thār thiu hand ni līthid* was **right**: he had recovered the
attested turn. **The proof is morphological and it is Gothic** — Gothic
welds the relative particle *-ei* to the demonstrative adverb:
Goth. *þar* "there" → ***þarei*** "where", *þaþrō* "thence" →
Goth. ***þaþrōei*** "whence".
Saxon and High German have lost the weld and use **the bare demonstrative
adverb** as a relative.

· **relative of PLACE → `thār`**: *the stedi, **thār** thiu hand ni līthid*.
Final verb. Optional reinforcement by the particle: *thār the*.
· **relative of TIME → NOT `thār`.** ⚠ **The two enquirers diverge here, and
the divergence is declared**: one holds that Germanic has **no** adverbial
relative of time at all and that one must go through the nominal antecedent
plus the particle — *an themu dagē, **the** hē quam*; the other proposes
*thō* in relative use — *an themu dagē, **thō** hē quam*. **They agree on
one point: `thār` is SPATIAL and does not extend to time.** The kit writes
*the*, the form the two agree on.
⚠ And both add that **Germanic clearly prefers the CORRELATIVE to the
embedded relative**: *thō hē quam, thō…* "when he came, then…" is
stylistically the better turn. **A translation instruction as much as a
rule of grammar.**
· **OBLIQUE relative → `thār` + POSTPOSED PREPOSITION**: *thiu erða,
**thār** hē **fon** gatogan ist*. Synthetic variant: *thanan* "whence".


⚠⚠ **AND IT IS THE BEST YIELD IN THE WHOLE FILE** — 90 %, "the best
established point" according to one of the two. The kit **already** carried
this turn, in one example, **with no rule to generalise it**. And it is no
hapax: it is the **central construction**, the one Dutch *waarvan* and
German *wovon* will come out of.
> **We are not filling a gap: we are restoring a system.**
At a stroke the whole series: ***thār … fon*** "whence" · ***thār … mit***
"wherewith" · ***thār … an*** "wherein" · ***thār … te*** "whereto".
⚠ **A retroactive gain, free of charge**: the interrogative *hwār* accepts
the same postposition — ***hwār fon*** "whence?", ***hwār mit*** "with
what?", ***hwār te*** "to what?". The kit did not have it.

### The partitive quantifier

**"a little of" → `luttil` + GENITIVE** — ***luttil watras***. ⚠⚠ **Written †*watares* until the 35th round, and that form broke TWO rules at once**: the stem is anaptyctic, so the vowel drops before the ending, and the rule on final syllables abrogates `-es` in favour of `-as`. †*watares* kept the syncopated vowel AND the 9th-century ending — *the exact fault the kit condemns under the name of `dages`, committed in a file promulgated two days AFTER the migration.* Reported by translator A of the 35th round. The kit already had the
rule before its eyes: it is that of *filu* + genitive, applied to its
antonym. **Nothing to learn, everything to allow** — Germanic freely
substantivises the neuter quantifier and gives it a partitive genitive
(Goth. *filu, leitil, ganōh*; OS *luttil, mēr, wiht*).
· **adverbial "a little"**: *luttilu* (adverbial dative-instrumental) — *hē
baid **luttilu*** "he waited a little". A function distinct from the
partitive.
  ⚠⚠ **AND IT IS NOT "LITTLE".** *luttilu* states a **positive** quantity;
  for the **deficit** ("he sleeps little", "he speaks little") it is
  adverbial `luttil`, the positive of the series `luttil / min / minnist` —
  see § *THE DEGREE OF THE ADVERB*. **Writing †*luttilu slāpid* says "she
  sleeps A little", nearly the opposite of what is meant.** *Cross-reference
  laid down 2026-08-21: the two sections existed and did not cite each
  other.*
· ⚠ **†*ain luttil* is to be set aside**: the indefinite article is not
grammaticalised at 550; the turn is late OHG and Middle High German.
· **"a few" → *sum* + genitive**, but it is a COUNTABLE: not to be confused
with *luttil*, which is a mass quantifier.
· ⚠ And the most Germanic way of hedging remains **litotes**: *ni filu*
"not much", often better than the direct quantifier.
⚠ The two translators' evasion — *mit luttlemu mōdē* — was not faulty, but
it said **something else**: "with a modest spirit", and not "with a little
courage". The partitive was available and regular.

### ⚠⚠ Three cells where the language does not make the distinction the source does

It is the most useful information in the file, and it is owned rather than
papered over:
· **"as long as" ≠ "for as long as"** — the distinction does not exist;
· **posteriority is UNDERSPECIFIED** — *thō* + preterite does the work most
of the time;
· **"to speak to" ≠ "to speak with"** — available, not systematic: build no
binding rule on it.


## Idiom (never a calque)

⚠⚠ **RE-LAID OUT 2026-08-16: one item to a line, six headings.** The
section was a single river of a paragraph in which thirty idioms followed
one another separated by middle dots — one could find nothing in it without
reading all of it. **Not one character was rewritten; only the layout
changed**, and the check covered the whole set of items.

⚠ **The rule remains the one in the title: never a calque.** Where the
source language has a verb and Frenkisk has a noun plus a verb, it is
Frenkisk that is right — *hungar hebbian*, never "to be famished"; *kalf
beran*, never a verb forged for "to calve".

### The impersonals — the subject is the THING, the person is in the dative

· it seems to me = mī thunkid
· I feel like = mik lustid (+gen)
· I miss you = mī langōd aftar thī
· I need = mī ist naud (+gen)
· to succeed / to fail = mī galingid/mislingid
· it is all one to me = that ist mī galīk
· there is = thār ist/sint (never "it gibid")

### The noun + verb turns — never a forged verb

· to be right = wār quethan
· to be hungry = hungar hebbian (never "to be famished")
· my name is = ik haitu X
· to pretend = dōn sō + pret. subj.
· to remember = gahuggian (+gen) / in hertan haldan
· to forget = fargetan
· **to calve** = **kalf beran** (and not a forged verb)
· to promise = **`gahaitan`** (+dat. pers., +acc. thing) — ⚠ *the verb is in the dictionary; this line prescribed a periphrasis for a hole that does not exist.* `sīn word geban` = "to give one's word"

### The set formulas

· **to invoke, at the head of a deed** = ***An Godes namon*** "in God's
name" — the kit had no formula of invocation at all, and the two
translators of the 22nd test built it separately on the same recorded
material, identically. It is the norm
· **to greet** = **Wis hail!** (to one) / **Wesad hail!** (to several, or in
the polite form) — it is ALSO the greeting, not only the toast: the kit had
no formula of greeting at all, and a language without a "good day" cannot
carry a dialogue — ⚠ and since 2026-09-07 the VERB exists too: `grōtian` +
accusative, "to greet, to address", on some thirty Heliand lines and
nineteen Gothic ones; the 84th round had to do without it
· **to be on the market** = **te kaupē wesan** (*ist thius kū te kaupē?*),
dative of the recorded *kaup*, rank (a) Du *te koop*
· **to ask a price, addressing someone** = **Hwat nimis thū furi sia?**
"what do you take for it?" — the recorded formula *Hwat gildid siu?* is
IMPERSONAL and loses the address, which the two translators of the 8th test
each reported independently as the defect of their solution
· good health! = Wis hail! — Drink hail!

### Dying — two registers, and the division is attested

· to die = **te Gode faran** in the Christian register and when the speaker
is speaking of HIS OWN death, **sterban** for the bare fact reported of a
third party (*ira man was gastorban* "her husband was dead") — the two
translators of 2026-08-09 made this division SEPARATELY and identically,
both giving it as uncertain; it is the norm

### The connectives

· therefore = bī thiu
· although = doh that (+subj.)
· not only… but also = nalles aino… ak auk
· both… and, in contradictory simultaneity = ~~endi… endi~~ ⚠⚠⚠ **ABROGATED 2026-08-25** — `endi … endi` is NOT a correlative (double enquiry, rated 80 % and 90 %). The Germanic correlative is another word (see the ruling on coordination) (the correlative was missing:
it was what flattened "both to freeze and to burn")
· indeed = gawisso

### Comparison

· **with respect to, as against = wither + accusative** (ain niowiht wither
that endilausa "a nothing as against the infinite"): to compare is to set
two things one against the other, like two weights on the wāga —
promulgated 2026-08-04, on the finding that for comparison the language had
only *than* "than", insufficient outside a comparative.
**Government of *than*** (2026-08-09): the comparison is ELLIPTICAL, the
compared term takes the case it would have in the understood clause — hence
the **nominative** when it would be the subject there: *bet than **ain
bodo*** "better than a messenger", *grimmiro than **alla thia wintrā*** ⚠ *(macron restored 2026-08-21 — accusative plural of an `-a` stem)*.
The two translators of the 4th test did it so, reporting that the kit did
not give the government.

## Calibration (bilingual reference texts)

"Ik Theudoberht, kuning thero Frankōnō, gibu thī thit alōd mit allero
mīnero mundburdi, that nihain grāfio noh nihain liud it thī ni binime."
= "I, Theudebert, king of the Franks, give you this allod with all my
protection, that no count and no man may take it from you."

"Hwār ist hēr ain heriberga? — Hwat gildid that beddi? — Te diuri!"
= "Where is there an inn here? — What does the bed cost? — Too dear!"

"Nio ni ist the wintar sō lang, that the lentin ni kumē."
= "Never is the winter so long that the spring does not come."


---

## ⚠⚠⚠ WHERE THE DICTIONARY AND THE INDEX ARE (2026-08-25)

**They are no longer in this file, and that is deliberate.**

· **`frenkisk-dictionary.md`** — THE COMPLETE DICTIONARY, the source of truth.
· **`frenkisk-index.md`** — the ENGLISH → FRENKISK index.

⚠ **These two are not read: they are queried.** The two translators of the 57th round opened 70 and 100 entries out of 1,803 — they made up **40 % of the file and 0 % of the reading**, and their weight prevented the grammar from being opened.

⚠⚠ **Look a word up in the index, then read it again in its entry in the lexicon** — the entry carries the class, the gender, the government and often a whole rule the index cannot render.
